# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file. Follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

Entries for the next release are in [`changelog.d/`](./changelog.d/README.md), one file per change; do not add lines here. `pnpm changelog:preview` prints them as this section.

## [0.0.53] - 2026-10-28

This release runs the desktop backend on the app's own runtime, opens a public web trial and refreshes the website. The desktop backend now runs on the packaged Electron 45 executable with RunAsNode instead of a separate Node download; every start, restart and recovery verifies the runtime, the main bundle and the preload bundle, and Windows gains an extract-once ZIP download. macOS builds require macOS 13.5 or later. Source and Docker deployments move to Node 26.12.1 and patched HTTP clients for the October 2026 security fixes, and the Docker image starts one Node process. The Render blueprint now deploys a playable trial in the browser-private form, the official one at [demo.covel.cc](https://demo.covel.cc/): saves stay in each player's browser, API keys travel only with each request and are never written to disk, a database or logs, and that form now leaves open only what visitors do with their own keys and saves. Players there see a notice that links to [Play on a public trial](../start/hosted-trial.md) and to the exact source commit, and can play with a model on their own computer or local network, which their browser relays to the server. A new browser save keeps the default plugins and the world's starting quests and items from its first sync, and forms and plugin panels shown during a turn unlock when the turn ends. The website gains live plugin examples, a download catalog for every platform, a language menu and a link to the trial. The getting started guide now opens with a language choice, and Dice Check no longer rolls against health or trust in a world without abilities. Read the [breaking changes](#breaking-changes-in-v0053) and the [upgrade notes](#upgrade-notes-for-v0053) before installing. macOS Apple Silicon and Windows x64 binaries are unsigned; the macOS build is not notarized.

Verified while preparing this release, on Linux x64: `pnpm check`, every Vitest and Node test suite (`turbo test --continue`: 43 of 44 tasks passed; the desktop suite passed 65 of its 66 tests, and the remaining one could not launch Electron because the machine lacks the system library `libnspr4.so`), `pnpm release:preflight`, `pnpm test:docs:packages`, and the documentation and website builds. The browser-private trial ran on Render's free plan from the merged blueprint, and its `/api/health` reported the browser-private form and the deployed commit. The local model relay had an adversarial security review before release; its findings (server-owned keys reaching a browser, uncapped relay streams, encoded path separators, plugin wires holding the relay, head-handling races and caches shared across players) were fixed with regression tests. Real play through the relay then found a truncated page, gameplay model calls that dropped the relay, a first browser sync that discarded the default plugins and starting content, and in-turn forms and plugin panels that stayed locked; all four were fixed with regression tests before the tag. After those fixes, with a real model through a local Magpie Responses endpoint: a production Docker image with the Render blueprint's settings and a 512 MiB limit was played in a browser through the relay (a new Emberback save with its default plugins, two turns, a container restart that restored the save, and a third turn, while the container itself could not reach the player's loopback port), the packaged macOS arm64 app was played, quit, relaunched and continued, and both completed Harukaze Academy's character creation, opening and a turn. On the release candidate, CI passed the static checks, every unit suite, `pnpm test:pg` against PostgreSQL, `pnpm e2e:smoke` with the community plugin fixtures, the website build and CodeQL. The first `Build Desktop` trial runs found Windows-only failures in desktop test fixtures and in the Windows shipped-artifact check, all fixed before the tag; the trial run of the final fixes built the macOS arm64 and Windows x64 packages, passed the Windows desktop regressions, and booted the shipped Windows ZIP and portable EXE to session preparation. The same Windows steps also passed on a local Windows 11 VM. On native macOS arm64 at `e7217a3c7`, `pnpm --filter @covel/desktop smoke:restart` passed with the real preload, IPC and origin guard, including navigation to a new backend port and version checks before and after restart; it uses two isolated HTTP fixtures, not a real model or a packaged backend restart. The macOS arm64 trial package from that commit's Build Desktop run was downloaded, its artifact digest checked, and its actual ZIP application verified locally: bundled runtime and resources, SQLite and vector support, builtin plugin loading with and without model configuration, world selection and session preparation, page reload, preload version 0.0.53, and clean process-group exit. On Linux x64 with the mise toolchain, `pnpm --filter @covel/desktop build` passed its staging boot check, `pnpm test:pg` passed against PostgreSQL 18 with pgvector, and the full `pnpm e2e` passed 140 tests; one AI world-generation test that relies on a one-second model timeout fails intermittently under load, as it does on v0.0.52. Not verified yet: the relay on the live HTTPS trial, which still runs v0.0.52 (browser permissions, reverse-proxy streaming and cold start); any play on Windows; Gatekeeper or DMG installation; and the published installers with their checksums and provenance.

### Breaking changes in v0.0.53

- **macOS desktop builds now require macOS 13.5 or later.** This declared minimum remains in place when the backend uses Electron RunAsNode.
- **Desktop staging checks no longer accept `--electron-node` or `COVEL_SMOKE_HOST_NODE`.** They always verify and run bundled Electron with RunAsNode and SQLite; missing or mismatched runtime resources fail the build.

### Added

- **Windows has an extract-once ZIP download.** Extract the folder once and run `Covel.exe` from it; the installer remains the recommended download. The single-file self-extracting EXE remains available with a native preparation notice during extraction. See [installation](../start/install.md).
- **Players on a public trial see where their saves and keys go.** When `/api/health` reports a shared server whose saves stay in the browser, the web app shows a dismissible notice: saves and API keys are kept in the browser, the server never writes keys to disk, a database or its logs, and the server may restart at any time. It links to the new "Play on a public trial" page and, when configured, to the exact source commit the instance runs. The notice, the guide's model step and each provider's key form in Settings → LLM → Providers & Models also ask players to create a temporary key for the trial and to delete or rotate it at their provider afterwards; the key form keeps the docs link after the notice is dismissed.
- **`/api/health` reports `deployment`.** `deployment.form` is `personal`, `browser-private` or `hosted`; `deployment.source` carries `COVEL_SOURCE_REPOSITORY` and `COVEL_SOURCE_COMMIT` (falling back to Render's `RENDER_GIT_COMMIT`) when they are valid. Fields: `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **The website and the READMEs point to the official web trial.** The homepage hero has a “Play in your browser” button and the downloads section links [demo.covel.cc](https://demo.covel.cc/); both READMEs describe the trial, what stays in the browser and its free-plan limits.
- **The getting started guide opens with a language choice.** Its first step lists every interface language in its own script and switches the interface as soon as one is chosen; the language drop-down above each step is gone. The guide is new, so players who finished an earlier one see it once more; their model settings are kept.
- **A public trial can use a model on the player's own computer or local network.** On a shared server, a provider endpoint such as `http://127.0.0.1:3425/v1`, `http://localhost:11434/v1` or `https://192.168.1.20/v1` is reached through the player's browser (the local model relay): the server hands the request to the browser over `GET /api/model-relay/stream`, and the browser calls the local model and posts the answer back. Public endpoints are still called by the server. The model server must allow the trial's origin through CORS, and the page must stay open; plain-http LAN addresses are not supported because browsers block them from an HTTPS page. Settings explains each case under the endpoint field. Steps: `docs/start/hosted-trial.md`; endpoints: `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **Download any supported platform from the website.** macOS and Windows builds are listed together with individual download and copy-link controls, regardless of the visitor's operating system. Windows reserves installer, ZIP and portable formats, recommends the installer, and marks unreleased files without inventing download links.
- **Explore plugins directly on the homepage.** Roll dice, advance a quest, inspect relationships and search the codex in localized, theme-aware browser examples.
- **The homepage foregrounds plugins, world packs and creation.** In-app world generation and workspace Agent Skills have distinct introductions, with small links to the growing plugin/world directories. Downloads are consolidated in one section, reached by sticky quick navigation.
- **The plugin examples now include an interactive relationship graph.** Character profiles and connections are separate from player affinity; optional image generation and speech synthesis extensions are introduced alongside the examples.
- **Authoring skills have a copyable installation command.** The homepage retains direct SKILL.md links, and download formats use a single compact platform catalog.
- **Homepage scenes follow each world’s actual plugin selection.** Three selectable moments show replies, checks, quests, world panels and their sample outcomes, with synchronized plugin highlighting and links to the world configuration.

### Changed

- **Desktop backend verification reads large runtime files asynchronously before starting the server.** Every start, restart, and recovery still checks the pinned runtime and main bundle afresh, while file reads yield to the desktop event loop. Failed checks still stop startup and request a complete reinstall.
- **Each desktop staging smoke checks the Electron fuse once before probing its versions.** Resource validation and the version probe share one preflight; later launches, restarts and recoveries still perform fresh checks.
- **Desktop backends use the packaged Electron 45.0.0 executable with RunAsNode.** The backend remains a separate child process with private lifecycle IPC. Standalone Node downloads, the extra executable and license, and runtime-selection configuration are removed. Source and Docker deployments keep Node 26.12.1; macOS still requires 13.5 or later.
- **Windows release validation exercises Electron 45 directly.** Desktop regressions run on Windows, and the shipped ZIP backend is checked after extraction into a path with spaces and Chinese characters. ZIP/portable UI checks cover reload and record artifact sizes. Backend smoke checks bound HTTP requests, wait for child termination before cleanup, and retain ZIP backend logs on failure.
- **Desktop packages carry fewer redundant files.** Bundled main-process dependencies are no longer copied again, and server staging removes the duplicate pnpm tree plus reviewed development-only files after compilation. TypeScript community plugins, native database support, authored content, locales and license notices are retained. This reduces payload size and file count; it does not establish a Windows startup-time improvement.
- **The Render blueprint deploys a playable public trial on the free plan.** `render.yaml` now runs the browser-private form (`DEPLOYMENT_TIER=self`, `STORE_BACKEND=memory`) instead of an operator-only `demo` host, listens on Render's port 10000, caps the V8 heap for 512 MB instances and names its source repository. Steps and limits: `docs/start/deploy-render.md`.
- **The Docker image starts one Node process.** The container runs `node --import tsx src/index.ts` instead of `pnpm exec tsx`, which saves about 100 MB at idle and delivers stop signals to the server directly.
- **`operatorTokenRequired` is `true` on every shared form.** A browser-private server now reports it, so the web settings show "Operator Access" and hide the media library there, as on hosted tiers.
- **The website now uses editable, localized HTML previews.** Gameplay and world-creation examples follow the page theme and language, with reusable world art, consistent icons, polished Chinese and English storytelling copy, and a pause control. Scenes crossfade on a twelve-second cycle with restrained entrance motion and stable layout.
- **Website languages are selected from a menu.** The theme-aware custom picker supports keyboard selection. The language registry supports additional editions, and selections persist in the URL and local preference.
- **The website opens in dark mode by default.** Manually selected themes and languages are remembered across refreshes and return visits; the saved theme is applied before the first paint.
- **The homepage separates play experiences from shared features.** Its fourth scene now presents Emberback Relay’s survival sandbox; multilingual support has its own feature panel.
- **Stable dependencies and development tools are refreshed.** Updates follow the repository's release waiting period and include pnpm, Vitest, Playwright, and the tools scaffold. See the dependency policy in [Package boundaries](../internals/packages.md).
- **The author SDK requires Node 26.12.1, like the host.** `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils` now declares `engines.node` `>=26.12.1 <27`, the same minimum as the repository, CI and the Docker image, which includes the October 2026 security fixes. Plugin projects on an earlier Node 26 release should move to 26.12.1.

### Fixed

- **SQLite vector search stays available in the Alpine Docker image.** The image preloads its installed glibc compatibility library so `sqlite-vec` can resolve its required symbols under musl. An offline build check loads the extension as the runtime user and fails the build if it cannot load.
- **Windows desktop backend launches hide the extra Node console window.** Starting or restarting the managed backend keeps its console hidden.
- **Desktop health checks release unused response bodies.** Repeated readiness and heartbeat checks no longer retain connections for bodies they never read.
- **Desktop package management explains failed restart checks and offers a retry.** Restart stays unavailable while desktop information is loading or failed, and becomes available after a successful check in a packaged app.
- **Desktop resource validation also checks the preload bundle.** Builds, packaged ASAR checks, and managed backend launches reject missing or mismatched preload bytes alongside the main bundle. Rebuild the complete desktop package; existing saves do not need recreation. These digests detect mixed or damaged builds and do not provide publisher signing or trusted-root tamper protection.
- **Dice checks without a usable ability no longer show an attribute label in the instruction language.** When no ranged ability is available, the check rules use modifier 0 and omit the optional `attribute` field from the receipt. `COVEL_INSTRUCTION_LOCALE` therefore cannot insert an English "None" into a Chinese session or a Chinese label into another language. See [RPG mechanics](../builtin/rpg.md) and [plugin language rules](../reference/plugins.md).
- **Dice Check no longer rolls against health or trust in a world without abilities.** When the player character has no ranged number in the `abilities` category, the check rules use modifier 0 and report no attribute, instead of asking the narrative to invent a modifier from health or trust. Missing character data uses the same rule.
- **Development desktop shells no longer offer to restart an externally managed backend.** The Desktop and Install & manage panes explain that the developer must restart `pnpm dev`; direct restart requests are rejected without changing the server or port.
- **Quick desktop restarts cancel an in-flight update check.** A stalled GitHub response no longer consumes the backend's shutdown budget and prevents its storage-close phase.
- **Recursive JSON schemas type-check with TypeScript 7.1.** Update Zod to 4.6.7 across the host, author SDK peer dependency, and tools scaffold, and give the tool-invocation factory an explicit portable return type. Runtime validation and plugin contracts stay unchanged; stable TypeScript 7.0.2 remains the project compiler.
- **Browser-private saves retain default dependencies and world starting content.** The first sync downloads the server's complete initialized state instead of overwriting it with an empty local seed. Quests, inventory, affinity, codex, and relationship features keep their required providers and starting data. Established saves keep their own selection and progress when the server restarts.
- **Character forms and plugin panels shown during a turn become usable when the turn finishes.** Their actions now run the current handlers instead of the ones captured when they appeared, so a stage panel's message button no longer stays inert after the turn; entered form values remain intact.

### Security

- **Desktop runtime verification fails closed across builds, packaging, startup, and restart.** Verify actual embedded versions and RunAsNode capability, bind target resources to the main bundle, check packaged ASAR bytes before release smoke, and strip inherited runtime-control environment variables without removing legitimate CA, proxy, or provider settings.
- **Release verification uses a patched ASAR reader and bounded brace expansion.** Pin the build-only archive reader to `@electron/asar` 4.3.1 and restrict the `brace-expansion` 5.0.12 override to `minimatch` 10, leaving older dependency lines unchanged.
- **Electron 45 includes the eMudhra certificate blocklist and the verified Chromium Brotli decoder fix.** Its embedded Node 24.22.0 is not equivalent to the full Node 24.22.1 security release. Core npm Undici 8.12.2, URL normalization, and `dns.lookup` reduce exposure, while native plugin networking and specialized direct PostgreSQL configurations retain residual risk. See the runtime boundaries in `docs/internals/security.md`.
- **Desktop packaging disables Node CLI debugging arguments and restricts the app entry point to ASAR.** RunAsNode and custom-CA environment support remain enabled; managed child startup strips `NODE_OPTIONS`. These fuse settings do not provide signing or cryptographic ASAR integrity verification. Loopback sidecar HTTP requests also reject redirects.
- **Source and Docker deployments use Node 26.12.1.** The minimum supported Node 26 version, mise, CI and Docker pins now include the October 2026 security fixes, including the native Brotli decoder fix. The Docker image remains pinned by digest.
- **Malformed compressed HTTP responses no longer terminate Covel through the affected Undici client.** Provider traffic and the desktop shell's sidecar client use npm Undici 8.12.2. Transitive Undici 6.x and 7.x copies receive their corresponding security releases without changing major versions. Exact reviewed security versions bypass the usual seven-day dependency delay.
- **The browser-private form keeps open only what visitors do with their own keys and saves.** Approving or running community plugin server code, diagnostics (including `GET /api/provider-keys`), model database refresh, host configuration and media cleanup now need the operator token, and server settings cannot be written. Creating sessions, generating worlds and listing or testing models stay open. Model endpoints may no longer resolve to loopback or private addresses there, and the Host check is off because session owner tokens are the boundary. Rules: `docs/internals/security.md#host-profile`.
- **The local model relay only carries what the player configured.** A request reaches the browser that named its random channel in `X-Model-Relay`, never another visitor's, and only when its endpoint came from the player's own settings with no server-owned key or header attached: the server's own model addresses and keys are never shown to a browser. The browser makes a request only when the URL is under one of the player's own provider endpoints on their computer or local network, with GET or POST, without cookies, without encoded path separators and without following redirects; anything else is refused without a request. Plugin wires never receive the relay. A server in a shared form adds `http://127.0.0.1:*` and `http://localhost:*` to the page's `connect-src` when it serves the page; the built page, personal servers and the desktop app keep the policy unchanged. Opening a channel is rate-limited like the event stream, a process holds at most 512 channels, and a reconnect closes the older stream. Rules: `docs/internals/security.md#local-model-relay`.
- **Malformed narrator-only comments no longer cause quadratic parsing time.** Repeated marker mentions in world lore are scanned without repeated suffix backtracking, preserving existing spoiler-hiding behavior.
- **Fallow is updated to 3.34.0.** The development analyzer fixes out-of-root config reads and path probes when analyzing untrusted repository content.

### Documentation

- **Deployment guidance now distinguishes personal instances from shared ones.** The API reference clarifies the desktop-token and browser-private exceptions to personal-instance authentication.
- **New pages explain the public trial.** "Play on a public trial" (`docs/start/hosted-trial.md`) tells players what stays in the browser, what lives only in server memory, how their API key is used and who could obtain it. "Deploy a trial on Render" (`docs/start/deploy-render.md`) gives the operator's steps, free-plan limits and what not to configure. `docs/internals/storage.md` lists the browser-private data that never reaches a checkpoint, and `docs/internals/security.md` documents the in-memory credential handoff to background jobs.
- **Release acceptance covers real play through the local model relay, and the README stills show that play.** `docs/internals/e2e-testing.md` gives a repeatable procedure for the packaged desktop app and a production Docker deployment: default plugins, two turns, a restart and a third turn, failure recovery, and records without personal paths or credentials. Both READMEs keep the demo GIF and replace their older stills with current gameplay screenshots taken that way, each in its edition's language: the English images come from English saves, since a session's content language is fixed when it is created. The guide also covers framing and screenshot clipping for later captures.
- **Magpie setup explains the extra requirements for public trials.** The player guides now cover allowed web origins, real gateway keys, the key section hidden when sharing is off, browser local-network permission, and 403/401 failures, and distinguish the player's loopback address from Render and the desktop app's direct connection.

### Upgrade notes for v0.0.53

- **Rebuild the complete desktop application to receive the runtime change.** Target and actual Electron/Node versions, RunAsNode capability, main-bundle/ASAR binding, and child environment cleanup remain verified. Players do not need a separate Node installation, and existing save data does not need recreation.
- **Update the runtime as well as the source or dependencies.** Source deployments need Node 26.12.1 or a newer 26.x security release; rebuild and restart Docker deployments. Desktop users need a rebuilt application to receive its Node, Electron, and HTTP-client updates; they do not need to install Node separately. No save-data recreation is required.
- **A browser-private server refuses to start with `COVEL_ALLOWED_HOSTS`.** The Host check no longer runs on a production server with `STORE_BACKEND=memory`, so the setting has no effect; remove it.
- **Create a new browser test save to verify starting content.** Existing development saves are not reseeded automatically; their progress remains authoritative. A missing dependency can still be enabled from its in-game notice.

## [0.0.52] - 2026-10-16

This release closes the findings of a whole-codebase audit and makes plugin manifests shorter. Bundled plugins get no interface a community plugin lacks, an agent guard gets the same context as a function handler, and a self-hosted instance answers only the host names it is told about. Manifests drop the fields that had one allowed value or only repeated a default (`type`, `optional`, the `scheduled` trigger, `agent.llm`, `schedule.completion`, …), a world keeps each character's roleplay notes in its character record, and a plugin names only a model role, never its own model or endpoint. Function runtimes use the model role they declare, AI world generation uses the player's story model, and a generated world no longer copies an NPC's identity into the player. The store now records the order in which writes were saved and drops an unused event column (data schema 6), so **an SQLite or PostgreSQL database created by an earlier version or an earlier v0.0.52 development build is refused at startup and must be recreated**; browser-private saves need nothing. Dead and duplicated code was removed across the packages. A failed setup or turn shows one recovery notice that names the provider and the other model uses routed to it, starting without a model key asks before the game begins, and a built-in remote provider without a key fails at once instead of sending the request. Magpie is a built-in local provider. Emberback's Chinese edition and the English editions of the three Chinese worlds were translated again for meaning, with plainer names; the worlds' rules state the modifier the dice actually use, and Dice Check offers only ability attributes for a check. Read the [breaking changes](#breaking-changes-in-v0052) and the [upgrade notes](#upgrade-notes-for-v0052) before installing. macOS Apple Silicon and Windows x64 binaries are unsigned; the macOS build is not notarized.

Verified for this release, on the release candidate: `pnpm check`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm test:pg`, the full `pnpm e2e` suite (one stage-mode spec timed out under full-suite load and passed three of three runs alone), `pnpm release:preflight`, `pnpm --filter @covel/desktop build` with its staging boot check, `pnpm --filter @covel/desktop smoke:restart` on macOS, and a `Build Desktop` trial run that built the macOS and Windows packages. In a production web build on a fresh SQLite database, the Chinese edition of Emberback was played in a browser with a real model (`codex/gpt-6-luna` through a local Magpie relay): the missing-key prompt before the first start, adding Magpie in Settings, character creation with a rejected empty name, six turns with dice checks on ability attributes only, a page reload and a server restart that restored the game, and the narrow layout. That play found the Knowledge Codex dropping every entry after the UI-parts change, fixed before the tag. Earlier in the cycle `pnpm e2e:verify` with a real model passed on Mistport, Haruka Academy and Emberback, and Mistport, Haruka Academy and two worlds generated in the app were played in a browser. Not verified: the published installers were not started on Windows, no session was played in the desktop app, and no request went to a first-party OpenAI, Anthropic or Google endpoint.

### Breaking changes in v0.0.52

- **A background runtime that lists the `suspend` tool no longer loads.** A runtime with `schedule.background: true` or `schedule.detached: true` that lists `suspend` in `agent.tools.builtin` is rejected at load, because no player waits on background work. Remove `suspend` from such a runtime, or run it in the foreground. Rule: `docs/reference/tools.md` (suspend).
- **Agent completion and suspension follow the declared tools.** A `tool-use` runtime with `afterTools` must successfully call one of those tools; reads alone no longer satisfy completion, including after resume. Without `afterTools`, successful business tools still count. Completing-tool runtimes no longer receive automatic `runtime-done` authorization. Plugins that returned sentinel-shaped data from their own tools must instead declare and call `suspend` or `runtime-done` as appropriate. No stored-data migration is needed.
- **`MediaStore.resolveUrl()` and `ctx.media.resolveUrl()` have been removed.** Plugin authors should use `ctx.media.get(ref)` to read bytes or pass a `MediaRef` to the UI for display. Browser clients that need a fetchable URL use `GET /api/sessions/:id/media-token`. Custom media stores and test doubles should remove the method; existing media data needs no changes. See [Media store](../reference/media-store.md).
- **The JSON file settings backend now requires an explicit IPC transport.** Integrations must call `createJsonFileBackend({ ipc })` or choose another settings backend; the REST transport options and server-managed secret markers have been removed. Desktop settings continue to use IPC, and browser settings use localStorage. See `docs/reference/settings-store.md`.
- **World packages no longer declare `schemaVersion`.** Remove it from `world.yaml`, `data/world.data.yaml`, and `world.data.override.yaml`; the current format has no version selector. Loading ignores unknown top-level fields, and `pnpm validate:world` warns about them, so existing packages still load. Source records, plugin contracts, and stored-data versions keep their own version fields. Existing worlds and sessions do not need to be recreated.
- **Static tool policy accepts only `builtin` and `local` sources.** Host integrations must remove the unused `third-party` source and its wildcard rule from their configuration and use the registry’s actual source. Registered community tools are `local`; community server-code and runtime authorization remain separate and unchanged. No stored session data needs rebuilding.
- **SQLite media paths are now relative to the media directory.** Development databases containing absolute `media_assets.path` values must be recreated. There is no migration or compatibility read. Back up the old database and media before recreating data.
- **Remote theme resources no longer load directly.** Reimport background images to embed them, or use local resources; remote fonts must be replaced. Theme imports and appearance edits download HTTPS raster images with CORS support, up to 5 MiB each. Allowed Markdown images use the same download path, so an image from a host that does not allow cross-origin reads (CORS) no longer shows, even after the player allows it. Existing settings backups do not download remote resources automatically. See `docs/reference/theme-packages.md`.
- **A suspension also records what its saved writes would change.** `pendingContinuation.takenUnder.writes` holds, for every plugin data key, state field, character, character schema and lorebook entry the buffered proposals write, a digest of its content when the runtime suspended. The field is required: development suspensions and browser checkpoints that hold a suspension from an earlier build must be recreated.
- **Session event producers must capture their incarnation.** Server EventBus instances log and drop untagged events, and subscription envelopes and oversized transport references carry `sessionIncarnation`. Upgrade and restart all server processes together. Existing session metadata remains valid; no database recreation is needed.
- **Bundled plugins no longer get a store community plugins lack.** A bundled function handler or agent guard used to receive a "trusted" `ctx.store` that read the world record and characters and wrote plugin data and characters directly. Every handler and guard now gets the same read-only `ctx.store`. Read characters, the character schema and the world record through `ctx.world`, write plugin data through `ctx.pluginData`, and return other writes as proposals (`withPendingProposals`). Rules: `docs/reference/plugins.md`.
- **An agent guard gets the same context as a function handler.** A community guard used to get only a read-only store: no `pluginData`, logger, gateway, utils or media, and `recursiveCall` was refused. Every guard now gets the handler's context, including `ctx.inputs`, `ctx.execution` and `ctx.recursiveCall`; a community plugin's `utils` still reaches only the addresses its `permissions.http` declares. When the guard returns or times out, `ctx.signal` is aborted and these capabilities stop working, as they do for a handler.
- **Only the current development formats are accepted (F-073).** Regenerate traces whose payload was wrapped in `data`; recreate sessions/checkpoints containing duplicate open NPC relation versions or wrapped `dice-check` rolls/message values. Recreate wrapped `{ rule, ... }` world-rule seed records as flat rules and reimport/recreate their affected session data. Recreate portrait records that rely on arbitrary session-prefixed character IDs; exact IDs and the current `npc-<blueprintId>` mapping remain valid. Normal current-format sessions need no rebuild.
- Re-enter nested `settings.json` `entries.ui.locale` through the language selector (the stored key is `entries["ui.locale"]`). Rename bare `keys.env` provider names to `*_API_KEY`, or re-enter those keys in Settings. Unrecognized lines are preserved, warned about, and never used as credentials. Provider-ID dictionaries in APIs remain supported.
- Reimport custom themes after replacing `--surface-app`, `--surface-panel`, and `--surface-panel-strong` with `--surface-page`, `--surface-rail`, and `--surface-elevated`. The old `covel:scheme` localStorage entry is ignored; current `ui.scheme` settings remain valid. All event transport publishers must be upgraded/restarted together because `stream` is required.
- **`POST /api/ai/generate-world` takes `brief.pack` instead of `brief.experienceMode`.** The value is the ID of a pack of the catalogue; an unknown ID returns `400`. A brief without `pack` names no pack: the world gets no `pluginPolicy.preset` (it used to get `traditional-story`). Documented in `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **A kernel input takes only `from`.** `io.inputs.<name>` with `from: { kernel: turn-digest@1 }` rejects `scope`, `select`, `required`, `accepts` and `recordAs`; they never did anything. Remove them from your RUNTIME.md. See `docs/reference/plugins.md`.
- **The game applies the full UI spec rules.** A panel with a relative `repeat.statePath` or an `$item` / `$bindItem` that starts with `/` is no longer shown; `pnpm validate:plugin` already rejected both. The host API `loadPluginUi()` and `LoadedRuntime.uiSpecs` are gone; use `loadPluginUiSpecs()`.
- **The compiled runtime manifest no longer carries package fields.** `RuntimeManifest` drops `displayName`, `entry`, `extensions`, `tags`, `events`, `ui`, `i18n`, `commands` and `worldProjections`; the loader never filled them on a runtime. Read them from the package declaration (`PLUGIN.md`, `ParsedPluginMd.manifest`). `userSettings` and `dataSchemas` stay, copied from the package. In `/api/plugins`, a runtime summary has no `tags` and a runtime contract has no `ui`; `/api/plugin-flows` steps have no `uiSlots`. Package `tags` are unchanged.
- **The in-code inject kind `runtime` is gone.** `input.inject` no longer accepts `{ kind: "runtime", from, field, as }`, which no `PLUGIN.md` could produce. Code that builds manifests for `executeTurn` reads an upstream result with an `inputs` binding (`inputs: { name: { from: { runtime } } }`). `@covel/context` no longer exports the synchronous `buildInjectBlocks`.
- **One JSON Schema for the compiled manifest.** `runtime-manifest.input.schema.json` is renamed `runtime-manifest.compiled.schema.json` and titled as the internal compiled form; `runtime-manifest.authoring.schema.json`, `runtimeManifestAuthoringSchema` and the `authoringTriggerConfigSchema` alias are removed. Authors keep using `runtime-manifest.schema.json`.
- **`store.getSession()` returns a read-only session view, not the session record.** Function handlers, guards, tools and plugin actions now get `PluginSessionView`: `phase`, `completedPlayerTurns`, `locale` and `setupRuntimes` (each entry `state`, plus `resolution`, `lastError`, `reason` or `warning` where they apply). Code that read another field of the record (`id`, `activePlugins`, `status`, …) gets `undefined`; take the session ID and language from `ctx.sessionId` and `ctx.locale`. If the session no longer exists, `getSession()` throws instead of returning `null`. Fields: `docs/reference/plugins.md`.
- **`ctx.execution` holds only `origin` and `sourceTurnId`.** The kernel's `executionId`, `logicalTurnId` and `countPolicy` are no longer handed to plugin code.
- **`shortId` and `shortIdBatch` require the context's `random`, and `ctx.random` is never missing.** The SDK no longer falls back to `crypto.randomUUID` or `Math.random` when no random source is passed. Pass `ctx.random` (or `context.random` in a tool) as the fourth argument; in tests, `makeRandom()` from `@covel/plugin-test-utils`.
- **A plugin can no longer name a kernel or world-package path as its data file.** `contributes.data.<ns>.authoring.source.path` rejects `world.yaml`, `WORLD.md`, `data/world.data.yaml`, `data/dimensions.yaml`, `data/lorebook.yaml`, `characters/characters.json`, `media/gallery.json`, `media/portraits.json`, `media/scenes.json`, `media/gallery/` and their language versions, in any case. A manifest that names one fails to load; pick another path. Rules: `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **`ctx.recursiveCall()` reads only `playerMessage` and `manualTrigger: { runtimeId, payload }`.** Every other field of the delta is ignored: the nested run keeps the parent's session, language, player settings and model overrides, and a plugin can no longer pass retry seeds, `sourceTurnId`, `runtimeIds`, `triggerEvent` or `detachedStage`. A wrongly typed `playerMessage` or `manualTrigger` is rejected. Handler context: `docs/plugins/local-code.md`.
- **A `PostRuntime` hook can no longer add or change writes.** `pendingProposals`, `effects` and `completion` of the replaced result always come from the runtime, and a result whose `status` a hook changed carries no writes. A `PostToolUse` hook replaces only what the model (or `ctx.tools.call`) reads; the tool's writes, events, success and approval status stay. Use `PreStateCommit` to stop a write. Rules: `docs/reference/hooks.md`.
- **A misspelled `DEPLOYMENT_TIER` stops the server at startup.** A value other than `self`, `demo` or `commercial` (case and surrounding spaces do not count) was read as `commercial`; the server now refuses to start and the error lists the accepted values. A hosted tier also refuses to start with `COVEL_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (the Host check runs only on `self`), and `commercial` with `COVEL_MEDIA_CLEANUP_ENABLED` (the cleanup endpoint stays closed there). Remove the setting or correct the tier. Rules: `docs/internals/security.md#host-profile`.
- **FilterContainer rows now use `props.itemTemplate`.** Replace `itemComponent`, `itemPropMap` and `itemLiteralProps` with a catalog subtree and `$item` bindings. Bind `items` to a state array with `$state` or `$item`; `$index`, editable fields and nested repeats keep their original array positions after filtering. Existing session data does not need recreation.
- **The unused `contributes.ui.left` placement is removed.** Use `right` or `message`. Loading ignores unsupported placements with a warning; `validate:plugin` reports an unknown field. UI discovery and API responses no longer advertise or return `left`.
- **Data schema version 5: an earlier SQLite or PostgreSQL database is refused at startup.** `turn_messages` has a new `write_seq` column, and the new `session_write_counters` and `record_changes` tables are filled by triggers. A database recorded at data schema 4 stops the server at startup with `StoreSchemaVersionError` ("written by an older Covel (data schema 4; this version uses 5)"), before any table is created or changed; one from before the version was recorded is refused for the missing `turn_messages.write_seq` column. Back up the SQLite file with its `-wal` and `-shm` files and start with a new data directory or `SQLITE_PATH`, or point `DATABASE_URL` at a new, empty PostgreSQL database. Browser-private saves need nothing. See `docs/internals/storage.md`.
- **`readTurnMessages` cursors are save positions.** A cursor a plugin stored from an earlier version is rejected with `Invalid turn message cursor`; read from the start again (`after` omitted). Pages now come in the order messages were saved, which is the time order except for background output saved after a later turn.
- **Store contract changes for a custom `DataStore`.** `listTurnMessagesAfter(sessionId, after, limit)` takes the last `writeSeq` read (or `null`) and returns messages with their `writeSeq`. New: `listRecordChanges` (the change feed of lorebook entries, characters and plugin data, deletions included), `getCharacter`, and on the vector capability `getVectorPayloads`.
- **A world has one character file.** The roleplay notes of a character (persona, voice, secrets, rules, example lines, relationships) are written in its record in `characters/characters.json`, under `blueprint`, and no longer in a separate `characters/main-cast.json`. The notes do not repeat the record's name, aliases, description, type or fields: a `name` or similar field in `blueprint` fails `pnpm validate:world`. Relationship keys are character ids (`npc-su-yao`, not `su-yao`). `main-cast.json` is no longer read; move each card's notes into the record of its character. The four bundled worlds are converted. Rules: `docs/reference/world-data.md#one-character-file-per-world`.
- **Character Profiles matches a character by its id only.** `character-blueprint` (0.0.37) stores the notes keyed by the character's id and no longer matches `npc-<card id>`, for the notes and for portraits alike. The read-only "Preset Characters" panel is gone: the characters are in the character list. Development sessions of Emberback, Haruka Academy, Lantern Barrow and Mistport created before this change keep the old notes keyed by card id, which the narrative no longer reads; recreate them.
- **The world generator writes characters as character records.** A generated character has `id`, `name`, `type`, `description`, `aliases` and `fields`; `attributes`, `role`, `instantiate`, `schemaVersion` and loose persona fields are dropped. Roleplay notes, when requested, go into each character's `blueprint`.
- **`io.visibility: plugin` is gone.** A runtime is `story` (the narrative provider) or `system` (every other runtime), and `system` is the default, so delete `visibility: plugin` and `visibility: system`. A package that still says `plugin` does not load; the error names the file and the field. `RuntimeManifest.outputKind`, the hook payloads and `GET /api/framework/capabilities` list only `story` and `system`. The plugin list no longer shows the "Auxiliary" badge. The defaults are listed under "Defaults" in `docs/en/reference/plugins.md`.
- **The `scheduled` trigger is gone; an `auto` runtime takes `schedule.every`.** Replace `trigger: { type: scheduled, interval: N }` with `schedule.every: N` and drop `trigger.type: auto`, which is the default. `startTurn`, `cooldownTurns` and `maxTriggerCount` stay under `schedule.trigger`. A package that still says `scheduled` or `interval` does not load; the error names the file and the field. The runtime summaries of `GET /api/plugins` and the plugin flow carry `trigger.every` instead of `trigger.interval`.
- **An agent that leaves out its model slot or loop limits gets new defaults.** `agent.model` defaults to `story` for a `story` runtime and `plugin` for any other, where it used to fall back to the request's `default` binding. `agent.loop` defaults to `timeoutMs` 120000, `callTimeoutMs` 60000, `maxRetries` 3 (a `story` runtime: 240000, 120000, 1), where `maxRetries` used to default to 1. Write the old values if a runtime needs them.
- **Every bundled plugin whose manifest changed has a new version.** Setup runtimes of existing development sessions run again at the next player action; recreate a session if its setup guard does not skip work already done.
- **A runtime no longer writes `type`.** A runtime with a `function` block is a function runtime; one without is an agent. Delete `type: agent` and `type: function`; a function runtime that sets nothing else writes `function: {}`. `function.handler` defaults to `./handler.js` next to the manifest; a function runtime with neither a written handler nor that file does not run, and `pnpm validate:plugin` and installation report it. An installed package that still writes `type` loads with the field left out and a warning, and `pnpm validate:plugin` reports it as an error. Rules: "Two kinds of runtime" in `docs/en/reference/plugins.md`.
- **The root `optional` list is gone.** A contract that a runtime reads through `io.inputs` or `schedule.needs` but that `requires` does not list is optional: it binds when a provider is active, and a required input without a provider keeps the runtime from running. Delete `optional:`; a package that still writes it loads with the field left out and a warning, and `pnpm validate:plugin` reports it. `GET /api/plugins/:id` still returns `optional`, now worked out from the runtimes.
- **`contributes.data.*.authoring.source.kind` is gone.** The format comes from `path`: `.yaml`/`.yml`, `.json`, or a media directory without an extension. Delete `kind`, and `key: id` (`key: filename` for a media directory), which is the default. A package that still writes `kind` loads with the field left out and a warning.
- **`package.json` is optional, and its `version` is not read.** The version lives only in `PLUGIN.md`; the check that the two versions match is gone, and the install preview and update check read `PLUGIN.md`. A package without `package.json` loads its `.js` files as ES modules; one that has a `package.json` still declares `"type": "module"`. An installed package keeps working; delete `version` from its `package.json`.
- **The installer names a package that writes no `id` after its folder.** The ID is the written `id`, else the top-level folder of the ZIP, the selected repository folder or the repository name, else the `package.json` name without its scope and `plugin-` prefix. A `package.json` name no longer has to match the written `id`. Order: `docs/en/reference/plugin-installation.md`.
- **Every bundled plugin has a new version.** Setup runtimes of existing development sessions run again at the next player action; recreate a session if its setup guard does not skip work already done.
- **The settings of an agent sit directly under `agent`.** `agent.loop.completion` moves to `agent.completion`, `agent.llm.toolChoice` to `agent.toolChoice`, and `agent.llm.reasoningEffort: disabled` becomes `agent.reasoning: false`; `agent.llm` is gone and `agent.loop` keeps only the time, retry and step limits. Move the three fields up one level. Rules: "Completion conditions" and "Tool choice and reasoning" in `docs/en/reference/plugins.md`.
- **`schedule.completion` is now `schedule.detached`.** Write `detached: true` in place of `completion: { mode: detached }`, and `detached: { settle: true, maxSettleWaitMs: … }` in place of `settle: before-next-execution`; `maxQueueMs` and `maxExecutionMs` stay. `overlap` and `stalePolicy` are gone: each had one allowed value, and detached jobs of a runtime still run one at a time and a stale job is still rejected. `GET /api/plugins` and `GET /api/plugins/:id` return `turnCompletion.settle` as a boolean and no longer return `overlap` or `stalePolicy`. Jobs already queued keep working.
- **`schedule.manual.execution` is now `schedule.background`.** Write `background: true` in place of `manual: { execution: background }`, and delete `manual: { execution: sync }`.
- **A manifest that still writes a removed nested field does not load.** `agent.llm`, `agent.loop.completion`, `schedule.completion` and `schedule.manual` are unknown keys inside fields the host reads, so loading, installation and `pnpm validate:plugin` reject the manifest and name the file and field.
- **Every bundled plugin whose manifest changed has a new version** (affinity, branch-reply, char-creator, character-blueprint, chat-mode-narrator, codex, dice-check, guide, history-compaction, memory, narrator, npc-graph, scene-stage, soundtrack, story-events, world-init, world-ir, world-time). Setup runtimes of existing development sessions run again at the next player action; recreate a session if its setup guard does not skip work already done. `guide` and `world-ir` now ask the model to call a tool on every step instead of naming their one tool, which is the only tool each of them sees.
- **A world's `pluginPolicy` is one built-in pack plus a few plugins.** Write `preset` (a pack of `packs/builtin.yaml`), `plus` (the plugins the world also wants), and optionally `pitch` (the `label`, `description` and `reason` the session-prep screen shows for that pack), `packs` (the IDs of the packs the player may pick) and `requires`. `presetId`, `requested`, `recommended`, `preferredTags` and `avoidedTags` are ignored with a `manifest-unknown-field` warning, and `packs` written as objects (packs a world defined itself) make the whole `pluginPolicy` invalid, so such a world starts with no plugin selection. Rewrite the policy of every world you wrote; worlds in `COVEL_USER_WORLDS_DIR` made by an earlier AI world creation must be generated again or edited by hand. `kind: core` plugins are never listed: every session has them. Reference: `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **The languages of a world come from its files.** `supportedLocales` is no longer a field of `world.yaml`: a world has an edition in each language that has a `world.<locale>.yaml` or a `WORLD.<locale>.md` at its root, plus `defaultLocale`. Delete the field. A `world.<locale>.yaml` or `WORLD.<locale>.md` now makes an edition even when the field did not list its language.
- **`dimensionSources` is removed.** Declare dimensions inline in `world.yaml` or as the whole table in `data/dimensions.yaml`.
- **A descriptor only writes what differs from the conventions.** Every world package is read by convention, with or without `data/world.data.yaml`. An entry whose `path` is a conventional source's path changes only the fields it writes (`enabled: false` turns the source off); any other entry adds a source. A descriptor that listed every conventional file still loads, but a conventional file it left out is now imported: turn it off with `enabled: false`, and delete the entries that repeat the conventions. Conventional sources have new IDs (for example `blueprints` instead of `cast`, `rules-campus-life` instead of `campusRules`), so an override descriptor that names a source of a bundled world must use the new ID, and sessions of the bundled worlds created before this change should be created again.
- **A plugin can no longer bring its own model or endpoint.** Before, an `llm.toml` inside a plugin's directory could set a provider, model, `baseUrl` and `protocol` for a role (`[plugin.<role>]`); the server used it in place of the player's choice, and the player's request keys followed it to the plugin's address. The file is no longer read; a plugin that shipped one now uses the player's model for that role. A plugin names only a role with `agent.model` / `function.model`; the player's (or operator's) model settings decide the model and where requests go. `pnpm validate:plugin` warns when a plugin still ships an `llm.toml`. Rules: `docs/reference/slots.md`.
- **`ctx.recursiveCall` rejects targets outside the caller's own plugin package.** Third-party plugins that named another package's runtime must use services for requests and responses, or events to trigger follow-up work. The error identifies the target runtime and caller package before nested execution starts. Bundled and community plugins follow the same rule; see [nested calls](../en/reference/plugins.md#nested-calls-ctxrecursivecall).
- **A suspension records what it was taken under.** Each suspension now saves the number of player turns in the history and its plugin's version (`pendingContinuation.takenUnder`). Development suspensions and browser checkpoints that hold a suspension from an earlier build must be recreated.
- **Data schema version 6: a database written at data schema 5 is refused at startup.** The `events` table drops its unused `target_runtime` column. A SQLite or PostgreSQL database recorded at data schema 5 stops the server with `StoreSchemaVersionError` before any table is changed. Back up the SQLite file with its `-wal` and `-shm` files and start with a new data directory or `SQLITE_PATH`, or point `DATABASE_URL` at a new, empty PostgreSQL database. Browser-private saves need nothing. See `docs/internals/storage.md`.
- **UI render blocks require `parts`.** The `{ type, ...content }` single-block shape is no longer accepted. Tools and handlers must send `{ parts: [...] }`; recreate development sessions containing uncommitted effects in the old shape. See `docs/reference/tools.md`.
- **Form fields require `name`.** The submission handler no longer accepts `id` as an alias. Update field definitions and recreate development sessions with pending forms that use only `id`.
- **Session events use one current payload shape.** `ui.rendered` always includes the committed `render` and `block`; `narrative.delta`, `narrative.completed`, and `runtime.started` require `kind`. Custom event producers must send these fields. Clients no longer rebuild UI blocks or infer a kind from runtime labels. See the session protocol reference.
- **`POST /api/ai/generate-world` takes `concept` only.** Clients must send `concept` instead of the removed `prompt` synonym. See `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **Generated world records no longer store `metadata.generatedPackageSummary`.** Clients must derive counts from `metadata.embeddedCharacters`, `metadata.embeddedLorebook` or the world package content.
- **`create-form` number and checkbox defaults must be numbers and booleans.** String defaults such as `"5"` and `"true"` are rejected. Plugin authors must update those fields to `5` and `true`; recreate affected development forms. See `docs/reference/tools.md`.

### Added

- **Root `permissions.http` in `PLUGIN.md`.** A package whose entry code calls an outside service declares the origin there. See `docs/reference/plugins.md`.
- **The game screen says which world data is missing.** The create-session response lists the world data that was not imported (`worldDataNotices`), and the game screen shows it, with a note that the data arrives when the plugin is turned on. Enabling a plugin returns what it imported (`worldData`), and the notice is updated.
- **A "Narrative Only" gameplay pack.** It turns on the narrator and World Dimensions and turns the other always-on plugins off: no opening form, character creation, world time, Story Memory or history compaction. It starts every bundled world.
- **Gameplay packs can turn plugins off.** A pack's `excluded` lists plugins, `kind: core` ones included, that choosing the pack turns off; the player can turn them back on, and the next pack lifts only the exclusions this one added. Reference: `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **"Back to the minimal set" on the session prep screen.** One click keeps only the narrative plugins, the plugin that holds the world's dimensions and what the world requires, and turns everything else off.
- **Self-hosters get a SQLite Docker stack, and it is the default.** `docker/docker-compose.sqlite.yml` runs one app container with no database service. The SQLite file, its media files and the installed worlds and plugins live in a named volume (`SQLITE_PATH=/home/node/.covel/data/covel.db`), so they survive recreating the container. It keeps the loopback binding and `DEPLOYMENT_TIER=self`, and it refuses to start without `COVEL_MEDIA_TOKEN_SECRET`. Run it with `pnpm docker:build`; details in `docs/reference/env-registry.md#docker-compose`.
- **Every plugin can import the plugin SDK and `zod` without shipping them.** The host resolves `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils` (with its `/dimensions` and `/prompts` subpaths) and `zod` to its own copies for the entry, handlers, guards and world projections of every plugin, bundled or installed. A plugin copied out of the repository loads as it does inside it; any other library it uses must still be bundled. `docs/plugins/essentials.md`, `docs/ship/package.md`.
- **`pnpm validate:world` checks the dimensions that plugin data names.** `contributes.data.<ns>.authoring.references` accepts `to: world-dimension`, and a reference `path` can write `**.` before a field to find it at any depth (`when.**.dimension`). A value that is not a dimension the world declares, or a path beside it (`within`) that the dimension's schema does not allow, is a `dimension-reference` error. story-events declares the dimensions in its event conditions, so a misspelled dimension ID or path, which made an event never fire, is reported before a session starts. Documented in `docs/reference/plugins.md` and `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **A session tells the player when its memory index was built with another embedding model, and rebuilds it in one click.** Before, changing the embedding model paused vector memory in older sessions until the old model settings were restored, retained the old vectors, and logged a warning after every turn. Now the session shows "Memory search is matching keywords only" with a "Rebuild index" button ("Clear index" when no embedding model is configured). The rebuild measures the new model first and changes nothing when its configuration is invalid or it does not answer; then one transaction deletes the session's old vectors and index progress and locks it to the new model, and the index is built again in the background. Endpoints: `GET /api/sessions/:id/memory-index` and `POST /api/sessions/:id/memory-index/rebuild` (`docs/reference/api.md`).
- **The plugin SDK types everything the host passes to a function handler or guard.** `PluginFunctionContext` in `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils` now has `execution`, `recursionDepth`, `recursiveCall`, `session`, `activation`, `gateway`, `utils`, and the media capabilities `media`, `images`, `speech`, `music` and `assetProgress`, with their types (`PluginImages`, `PluginMediaRef`, …) and `PluginTurnDigest` for the `turn-digest@1` kernel input. The host builds its context from these types, and a compile-time check plus a runtime test fail when the two drift apart.
- **"Restore the package version" brings an edited world back to its files.** The world's detail page shows that a package world was edited and offers the package version back (`POST /api/worlds/:id/restore-package`); after that the record follows its files again. Documented in `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **Filtered rows can combine layouts, buttons and badges.** Their templates use the normal json-render scope and action handlers, with shared static and runtime validation.
- **`$map` displays translatable labels for data values.** Translate labels and string fallbacks through plugin locale catalogs. Character trait type and category badges now use these labels.
- **The player is told which plugins are off and why.** The session prep screen lists the plugins a selection will turn off, what each one needs and which installed plugin provides it, with a button to turn that plugin on. The game screen does the same for the session's plugins that cannot run, in the player's language.
- **An explanation when no plugin tells the story.** When none of the enabled plugins writes narration, the session prep screen and the game screen say that turns will not tell a story and offer the installed narrative plugins, or point to Settings → Install & manage when none is installed.
- **A plugin can take its data from the world's character records.** `contributes.data.<namespace>.authoring.characterField` names a field of a character record (in place of `source`); the kernel imports the record as a character either way and gives that field to the active plugins that accept the contract, keyed by the character's id. Without such a plugin the field is ignored. `pnpm validate:world` checks the field against the receiver's schema, warns when no plugin reads it or a new session does not enable one, and locale files translate it like the rest of the record. `pnpm describe:authoring` and `GET /api/framework/authoring` report `characterField`. Reference: `docs/reference/plugins.md`.
- **A world kept in a store imports its characters like a package.** `metadata.embeddedCharacters` goes through the same import as `characters/characters.json`, so plugin fields in those records reach their plugins and the import ledger records each character.
- **`pnpm validate:plugin` warns about a field written with its default value.** It names the file and field (`is the default value; remove it`) and the package still passes. It also warns when a package with an `entry` module or a setup runtime has no `version`, since setup reruns only when the version changes.
- **`agent.finishWith: <tool>` ends an agent after one tool.** The run completes once that tool succeeds and fails without it, and the model must call a tool on every step unless `agent.toolChoice` says otherwise. It replaces `completion: { require: tool-use, afterTools: [<tool>] }` with `toolChoice: required`; the full `completion` stays for several tools or `runtime-done`, and the two cannot be written together.
- **Hooks and extensions have a one-line form.** `contributes.hooks: [PreToolUse]` lists hook events by name, and `contributes.extensions: [prompt.segment@1/notes]` writes an extension as `<point>/<id>`. The object form stays for `enforce`, `order`, `slot`, `watch` and `preview`.
- **`pnpm validate:plugin` warns about more fields written with their default or in a long form.** New defaults: `schedule.detached: false`, `schedule.detached.settle: false`, `schedule.background: false`, `agent.reasoning: true`, `agent.toolChoice: required` beside `finishWith`, and `contributes.extensions[].order: 0`. A hook with only `event`, an extension with only `point` and `id`, and the long form of `finishWith` each get a warning that names the shorter form. Table: "Defaults" in `docs/en/reference/plugins.md`.
- **The smallest world is `name`, `summary`, `defaultLocale` and `WORLD.md`.** `id` is optional: a world takes the name of its folder, or of its ZIP file when installed from one. `docs/worlds/first-world.md` starts from this world.
- **Rule files go in `data/rules/*.yaml`.** A plugin's `authoring.source.path` can end in `*.<extension>`: each such file of the folder is a conventional source, imported in file-name order, so a world adds a set of rules by adding a file.
- **`pnpm validate:world` reports values that need not be written.** A `default-value` warning names a `characterSchema.types` equal to the default `[npc, companion]` and a `kind: core` plugin in `pluginPolicy.plus`; `unknown-pack`, `preset-not-offered` and `pitch-without-preset` check the new policy fields.
- **Plugin lists show partial-load warnings.** Session prep, the session plugin list, and installed plugins in Settings identify each unavailable runtime and ignored manifest field, with expandable reasons. Plugin API responses carry the same diagnostics with package-relative paths, and reloading a corrected plugin clears its warnings.
- **A notice for plugins a save cannot run.** When a confirmed plugin is not installed, failed to load, or its installed version lacks a provider or conflicts with another member, the game screen names it and play goes on without it. `GET /api/sessions/:id/plugins` lists them in `unavailable`. Such a plugin is off everywhere: no runtime, hook, service, action, form validator, panel or memory search reaches it, and a direct call to one of its actions returns `404 plugin_not_active` with the reason.
- **The showcase website tells a visitor how to open the unsigned app.** Under the download button there is now a note for the visitor's system. On macOS, which reports the build as "damaged" after a browser download, it gives the command that removes the quarantine attribute: `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/Covel.app`. On Windows, where SmartScreen shows "Windows protected your PC", it names the two clicks: "More info", then "Run anyway".
- **`pnpm validate:world` checks the character attributes that plugin data names.** A plugin declares in `contributes.data.<namespace>.authoring.references` which values of its records are the `id` of an attribute of the world's `characterSchema` (`path`, `to: character-attribute`, optional `type` and `within`). A value that names no attribute, one of the wrong type, or a number outside the attribute's `min` and `max` is an `attribute-reference` error with the file and the place in it. `tabletop-rules` declares it for its point-buy rules, so a mistyped attribute `id` in `data/tabletop-rules.json` no longer waits until a session starts to fail. Rules: `docs/reference/plugins.md`, `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **`pnpm validate:world` warns about a field that no model reads.** A field of a world-data record or a character whose name ends in `At` or is `timestamp`, with an ISO time as its value, is left out of what a model reads as bookkeeping. Each one now gives a `model-hidden-field` warning that names the file, the record and the field. Rename a date of the story (`date`, `diedOn`) or write it in another form to keep it visible. An `id`, or a field whose name ends in `Id` / `Ids`, that holds a UUID is left out the same way and gets the same warning, with the hint to use a readable ID such as `npc-lin-yao`.
- **Worlds show when their package has changed.** Bundled and installed worlds display an "Update available" badge when files on disk differ from the imported version. "Restore the package version" applies the package’s world data and settings while preserving saved sessions.
- **Magpie is a built-in local provider.** Pick it under "Provider" when adding a provider: it fills in `http://127.0.0.1:3425/v1` with OpenAI Chat and needs no API key. A model can switch to OpenAI Responses or Anthropic Messages under its "API protocol". See the [slot reference](../reference/slots.md).

### Changed

- **A normal shutdown puts unfinished background jobs back in the queue.** A job that had not started its commit used to end as `cancelled` (`worker-shutdown`) and needed a retry by hand. It now goes back to `queued` with the same job ID once its run has stopped, and runs again after the restart; a job already committing finishes its commit. What the stopped run did outside (a provider call) is done again, so a background job must be safe to rerun. A crash still ends in `orphaned`. Details: `docs/reference/protocol.md`.
- **Model lookup prices live only in `capability.pricing`.** `GET /api/model-db/lookup` no longer duplicates token prices directly under `capability`; clients must read the nested fields. No stored model data needs to be recreated.
- **More natural English in Mistport and Haruka Academy, and plainer hints in the app.** The English editions of the two Chinese worlds read less like a translation and use the names their world guides use. The English guide of Haruka Academy has its whole opening scene again, and a draft leftover in its opening scenario is gone. Longer hints and descriptions in the settings, session and theme screens say what happens in plain words.
- **Plainer wording in a few places.** The Chinese guide of the Emberback world reads as Chinese prose instead of a close translation, the onboarding guide no longer asks a question in a heading and names the labels the game shows during a turn, and the first-session guide and the Mistport pack description lose a piece of jargon each.
- **Covel refuses a database written by another data schema instead of failing later.** SQLite records the data schema version in `PRAGMA user_version` and PostgreSQL in `covel_schema_meta`. A database recorded with another version, or an older one that lacks a column, is refused at startup before any table is touched, with `StoreSchemaVersionError` telling you to back up and use a new data directory; the desktop app shows this on its startup screen. Before, such a database opened and then failed with `no such column` on use. Rule: `docs/internals/storage.md#capabilities-and-migrations`.
- **Desktop builds use Electron 44.6.0.** The runtime and packaging tools receive stable fixes; the macOS 13 minimum is unchanged.
- **Source toolchains pin pnpm 12.10.1 and Node 26.10.0.** Mise, CI and the Docker image pin Node 26.10.0, the newest release eligible for the seven-day release window at this update. Dependency installation retains that waiting period without an Oxlint exception.
- **Switching the desktop data directory can bring your data along.** After you pick a new directory in Settings → Desktop, Covel asks whether to copy your existing data or start empty. With "Copy my data", the next start copies the database files and `worlds/` to the new location before the backend opens the database, and switches `data_root` only after the copy succeeded; a dialog reports the result. Nothing in the old directory is moved or deleted, nothing in the new one is overwritten (a directory that already holds Covel data is used as it is), and a failed copy is removed while the app keeps using the old directory. Editing `config.toml` by hand and `PUT /api/config/data-root` still copy nothing; the settings page now says where the old data stays. Rules: `docs/reference/desktop-config.md`.
- **`pnpm docker:*` now runs the SQLite stack; PostgreSQL moved to `pnpm docker:pg:*`.** `docker:up`, `docker:build`, `docker:down`, `docker:down-all` and `docker:logs` use `docker/docker-compose.sqlite.yml`. The PostgreSQL stack in `docker/docker-compose.yml` is unchanged and has the same five commands as `docker:pg:up`, `docker:pg:build`, `docker:pg:down`, `docker:pg:down-all` and `docker:pg:logs`.
- **`.env.example` has the PostgreSQL variables commented out.** SQLite needs none of them. Uncomment the "PostgreSQL (optional)" block before `pnpm db:up`, `pnpm dev:pg`, `pnpm test:pg` or `pnpm docker:pg:build`.
- **Plainer wording in the world, recipe, shipping and plugin guides.** A few sentences in these chapters of the documentation read more naturally, in Chinese and in English; no instruction or example changed.
- **The Chinese edition of Emberback reads as Chinese.** Its characters, dimensions, items, quests, rules, lorebook and memory blocks were rewritten from close translation into plain Chinese, with the names the Chinese world guide uses. A leftover English fragment in a character's fears is gone, and dice checks use the glossary's Chinese term everywhere.
- **A runtime has one waiting suspension.** When a runtime suspends again, its older suspension is voided in the same transaction, settled like an abandon (its turn may count and release held-back jobs). The resume-time `superseded` reason of `suspension_outdated` is gone, and the web client drops the older item when the new `turn.suspended` arrives.
- **Bundled plugins declare the SDK and `zod` only as development dependencies.** Their `package.json` files no longer list runtime `dependencies`, so each bundled plugin passes the same package check the installer applies to a ZIP, GitHub or collection install. Plugin authors list these two packages under `devDependencies` too: `docs/ship/publish.md`.
- **A changed embedding model is logged once per session.** Ingestion notes the mismatch once instead of after every turn, and memory search falls back to keywords without a warning each time. An index write now names the model it was embedded with, so vectors from the model an index was moved away from are refused (`docs/reference/slots.md`).
- **AI world creation offers every gameplay pack.** The "Player Experience" choices are now the packs of the server's catalogue (`packs/builtin.yaml`), with their own names and descriptions, so "Low Cost" and any pack added later can be chosen too; before, only two fixed choices existed. A pack's `defaultViewMode` is data of the pack: a world generated for "Dialogue Mode" opens on the stage because that pack says so, not because the generator knows its ID. `GET /api/framework/authoring` lists the catalogue in a new `packs` field, and `pnpm describe:authoring` prints it.
- **Plugin code picks instructions by the same rule as prompt bodies.** `pickLocaleText` now chooses with `instructionLocaleFor`, so `COVEL_INSTRUCTION_LOCALE` also switches the instructions that bundled plugins write in code (dice checks, story events, scene cast, memory, NPC relationships, …), and every Simplified Chinese tag such as `zh-Hans-CN` or `zh-SG` reads the Chinese ones. Before, forcing `en` still left Chinese blocks in a Chinese session.
- **A plugin's own prompt templates exist only in English and Simplified Chinese.** `createPromptLoader` from `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils/prompts` reads `<name>.md` and, for a Chinese session, `<name>.zh.md`; other language files are not read and `pnpm check:prompts` reports them. History summaries in a Russian session now get English instructions and are still written in Russian. The framework's own `prompts/` keep their lookup by any locale.
- **An unknown manifest field no longer stops a plugin.** When `PLUGIN.md`, a `RUNTIME.md` or the inline `runtime` has a field this version does not know, such as an old field that was removed or one written for a newer Covel, the plugin loads and installs without that field and the server log names the file and the field. `pnpm validate:plugin` still reports it as an error. An unknown key inside a known field (`permissions.fs`, `io.conceal`) and a misspelling of a field that limits or identifies the package (`permision`, `gaurd`, `covle`) still fail the load. See `docs/reference/plugins.md`.
- **One copy of the manifest rules.** Loading, `pnpm validate:plugin` and installation check RUNTIME.md and the inline `runtime` with the same schema, and UI specs with the same rules, so a package that passes validation is not dropped or stripped in the game. A manual or event runtime with `schedule.manual.execution: background` that reads a same-execution input, another plugin's `pluginId` in a UI spec, a newer `specVersion`, a webview entry that is not a local `.html` of at most 512 KiB, and an unknown `surfaces` value are now reported by validation instead of failing in the game.
- **A runtime that can never run no longer hides itself.** An `auto` runtime without `schedule.stage`, or a background manual/event runtime that reads a same-execution input, is left out at load with the file, field and reason in the server log; the rest of its package loads, and its output contract no longer counts as provided. Validation and installation report it as an error.
- **One broken UI spec hides only itself.** A spec that cannot be read or breaks a rule is reported in `/api/ui-specs` `diagnostics[]`; the plugin's other panels still show.
- **An empty account or a refused key is no longer retried at the same provider, and now switches to a configured backup.** A 429 `insufficient_quota`, a 402, a 401 / 403, or a 400 that says the balance is used up is sent once: the HTTP layer and the runtime no longer resend it, and a fallback model the player configured takes over (before, a 429 quota error was resent up to three times and a 402 or 401 never reached the backup). The kinds are `ProviderFailureKind` and `isAccountFailure` in `@covel/shared`. Rules: `docs/reference/slots.md`.
- **A world starts even when something it relies on is missing.** A contract the world requires (`pluginPolicy.requires`) with no provider, or with several and none chosen, no longer stops session creation with `400 world_requirement_unmet`: the session starts without that part, the session-prep screen says what is missing instead of turning the start button off, and the response lists it in `unmetRequirements`. Documented in `docs/reference/api.md` and `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **A plugin action has 30 seconds.** An action handler runs inside the session lock; one that does not finish in 30 seconds now fails with `504 handler_timeout` and releases the session after already-started store calls settle, instead of holding every request for the session (deleting it included) until the server restarts. The handler gets `ctx.signal`, which aborts at the limit, and its `ctx.store` refuses every call after the action ends. Writes made before the limit are kept. Details: `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **The world record is the world; a package's files are only what it was installed from.** New sessions, the world-data preflight and `sync-data` / `sync-dimensions` now use the dimensions and the character schema of the world record, so dimensions saved in the world editor or imported on the session-prep screen reach new sessions and syncs for bundled, installed and generated package worlds too (before, a world with a package read them from its files and the edit had no effect). The characters, lorebook entries, plugin data and media under `data/` and `media/` are still read from the package. Rules: `docs/reference/world-data.md` ("World records and world packages").
- **A generated world keeps its characters under one key.** The record of a world the generator made no longer repeats its characters in `metadata.characterBlueprints`; they are in `metadata.embeddedCharacters`, as for every other world. Worlds made by earlier builds already have both keys and keep working.
- **The deployment form is decided once, at startup.** The server combines the tier, the storage it runs on and the tokens into a host profile (`apps/server/src/host-profile.ts`); route guards, media routes, server settings, a request's own model settings and trace retention ask it instead of reading environment variables. `@covel/ai-provider` receives its local-network and community-wire rules from the host (`configureProviderHostPolicy`) and no longer reads `DEPLOYMENT_TIER`. The desktop app always starts its server with `DEPLOYMENT_TIER=self`. A test lists every API route with the host actions it needs.
- **`POST /api/llm-config/reload` refuses a missing desktop token with `desktop_rest_token_invalid`**, the same code as the other desktop config routes (it was `unauthorized`; the status stays `401`).
- **One prefix for every temporary media reference.** World-data imports now pin their bytes as `media-pin:<uuid>` instead of `world-data-import:<uuid>`, and `POST /api/media/cleanup` expires `media-pin:` references older than 24 hours. A `world-data-import:` reference left in development data no longer expires; recreate that data or remove the reference by hand.
