- **A missing dependency turns its dependents off instead of refusing the game.** Turning off a plugin others need (say Pre-Game Setup, which Character Creator needs) no longer makes session creation fail with a contract name. The plugins that need it are turned off, the game starts, and they come back once the dependency is turned on again. Creation now fails only when a plugin the player asked for conflicts with another one, with `400 plugin_rejected` naming the plugin. Rules: `docs/reference/plugins.md` (session plugin selection).
- **Bundled manifests and the plugin scaffold write only what cannot be defaulted.** Defaults such as `cardinality: one`, `required: true`, `format: summary`, `enforce: normal`, `role: system` and `contributes.data.*.version: 1` are left out, and the `analyst` scaffold no longer sets its own loop limits.
- **The loader works out the fields an author used to repeat.** `id` is the directory name, `kind` is `plugin`, `entry` is `./server/index.js` when that file exists, `provides` takes in every runtime's `io.output.contract`, and the `ui:*` and `cost:*` tags come from the package's panels, actions and runtimes (a written one is ignored; authors write only tags such as `mode:*`). A written `id` must still equal the directory name. `pnpm validate:plugin` warns about a written value equal to the worked-out one. A plugin can now be one `PLUGIN.md` with a description, a stage and a prompt. Table: "Defaults" in `docs/en/reference/plugins.md`.
- **The default scaffold writes no `package.json`, and bundled manifests drop the derived fields.** `pnpm create-plugin` writes `version: "0.1.0"` in `PLUGIN.md` only; `--with-tools` keeps a workspace `package.json` without `version`. The guides `first-plugin.md` and `prompt-only.md` start from the one-file plugin.
- **Docker Compose and CI use pgvector 0.8.7** on PostgreSQL 18 (`pgvector/pgvector:0.8.7-pg18`, pinned by its multi-platform digest).
- **A plugin package must declare `"type": "module"` in its `package.json`.** `pnpm validate:plugin` and the ZIP and GitHub installers now refuse a package without it. Plugin packages were always ESM; without the line Node read the package's `.js` files as CommonJS, so an `import` failed and the development reload never picked up a change. The bundled plugins and both scaffolds already declare it. An existing package without the line needs it added.
- **The prep screen names plugin tags and summarizes world data in the player's words.** The derived tags show as "Calls a model", "No model calls", "Side panel", "Message cards" and "Player actions" instead of `cost:llm` or `ui:manual-action`, in the filter chips, the group headings and the plugin badges, and a plugin row shows all its tags. The world-data section counts what will be imported (characters, lore entries, images, plugin records) instead of listing storage addresses.
- **Turning one plugin on or off says what else moved.** When the switch also turned on a provider another plugin needs, or turned off a plugin that conflicts with the one chosen, a short notice names them and says why.
- **The prep screen survives a reload of the tab.** The world being prepared is remembered for the tab; opening the app anew still starts on the world list.
- **World-data notices carry a code.** `worldDataNotices` items of `POST /api/sessions` and the import after a plugin is turned on have an optional `code` (`dimension-provider-missing`, `receiver-missing`); the game screen says those in the interface language and folds any other English message under "Details". `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **A refused toggle names the provider that must stay on.** The `409 dimension_provider_required` response has `details.pluginId` and `details.displayName`, and the toast names that plugin. A refusal the app knows no longer prints the raw response under the toast.
- **A save keeps the plugins the player confirmed.** The plugin list, the slash commands and the executor now read one set: the session's `activePlugins`, checked against the installed versions. A plugin installed later, a `core` one included, no longer joins an old save, and the list no longer shows a plugin as enabled when it does not run (or offers a command that then answers `command_not_active`). A development hot reload no longer rewrites `activePlugins` either. To recompute the set from the installed plugins, enable or disable a plugin. Rules: `docs/reference/plugins.md` (session plugin selection).
- **Inside `withTransaction`, write only through `tx`.** A `DataStore` write made through the root store from inside the callback now throws `RootStoreWriteInTransactionError` on every backend and writes nothing; it used to join the transaction on SQLite and commit on its own on PostgreSQL. Plugin authors are not affected: plugins never see the store.
- **An out-of-date suspension is withdrawn instead of resumed.** When the player has played another turn or the plugin changed version, `POST /api/sessions/:id/suspensions/:suspensionId/resume` voids the suspension and answers `409` with `code: "suspension_outdated"`; the web client removes it and tells the player. Rules: `docs/reference/api.md` (Out-of-date suspended items).
- **`queryDimensionSnapshot` builds its model text without bookkeeping.** The `_text` it returns from `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils/dimensions` now selects paths and pages inside the `modelFacingJson` projection, as `world-dimension-get` already did, so a plugin tool that hands it to a model never shows a session ID, row UUID or write time. `results` keep the full values. Rule: [`world-dimension-get`](../reference/tools.md#world-dimension-get).
- **The Chinese edition of Emberback reads as natural Chinese.** Every text in `worlds/emberback` was translated again for meaning rather than word for word, and checked line by line against the English: 37 meaning errors are fixed (among them the starting location, the convoy states and the signal-log values the model must write). The five player attributes have plainer Chinese labels, and four names that were wrong in meaning are renamed: the Switchyard, the Long Blackout, Mirror Farm (no longer sharing a name with the Solar Fields) and June Okafor. Sessions created before this change keep the text they were created with.
- **Emberback's Chinese names are plainer and used one way throughout.** The Chinese edition renames the place, the Duskline, the Crownfire and its front, the Nightglass observatory, the Nightward Shelf and the Condenser Yard; Dr. Mina Park, Priya Nair and Tomas Reed's title; the linekeeper, the crawler and Echo Reading with its two top ranks; the three history events; and the storm reader, trade chits, cryo-pack and field radio. Only the Chinese files change; the English edition, ids and keys are untouched. Sessions created before this change keep the names they were created with.
- **Provider account failures explain which model uses share the problem.** Setup, recovery and failed-task notices name the provider and list the session’s active model uses routed to it. The failed provider and model survive reloads; authentication recovery opens provider settings to fix the shared key.
- **Starting without a model key offers a choice.** Session prep names the providers and text uses missing credentials, with actions to add a key or start anyway. Built-in remote providers without a key fail before any network request and are not retried; local and custom endpoints can still work without keys. Missing-key notices distinguish an absent key from an invalid one.
- **Setup and failed turns have one clear recovery notice.** The notice explains the recorded cause, links to model settings when a key, permission, endpoint or rate limit needs attention, and keeps retry in one place. Header and composer labels no longer repeat the failure while recovery is visible; timeline summaries stay muted with expandable details. Setup execution records stay neutral after play starts or the page reloads. See [Built-in App Features](../en/builtin/features.md#retry).
- **API validation errors are more consistent.** Lorebook writes and model list/ping requests report coded JSON-body errors; session execution/control and world-data sync report `session_not_found` for missing sessions. Plugin RPC field errors and session plugin mutation errors use shared validation wording. Status codes and approval requirements are unchanged. See [HTTP API](../reference/api.md).
- **The English editions of Harukaze Academy, Mistport and Lantern Barrow read as natural English.** Every English text of the three Chinese worlds was translated again for meaning rather than word for word and checked line by line against the Chinese by a second reviewer. About 60 meaning errors are fixed, among them lines that said the opposite of the source (a tide stage labelled "Receded", a lesson "too cheap to call a monopoly"), a student committee chair called "Head Librarian", and a torch that burned "about an hour" where the world's clock counts in phases. Characters have distinct voices, and some player-facing labels are plainer (Harukaze Academy's relationship stages are now Just met / Friends / Crush / On the verge / Together). Some names are clearer: Harukaze Academy, Mistport: The Riftide Chronicle, Captain Grey, Speaker Chen, the Tide-Seal Decree, the Tidewielder, the deep ebb, President Haruka Saegusa, the Newspaper Club and its Fact-or-Rumor Wall, and the Evening Breeze Lanterns. Sessions created before this change keep the text they were created with.
- **Lantern Barrow's English edition has plainer names.** The world is now "Lantern Barrow: The First Delve", the keeper is the Lampkeeper (Lampkeeper Ysolde, the Lampkeeper's Cottage) and the marsh-king is the Marsh King. Sessions created before this change keep the names they were created with.

### Removed

- **Unreachable bundled tool registrations are removed.** `list-npc-graph`, `set-world-schema`, and `set-world-dimensions` had no runtime callers. World initialization keeps its private schema and proposal helpers behind `initialize-world`.
- **The IndexedDB `MediaStore` and the `@covel/store/indexeddb` entry point.** Nothing used them: browser-private play keeps its media on the server, and the web app's display cache is separate. `MediaStore.delete()` also loses its `opts.force` parameter, which no backend read; `delete(id)` always removes the asset with every claim on it.
- Removed the unused package import entry for the private runtime test harness. Plugin authors continue to run cases with `pnpm test:runtime`.
- **`POST /api/events/emit` no longer takes `targetRuntime`.** The field never chose a receiver: the event goes to the clients subscribed to the session and triggers no runtime. A request that still sends the field succeeds, and the field is ignored. See the [API reference](../reference/api.md).
- **Compiled runtime manifests and `/api/plugin-flows` no longer expose `pluginType`.** Package manifests, plugin summaries, and flow entries use `kind` (`core` or `plugin`); runtime manifests carry no package category. Update clients that read the removed field. See the [API reference](../reference/api.md).

### Fixed

- **Stop works before a turn starts and between the setup turn and the opening.** A stop pressed while the action waited for the session or for the previous turn's memory update used to answer "no turn in progress"; one pressed after setup was saved answered OK and the opening still ran. A stop now applies to the player's whole action: an action that has not started does not start and saves nothing, and an opening that has not started does not start. The stopped opening shows as stopped and can be retried, and it then runs as the opening. Once an action has nothing left to stop, `POST /api/sessions/:id/abort` returns `409`.
- **A saved turn no longer ends in an error when its debug record fails.** If writing the `turn.completed` trace (or announcing background jobs) failed after the turn was saved, the stream ended with `error.occurred` and the opening was skipped. These steps are now only logged: the stream ends with `execution.completed`, and the opening runs.
- **A resent message whose turn is already saved is refused at once.** It used to run the whole turn, model calls included, before the commit refused it. It now ends with `error.occurred` and the code `input_already_committed`, before any model call.
- **A stale retry says what happened.** Retrying a task whose turn has moved on, or recovering a turn that can no longer be recovered, showed "Internal server error" in production. The stream now gives the reason with a code (`retry_source_stale`, `retry_target_not_failed`, `recovery_unavailable`, …), and the app asks the player to refresh the task statuses. Codes: `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **The world art scripts no longer lose a paid picture when `cwebp` is missing.** `scripts/generate-portraits.mjs` and `scripts/generate-scenes.mjs` asked the image provider for a picture and only then found that the WebP encoder was not installed, so the picture was paid for and discarded. They now check for `cwebp` before the first request and stop with the install hint.
- **Deferred tool discovery describes both builtin and plugin tools.** The `search-tools` description identifies the calling plugin without attributing every tool to it.
- **Plugin diagnostics and debug commands use the shared locale resolver.** Compatible Simplified Chinese locales such as `zh-SG` now receive Chinese messages.
- **The memory backend reports the session's embedding lock consistently.** Locking and rebuilding an index update its model ID and lock time; clearing the index removes both, as on SQLite and PostgreSQL.
- **Startup failures show recovery controls even when the splash loads slowly.** The desktop shell waits for the splash to load before delivering the error.
- **Connection tests and model lists use newly saved keys immediately.** Replacing or deleting a desktop key updates the same live key map used by turns, while preserving external environment keys and their precedence (F-033).
- **Stage plugin messages wait for the player to send.** `sendMessage` fills the stage input so the player can review and edit it (F-013).
- **Stage plugin panels share loaded definitions and offer retry on failure.** A failed request shows a localized message without blocking play (F-066).
- **Bundled worlds refresh even before a user worlds directory exists.** Missing directories count as empty inventories.
- **Failed dimension reads preserve the last complete world.** Startup and hot reload reject incomplete dimension sources without sending a dimension-change notice.
- **Dimension and gallery translations follow the declared locale names.** Bare-language overlay files reported as ignored are no longer compiled into catalog or session data.
- **A background job whose runtime suspends now fails instead of showing as succeeded.** When a background manual run, or an event runtime it triggered, suspended, the job was marked `succeeded` (or failed with a misleading reason) while the runtime waited for the player. The job is now recorded as `failed` and commits nothing.
- **A background job no longer commits the results of a plugin version that was reloaded while it ran.** In development a plugin reload could swap the plugin between the start of a job and its commit; the commit now checks the version again and the job ends as `stale`.
- **Repaired tool arguments remain valid in subsequent model requests.** History reflects JSON repairs and `PreToolUse` rewrites, including native provider continuations. Invalid arguments retain their original text and error result. Malformed-history rejections no longer trigger an unchanged retry (F-026).
- **Accepted tool results keep one terminal outcome.** Recording and completion traces follow result acceptance; telemetry failures only warn and preserve proposals. Later cancellation still aborts the enclosing runtime (F-146). Sentinel checks include the tool name (F-147), and declared completion tools govern the tool-use gate (F-150).
- **World translation and generation keep the last good package on failed saves.** File write-back uses staging and the ZIP-install package preflight, restores replaced files on record-save failure, and retains save-time conflict checks.
- **One broken authoring contract no longer disables world creation.** Unreadable examples and missing, invalid, or conflicting schemas skip only the affected contract, with diagnostics for authors and generation warnings.
- **World generation, revision, and translation share their documented SSE exclusion.** A second operation is rejected before model work begins, and admission is released after cleanup.
- **Missing runtime manifests have consistent diagnostics.** Put shared code in `lib/`; every directory under `runtimes/` needs `RUNTIME.md`. Validation and installation report the offending path, while the loader warns and keeps the package’s other runtimes available. See the [plugin reference](../en/reference/plugins.md#package-and-runtime-files).
- **A community plugin approved after the game starts gets the world's data.** Session creation skips the world data that only a plugin waiting for approval receives. Enabling a plugin in the game now imports that data once for the plugins that just joined, and leaves the other plugins' data and anything the game changed alone. A community plugin's world projection runs then, with its approval. Reference: `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **The "memory could not be condensed" notice now appears when compaction keeps failing.** The count of failed attempts in a row was kept per request for requests that send their own model settings, so it restarted at 0 every turn and the notice never appeared. The count now lasts for the server process and is cleared when a session ends or is deleted. The notice no longer names a plugin; it asks the player to disable the plugin that condenses old turns.
- **Submitted forms keep their saved values.** Empty or missing answers no longer read a live form draft. Development sessions without saved answers must be recreated and submitted again to retain them.
- **Plugin flow document links use discovery metadata.** Missing paths stay empty instead of guessing a runtime document location.
- **World validation and import catch invalid dimension condition values.** Plugins declare candidate fields through `authoring.references.values`; story events now check equality and membership candidates against dimension schemas before any import writes.
- **In-game plugin toggle failures explain the problem in the UI language.** Known error codes have Chinese, English and Russian messages; unknown failures use a localized generic message, with the original server response retained in the details. Hyphenated plugin-resolution codes are preserved by the client.
- **AI world creation and revision honor the player's model bindings.** Authoring uses the `story` role by default; API callers can select another role or preset. Every generated part, retry, and repair keeps the selected role and request-scoped provider keys.
- **Concealed runtime tool calls stay out of turn digests.** Their status remains visible, but their arguments no longer reach digest consumers (F-044).
- **Models no longer receive bookkeeping from character schemas, dimension queries or activation payloads.** Full records remain available to code, and archival search omits UUID source keys (F-045, F-149, F-157).
- **Model settings keep partial input until it is committed.** Number fields and sliders use drafts, and consecutive saves no longer discard later edits (F-037).
- **Model discovery follows the selected connection.** Changing the target clears old results and list selections while preserving manually entered IDs (F-094).
- **Destructive settings actions ask for confirmation.** Provider and model deletion explain the affected keys and role bindings; plugin removal and theme deletion also require confirmation (F-191).
- **Read-only settings retain recovery tools.** Export, backup viewing and downloads remain available while setting writes stay disabled (F-199).
- **World revisions can be cancelled while running.** The request stays available for editing and retry, and late events from the cancelled request are ignored.
- **Unsaved world dimension drafts are protected on cancel and reload.** Discarding edits asks for confirmation, including incomplete form input and unapplied JSON. Successful saves clear the saved drafts' warning; failed saves keep it.
- **Panel providers use the same naming rule in every group.** Provider buttons show the plugin's localized name or ID, with no special treatment for `chat-mode`. Browser world saves also stop interpreting the removed `characterBlueprintSources` manifest field inside opaque metadata.
- **PostgreSQL servers that start together no longer fail on table creation.** Startup table creation now runs under one advisory lock, so two servers booting against one database create the tables in turn.
- **A game goes on when the plugin that keeps its dimensions is gone.** When the plugin a session's dimensions belong to was uninstalled, failed to load or lost a dependency, every turn stopped, the session view failed, and the player could not turn any plugin on or off. Now the story goes on with the dimensions as they were saved, nothing changes them, and the game screen says they are paused until that plugin is back. Other plugins can be turned on and off meanwhile. Rules: `docs/reference/world-model.md`.
- **Several plugins that keep world dimensions are explained, not a server error.** Starting a game with more than one of them failed with "Conflicting dimension providers". Preflight and session creation now answer `409 dimension_provider_conflict` with the plugins in `details.candidates`, and the prep screen asks the player to keep one of them. Turning on a second one during a game is refused with the same code, and a turn that meets several names them and says what to turn off. Codes: `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **`llm.toml.example` no longer suggests a field the loader rejects.** The commented `thinking = { type = "enabled" }` line is now `reasoningEffort = "disabled"`; `thinking` fails the load of the file.
- **Schema warnings no longer recommend a plugin tool the caller cannot use.** Undeclared character fields remain a soft warning with the available attribute IDs.
- **Unprefixed local runtime references receive the correct guidance.** Validation suggests the complete package/runtime ID rather than a cross-package contract.
- **World setup reuses authored schemas consistently and silently.** Guard output now matches the published contract, accepts small authored schemas, and only adopts missing dimensions when setup reruns.
- **Random world time follows the host random stream.** Fixed seeds reproduce time steps across different session and turn IDs; repeated calls in one turn reuse the settled result.
- **Session labels and errors follow the interface language.** Canvas, storage, plugin failures, error badges and media jobs use translated text; punctuation and numbered labels are part of the translations.
- **Image generation shares the global confirmation queue.** Its authorization prompt no longer opens a separate dialog above another confirmation.
- **Forms without an interaction submission target show an error.** Their values remain editable instead of being sent as JSON chat text or marked submitted.
- **Forks preserve the parent conversation’s message order.** Messages written in the same millisecond keep their write order across repeated forks and browser checkpoint pagination, instead of being re-sorted by random IDs.
- **Session preparation shows the world edition you will play.** The world name and description follow the session content language throughout the preparation page (F-057).
- **Failed save-list reads can be retried.** The preparation page shows a loading state and a retryable error instead of making unavailable saves look absent (F-072).
- **World data checks follow the latest plugin selection.** Late preflight successes and failures no longer replace newer diagnostics (F-078).
- **Long sessions keep bounded state-patch caches and asset progress.** Only the latest field values are retained, and state panels still refresh for every new change (F-058).
- **Loading older messages skips client-clock echoes when choosing the cursor.** Recovered server messages replace local timestamps (F-063).
- **Rejected input stays separate from later actions.** Local echoes receive a turn only from their own request (F-064).
- **Switching sessions no longer waits for another session’s action stream.** Subscription pauses belong to the session running the action (F-059).
- **Returning to a session reloads its UI slots without stale previews.** Departure and host cleanup release the previous session’s slot cache (F-061).
- **Recovery polling slows down during outages and pauses in hidden tabs.** Failure retries back off to 30 seconds, reset after success, and avoid a second HTTP retry loop (F-065).
- **Native EventSource reconnects resume missed events.** The subscription stream accepts `Last-Event-ID` as well as the existing query cursor, with the query parameter taking precedence. Stale header cursors receive the same reset response.
- **Keyboard and screen reader controls work throughout the session view.** Resize handles show focus, notifications pause while focused, and portrait replacement, copy, and retry controls are available without hover. Session loading has a spoken status and the overflow controls use native button navigation.
- **Queued confirmations start each request with focus on Cancel.** Stage narration is readable separately from its advance button, with paragraph-level live announcements.
- **Leaving the world editor right after choosing to keep editing asks again.** A second attempt to leave, made before the editor had redrawn, was blocked without showing the discard dialog.
- **Desktop “Copy my data” includes stored media.** The copy stages `media/` alongside `worlds/` before placing the database. Reads, repairs, new writes and deletions use the current media root after relocation. Backup instructions now include `media/`.
- **Themes cannot probe saved API keys through CSS selectors or load resources from arbitrary hosts (F-009).** Credential inputs no longer render saved values or reflect new drafts into HTML attributes. Page CSP restricts CSS images, fonts and styles in web and desktop, including development mode. See `docs/internals/security.md`.
- **A resume no longer overwrites a write made after the suspension.** When a background job, a manual run or any other commit changed a record the suspension would write, the resume answers `409 suspension_outdated` with `details.reason: "data-changed"`, voids the suspension and writes nothing; the record keeps the newer value. The check runs before the runtime and again inside the commit transaction. A write to another key does not block the resume. Rules: `docs/reference/api.md` (Out-of-date suspended items).
- **A suspended setup runtime no longer uses up its retries.** Its attempt is recorded as suspended instead of successful, so a setup runtime with `maxTriggerCount: 1` no longer becomes `blocked` as soon as it suspends. When the suspension is abandoned, expires or is voided, the attempt ends `failed` and the runtime goes back to `pending` (or `blocked` when the retries are used up), so it can be retried or skipped.
- **Session prep separates community plugins that need approval from enabled plugins (F-021).** Pending selections stay checked for consent after creation, but no longer inflate enabled counts, runtime previews or world-data preflight. A notice explains that their world data is imported once after approval and enablement; the game can still start. See [World data](../en/reference/world-data.md#receivers-that-join-after-the-game-starts).
- **Session world labels follow the edition being played.** The header, chapter banner, opening summary, and window title use the session's content language even after switching the interface language (F-057).
- **Plugin random ranges behave the same in tests and games.** `makeRandom().int(min, max)` and seeded or unseeded `ctx.random.int` share argument checks and `RangeError` messages. Equal bounds, fractional bounds, and ranges whose width is at least `2 ** 48` are rejected everywhere (F-128).
- **Subscriptions no longer read the session for every frame (F-201).** Producer incarnation tags and ordered deletion controls isolate replay and live events across same-ID recreation. Transport resets trigger a recheck and reconnect; stale frames are dropped while a still-current subscription stays open. Unauthorized connections cannot evict replay state, and hidden writes and empty batches avoid event-related session reads.
- **Failed turns explain how to fix model errors.** Runtime failure events retain the provider failure category, and the turn UI reuses the connection test remedies while keeping the provider message in details. Concealed runtimes keep their fixed error text.
- **Account failures skip backups with the same credentials.** After a refused key (401) or a balance or billing failure, a call skips targets with the same provider, base URL, and key, while still trying configured independent targets. A 403 or a rate limit still tries a same-account backup, because it can apply to one model only.
- **Plugin author tests use current contracts.** Tool integration tests compile a valid manual function manifest; runtime case files reject misspelled fields and malformed expectations before execution, with file and field paths in errors (F-128, F-130).
- **New plugins start with matching versions.** Every scaffold mode sets both package and manifest versions to `0.1.0`, includes a runtime-case editor schema, and is covered by static validation (F-131).
- **Plugin text wires receive clean prompts and session-language instructions.** Internal cache markers are removed at the wire boundary, and structured-output instructions and the Anthropic history opener follow the session instruction language (F-118, F-119).
- **Explicit model targets are checked before provider requests.** Speech, transcription and music reject incompatible slots or presets with `CONFIG_ERROR` (F-168).
- **Media lists and job lists use neutral defaults.** Gallery and preview downloads use the media format instead of a forced PNG name, and audio/video controls remain directly usable.
- **GraphCanvas supports unweighted edges.** Strength, polarity colors, and descriptive mappings are optional; unweighted edges use neutral styling and the graph empty state can be customized.
- **Memory search keeps distinct hits and returns the relevant passage.** Vector recall and keyword top-ups deduplicate by message ID; vector recall and archival use the same query-centered excerpts as keyword search. The tool preserves these excerpts and describes queries without assuming a keyword-only backend (F-154, F-155).
- **Plugin labels distinguish default selection from source.** Default plugins are labeled “Enabled by default” and remain switchable; bundled plugins are labeled “Builtin”. Session prep groups plugins by their first declared tag, including custom tags.
- **Stage decisions show only the recap supplied by the slot provider.** Missing or empty recaps no longer copy story text. Narration and free input remain available without suggestions; turn-scoped choices wait for a matching story turn, while choices that omit the optional turnId remain unscoped.
- **Function text calls use their declared model role by default.** Calls without `presetId` now use `function.model`; an explicit handler choice still wins. Session model overrides remain agent-only, and other generation capabilities keep their own roles.
- **Function model roles are visible before and during play.** The preparation screen and session plugin list show the declared role, resolved model, fallback label, and unavailable state as read-only text. See [Model roles](../reference/slots.md).
- **A browser-private session no longer gets stuck after an interrupted turn and a world edit.** When the page closed right after a turn's result was saved, and the world was edited before the next turn, every later action failed with "already committed with different data". The result and the end of the pending turn are now saved in one write, and a repeated result is recognised by what the server sent, not by the browser's copy of the world. Flow: `docs/internals/storage.md`.
- **An agent guard reads its runtime's input bindings.** `ctx.inputs` was missing in a guard, so a guard could not see the upstream values its runtime declares in `io.inputs`.
- **`tabletop-rules` follows the instruction-language rule.** Its check instructions now follow `COVEL_INSTRUCTION_LOCALE` like every other bundled plugin, and attribute names resolve by language code (`ru` for a `ru-RU` session) through the SDK's `resolveI18nText`.
- **Entry badges use their provider's localized labels.** Codex, quest and world-rule panels declare their category labels in their specs; cards use provider-owned category and rarity options with neutral defaults for missing metadata.
- **Historical plugin messages show their provider's title.** Checks and quest changes no longer appear under “Earlier suggestions”. Candidate lists use neutral default labels, and unused branch-reply strings have been removed.
- **Collection rollback respects the world deletion lifecycle.** Worlds that acquired sessions during installation are retained, and failures report which packages were removed and which remain.
- **Collection archives share one 200 MiB extraction budget.** GitHub preview, batch installation, and offline ZIP import reject oversized sets before writing packages; repeated references to one repository commit count once.
- Plugin inputs now follow their declarations: dice receipts require `checks`, plugin-data reads use direct values, and projected narrative bindings contain strings. Obsolete resizer/preloader parameters are removed, and missing plugin labels no longer display a runtime ID. Revisionless settings backends and normal missing-data degradation remain supported.
- **Multi-pod event fan-out no longer loses events silently or grows without bound.** When a PostgreSQL NOTIFY stalls, each session queues at most 256 frames and drops the rest with a warning; the other pods see the gap and tell their clients to reload. A receiving pod keeps one ordering state per sender and session, reclaims it when the sender starts a new stream, and evicts the least recently used one, delivering any parked events and resetting clients first. Rules: `docs/reference/protocol.md`.
- Function runtime text, object, and evaluation calls use the remaining execution time instead of an extra 120-second gateway ceiling. HTTP retries, fallback targets, and a stalled resend share one transport allowance.
- The process-wide text concurrency limit now covers gateway calls from functions, background jobs, and direct callers as well as agents. Streams hold their slot until they finish or stop; queued agent calls retain their execution-time credit.
- **GitHub previews show package descriptions in the UI language.** Plugin locale files and world translation overlays remain available as `I18nText` through installation and update previews.
- **Plugin code notices include all declared imports and TypeScript module files.** Preview and ZIP metadata use the loader’s import enumeration, including world projections, with `.mts` and `.cts` file detection.
- **Every plugin install path requires self-contained runtime files.** GitHub, ZIP, offline collections, and updates reject nonempty runtime dependency declarations with instructions for the author; development dependencies remain allowed.
- **Manifest errors name the file and the field you wrote.** A cross-field error in an inline `runtime` now names `PLUGIN.md`, and errors use the authored paths (`schedule.detached`, `io.visibility`, `agent.completion.require`) instead of internal names (`turnCompletion`, `outputKind`).
- **The `schedule.after` contract description no longer says it must be a dependency.** An `after` contract only orders and does not have to be listed in `requires` or `optional`.
- **`validatePluginManifest` checks a `PLUGIN.md`.** It validated the frontmatter against the compiled runtime schema and rejected every real package manifest; it now uses the package manifest schema.
- **A runtime that can emit events stays in the foreground.** A `schedule.detached` runtime that lists `emit-event` in its tools, or declares `agent.advertiseEvents: true`, is no longer moved to the background; the check read a package field that was always empty on a runtime.
- **A generation that ended for a reason the provider did not explain is no longer taken as complete.** `finishReason: other` (for example Anthropic's `pause_turn` or DeepSeek's `insufficient_system_resource`) used to be read as a normal `stop`. A story keeps the text already written and the app says it is not finished, so the player can retry the turn; other runtime answers, world generation and the history summary fail instead of saving half a result. The vocabulary also maps more documented provider words (Mistral `model_length`, Gemini `SPII` / `IMAGE_SAFETY` / `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL`, Cohere `ERROR_TOXIC` / `ERROR_LIMIT`). Rules: `docs/reference/slots.md`.
- **Failed model calls count the tokens the provider reported.** A refusal, an error finish reason, an error body or a stream that reaches EOF without its terminal event now carries the reported usage in `error.details.usage`; it reaches `llm.responded` / `gateway.failed`, and the debug page's cost view counts `gateway.failed`. Model calls made by kernel extension providers, such as history compaction, are traced as `gateway.*` events under the provider, so their cost is no longer invisible.
- Character Traits reads the session character schema through the generic `session.characterSchema` panel source and updates after schema commits.
- Plugin state tables stay under `gameState.tables` and cannot overwrite World Model fields. Malformed dimensions show a local notice while the opening scene remains usable.
- Resuming a community plugin requests missing server-code and runtime approvals before execution, rechecks grants under the session lock, and retries after the player approves. Denial or switching sessions retains the pending suspension (F-172).
- Invalid resume schemas now refuse execution with `resume_schema_invalid` instead of bypassing validation. The suspension stays pending and can be abandoned (F-174).
- **Image navigation opens the panel that declares its destination.** Right-panel specs can declare `navTarget: "images"`; an image icon alone no longer captures navigation. Once panels finish loading, missing destinations clear the request and show a notice. Lazy loading and explicit loading retries retain the request.
- **The SDK README and help texts match the package.** The README states the real `zod` peer range and every subpath export, the `tool()` example imports from `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils`, the `covel-test-runtime` help uses a scaffolded plugin, and the `plugin-with-tools` template no longer ships an `.npmrc` that pointed at a file it must not have. `docs/plugins/essentials.md` lists `covel.registerWires` → `contributes.wires`.
- **A world with dimensions can be played without a dimension plugin.** When no active plugin provides `world.dimensions@1`, creating a session failed with a server error ("Dimension provider unavailable"), and so did the world-data preflight and sync. The dimensions are now left out: the session starts without them, the response lists them in `ignoredDimensions`, the preflight and the session-prep screen warn (`dimension-provider-missing`), and a sync keeps the dimension records the session already has.
- **Refreshing during a turn shows the plugin panel and commands at once.** `GET /api/sessions/:id/plugins` reads the saved session record without waiting for the running turn or for background jobs.
- **A background job no model can run no longer slows every action.** When a `schedule.detached: { settle: true }` job has no usable model credentials, the next action stops waiting up to two minutes for it; the trace records `execution.settle-skipped` with the jobs it skipped.
- **Session locks are safer.** Background work started while a session lock is held no longer counts as its owner, and taking several lock keys at once sorts them inside the lock, so two overlapping requests cannot deadlock.
- **Settings controls expose their names, state and results to assistive technology.** Operation notices announce success or failure, configuration sources use segmented buttons, provider and proxy inputs have labels, capability and dark-mode toggles expose their state, and the settings search retains a visible focus ring.
- Localize settings text, units, plural counts, numbers, and dates; use theme warning colours and report import and desktop operation results consistently. Hide custom-only settings from the generic form.
- Resolve theme fallbacks without rewriting saved preferences, show advisory contrast checks on theme import/save, translate builtin theme labels in every UI catalog, and constrain world accent lightness.
- Load worlds with invalid accent colours using the default accent and a server warning; keep format and lightness errors in `pnpm validate:world` for authors to fix.
- Accept nested CSS selector lists in theme imports, reject builtin theme IDs with localized errors, and confirm before save-as replaces an existing custom theme. Preserve themes changed during confirmation.
- **Unsaved prose remains readable after failed stream recovery.** Once recovery confirms a failed or interrupted turn, the web client labels the local text as unsaved and offers the available retry beside it, without mixing it into saved history or stage content.
- **A world without a descriptor is read by the plugins its session runs.** Before, any installed plugin, even one that failed to load or was never enabled, could make a conventional file such as `data/quests.yaml` stop being imported for every world. Now only the session's active plugins count. When two of them name the same file, the session still starts without it, the server logs a `[sessions]` warning and the world data preflight returns a `convention-collision` warning.
- **`pnpm validate:world` checks a world against the plugins a new session starts with.** A source whose receiving plugin is not enabled by default gets an `unrequested-receiver` warning, and a file that several scanned plugins name gets `convention-collision` (an error when a default session enables two of them). Records are still checked against every scanned plugin.
- **Edits survive a removed world package.** Startup cleanup keeps edited world records even when the original package disappears and no session uses them.
- **Edits to a bundled, ZIP-installed, hand-placed or generated world survive a restart and a file change.** Only GitHub installs were marked `packageModified` when edited, so the startup seed and the file watcher silently replaced every other edited world with its files. Every world that came from a package is now marked, and the seed, file reloads, translations and updates keep the edited record.
- **New players no longer inherit a lore character's name and biography from generated schema defaults.** World initialization clears generated biography defaults, and player creation uses the submitted name for declared text name attributes. Both schema-generation prompt languages explain that defaults belong to a new, unnamed character. World Dimensions is now 0.0.42 and Character Creator is 0.0.41; see `docs/reference/tools.md`.
- The session header's "More" menu opens over the notice bars below the header. Before, a menu item that lay over a notice bar (for example "Your selected community plugins need authorization…" or "World dimensions are paused…") could not be clicked.
- **Long sessions no longer get slower on every turn because of how history is read.** SQLite and PostgreSQL now have indexes for the turn-message reads made on every turn: the prompt history, recent messages and the player-message count. They also have indexes for snapshots, and PostgreSQL gains the turn-result index SQLite already had. Pruning old auto snapshots is a single statement that no longer scans every session's snapshots. Existing databases get the new indexes at their next start.
- **A function runtime that a `PostRuntime` hook turns from failed into success passes `output.schema`.** The recovered `output` is checked like a normal success, and the writes the handler buffered before it failed are not committed.
- **Interrupted installs and removals recover at startup.** Unfinished staging files are cleaned up, pending world deletions can be retried, and plugin uninstall moves the whole package out of discovery before deleting files (F-177, F-182).
- **World packages cannot supply their own installation receipt.** ZIP, GitHub, and collection imports reject `.covel-install.json` and case variants before installation, preventing forged source and update metadata (F-178).
- **Forking past a deleted picture works.** A fork whose history names media that is no longer stored (for example, removed with "Delete" in Settings → Stored media) used to fail with `409 media_reference_missing`. It now goes ahead: the child keeps its messages and data, shows the "media unavailable" placeholder there, and the `POST /api/sessions/:id/fork` response reports `missingMediaCount`, the number of distinct missing files. Media that exists but that the parent may not read is still refused with `403 media_reference_forbidden`.
- **World-data sync no longer takes away pictures a session has already shown.** When a sync drops a media index row, the session keeps its reference if the file still appears in its other index rows (identical files share one ID), messages, snapshots or other history. Pictures in earlier turns stay readable, and forks of snapshots from before the sync keep working.
- **Media generation no longer slows down as other sessions add media.** The cache lookup behind `ctx.images.generate()`, `ctx.speech.generate()` and `ctx.music.generate()` now reads only the calling session's own assets (SQLite and PostgreSQL query by owner, using the owner index) instead of the whole media inventory.
- **`MEDIA_BACKEND=pg` without `DATABASE_URL` stops the server.** It used to turn media off without a word, so every media route answered 503. Set `MEDIA_BACKEND=none` to run without media on purpose.
- **Temporary media references that a crashed world-data import left behind now expire.** `POST /api/media/cleanup` releases every temporary `media-pin:<uuid>` reference created 24 hours ago or earlier: a dry run lists them in the new `expiredRefs` field of the result, and a real run removes them, so their bytes can be reclaimed like any other unclaimed media. A younger reference keeps protecting its file, the limit is fixed, and nothing expires outside that opt-in endpoint. No stored schema changed. `MediaLifecyclePolicy` gains `expireRefs` and `MediaCleanupResult` gains `expiredRefs`. Rule: `docs/reference/media-store.md`.
- **Fresh media can no longer be deleted by a cleanup that runs while it is being saved.** Uploads, `ctx.media.put()` and `ctx.media.ingestUrl()` now store their bytes together with a temporary `media-pin:<uuid>` reference and drop it once the session owns them. Before, "Delete all unused" or `POST /api/media/cleanup` could remove an image between the two steps and leave a placeholder. Rule: `docs/reference/media-store.md`.
- **A failed multi-image `ctx.images.generate()` call leaves no images behind.** Every image is stored first and the session claims them together in one transaction (`MediaStore.claimAssets()`, also used by uploads and world-data imports). When one image fails to download or save, or recording a claim fails part-way, none is claimed and the bytes are left to media cleanup, instead of a partial batch that stayed in the session.
- **A failed session delete keeps the session's media readable.** The session is deleted before its media claims are released, so a delete that fails no longer leaves a session that shows placeholders for its own pictures. When finishing a new session fails and deleting it fails too, the import's temporary references are still released.
- **Semantic recall no longer loses a message saved late.** The recall index moved along message timestamps, so a background execution's message, saved after a later turn had been indexed, was never embedded. It now moves along the order messages were saved; a plugin's `readTurnMessages` had the same gap and is fixed with it (F-025).
- **Archival memory no longer rereads the whole corpus every turn.** After each turn the archival index read every character, lorebook entry and searchable plugin data record and rewrote a hash of all of them; a semantic archival search read them all again. It now reads only the records changed since its last position (deletions are kept as tombstones), skips embedding when the stored vector already has the same text, and a search reads only the records its hits name (F-039).
- **Narrative Only and the minimal-set reset no longer retain World Dimensions by default.** The pack excludes `world-init`, and the reset keeps only narrative plugins and the dependencies declared by them and the world. All bundled worlds can start with only the narrator active; dimensions are skipped with a notice when their provider is inactive.
- **On PostgreSQL, a busy lock pool no longer shows an idle session as running.** When the server cannot get a lock connection within 200 ms, `GET /api/sessions/:id/execution` (and the `execution` field of `/view`) now answers `unknown` instead of `running`; a lock held by another process still answers `running`. The client shows that it is checking, checks again less often each time, and after five `unknown` answers stops and asks the player to choose "Check status". An `unknown` status never offers a retry, moves unsaved text away or finishes a turn's steps. Rules: `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **Concurrent session metadata updates of different keys no longer lose one another on PostgreSQL.** `updateSession` merges `metadata` inside one UPDATE there, and a key set to `undefined` is removed on every backend. Two updates of the same key still have one winner. Rules: `docs/internals/storage.md`.
- **Plugin data writes use the same rules for every plugin.** The management API now allows `PUT` and `DELETE` for active `kind: core` plugins, just as for other plugins. Reserved namespaces and inactive plugins remain protected. See `docs/reference/api.md`.
- **`GET /api/framework/capabilities` lists the builtin tools the server runs.** It used to return a hand-written list that had drifted from the registry.
- **`pnpm validate:world` also warns about an ID that is a UUID.** A field named `id`, or ending in `Id` / `Ids`, that holds a UUID is left out of what a model reads, like a write time. The `model-hidden-field` warning now reports it too and suggests a readable ID.
- **A development hot reload of a community plugin now picks up changes in the modules its entry, handlers and guards import.** Editing a file such as `lib/helper.js` used to reload the plugin with the old helper until the server restarted. A reload now gives the plugin a fresh module graph: relative ES module imports that resolve inside the plugin directory are evaluated again, while files outside it, `node_modules` packages, `.cjs` and `.node` files stay shared. Production, bundled plugins and the first activation resolve modules as before. Each reload leaves one more copy of the plugin's modules in memory until the server restarts. Rules: `docs/reference/plugin-extensions.md`. Under `pnpm dev` a change to a code file the server has already loaded still restarts the whole backend, because `tsx watch` follows every loaded module; the reload described here is what runs when the server is started without that watcher, and for manifests, UI files and translations.
- The with-tools plugin scaffold uses the public SDK to build proposal envelopes. Static checks now reject direct random sources in plugins and templates, and keep both known-limit ledgers in sync with source markers.
- **A malformed write from an optional plugin no longer fails the whole turn.** A proposal such as a `narrative.append` with no `content`, or a `plugin.data` value with a BigInt or a circular reference, used to make the store write throw on SQLite and PostgreSQL. That rolled back the turn and its story, so every turn of the session failed. The memory backend stored an empty message instead. Every proposal is now checked against the schema of its type before it commits. A malformed one is rejected with the code `proposal-invalid`: the plugin's writes for that turn are dropped and the story is kept. Values are committed as their JSON copy, so all backends store the same thing.
- **A write that refers to a picture the session does not hold is refused at commit.** A `MediaRef` from another session, or one whose file was deleted, was saved into messages and plugin data, and failed only later, when the session was forked (`403` / `409`). Every `MediaRef` in a proposal is now checked against the session when the proposal commits; one that fails rejects the proposal (`media-not-found`, `media-ownership-invalid`, `media-mime-mismatch`). A ref that passes is stored with the stored MIME type and size and without `url`.
- **The plugin list keeps each plugin's tags in their order.** `GET /api/plugins` sorted them, so a derived tag came before the ones the author wrote. The prep screen now groups plugins on purpose by whether they call a model ("Calls a model", "No model calls"), and a plugin that has no such tag under its first tag.
- **A plugin pack chosen after "Back to the minimal set" gets its plugins back.** The minimal set excluded the pack's plugins for good, so choosing a pack afterwards left them off.
- **World lists name each world in the edition the player gets.** The showcase, the other list layouts and the delete and language dialogs showed a world's own language (an English interface showed the Chinese names of worlds that have an English edition).
- **The world detail page shows dimension values in the play language.** Values kept as locale maps printed every language, booleans and empty values printed `false` and `null`, and dimension names ignored the play language. Its update note no longer tells the player to open the page they are on.
- **Skipped tasks say why in words.** The turn's task list printed codes such as `upstream-failed`; the needs gate now reports that code like the input gate, and the app translates every gate code, also for a turn shown again after a reload.
- **Approval dialogs name the plugin.** Turning on a community plugin asked "Plugin qa-broken requests to run covel:plugin-server-code"; every approval now names the plugin by its display name, and the server-code approval explains what it allows.
- **The World item of the top bar leaves world details and the dimension editor.** It did nothing there; the editor asks first when it has unsaved changes.
- **A world whose package changed on disk shows its update without a reload.** The world list is fetched again when the player comes back to it and when the window regains the focus.
- **An interjection stays where the turn saves it.** It showed below the streaming story text and, after a reload, above it; it now goes above it at once, and the text written after it starts a new paragraph while it streams.
- **Lists in player text use the interface language's separators**, and the session hints no longer show contract IDs such as `world.dimensions@1`. The hint for installing a plugin ZIP says where `PLUGIN.md` goes. A plugin's name on the prep screen is no longer cut short by its badges, dimension tables in the narrow side panel scroll instead of squeezing a column to one character per line, and the Russian language notice reads correctly.
- **"Revise this world" no longer destroys files a person added to an AI-generated world.** `.covel-generated.json` now lists every file the app wrote into the package with a hash of its content. When the package holds a file that is not in the list, or a listed file whose content was edited, `POST /api/ai/revise-world` returns `409 world_package_modified` with the files in `details.added` / `details.changed`, the world page names them, and the package is left as it is. The check runs before the model is called and again just before the package is replaced. Translations made in the app are recorded in the list, so a translated world can still be revised. Rules: `docs/reference/api.md` (revise-world) and `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **Generated worlds can finish setup after character schema generation.** The world initialization output contract no longer requires the completion signal that the kernel separates from business output. The contract still validates both generated and author-provided schemas; see `docs/reference/plugins.md`.
- **A model role that is not set up no longer shows as an error.** The prep screen and the session's plugin list said "Model role plugin is unavailable" for a role the player never defined, although the game runs it on the first text role. They now say "plugin is not set up; uses story · …"; a role that is defined but cannot be used is still reported.
- **Reply Variants is listed as calling a model.** It asks the fast role for alternative phrasings, and now declares that role, so its tag reads "Calls a model".
- SQLite saves can be deleted when the optional vector extension cannot load, even in databases with existing vector indexes. Orphan vectors are reclaimed when the extension next loads successfully (F-017).
- Concurrent startups cannot both take over a stale SQLite database lock. Takeover is serialized, uses a rename and an ownership read-back check, and counts relative and absolute paths as the same lock (F-113).
- **Stopped openings offer Continue, and messages stopped before a turn starts return to the input box.** Newer draft text is preserved in both text and stage views.
- **The world editor confirms before navigating away with unsaved changes.** In-app links and Back now use the same discard prompt as Cancel, including incomplete form input and unapplied JSON.
- **MemoryStore, SQLite and PostgreSQL now agree on four storage rules.**
  - Rows with the same timestamp come back in the same order on every backend: events, state changes, interaction records, snapshots and suspensions break ties by ID, and state schemas are listed by table name, so an update no longer moves a row on PostgreSQL and snapshots and checkpoints list them in a stable order.
  - MemoryStore rejects a second turn result, tool call, runtime output, interaction record, state change, event, trace, player input or session summary under an existing ID, as SQLite and PostgreSQL always did.
  - A batch of lorebook entries is written whole or not at all on SQLite and PostgreSQL.
  - A transaction no longer copies every MemoryStore collection when it calls `queryTurnResults`, `queryTraceEvents` or `queryPluginData`.
- **Schema startup errors identify the database that was refused.** `StoreSchemaVersionError.database` and its message include the absolute SQLite file path, or the PostgreSQL host, port and database name without credentials. SQLite errors explain how to move aside or delete disposable development data. The development-path documentation now distinguishes an existing Covel home from the `apps/server/data/covel.db` fallback.
- **Abandoning or expiring the last suspension of a turn ends that turn.** The turn now counts and the detached background jobs it held back are queued, as when the last suspension resumes; before, they were dropped. The expiry sweep settles each record under its session lock.
- **Progress jobs of a suspended runtime no longer wait forever.** Jobs a function runtime reported before suspending end `succeeded` when the resume commits and `cancelled` when the suspension is abandoned, expires or is voided.
- **An interjection is saved with its turn.** A message sent with "interject" during a turn used to be saved to the chat log at once, but never to the conversation history that later turns read, so the model forgot it after the turn; and a stopped or failed turn still left it in the chat log. It is now saved when its turn is saved, to both, in the same transaction, after the player's message of the turn. A turn that is not saved keeps neither. It does not count as a new player turn.
- **`pnpm validate:plugin` checks a plugin's UI spec files.** A misspelt component name, a spec with neither or both of `view` and `webview`, a `repeat.statePath` that is not an absolute path string or `{ "$item": "field" }`, and an `$item` / `$bindItem` field name with a leading slash now fail validation with the spec file and the JSON path of the node, instead of showing only as a missing panel in the app. The ZIP install runs the same checks, so a package with such a spec is refused. Rules: `docs/reference/plugins.md` and `docs/reference/ui-panels.md`.
- **Opening a session no longer misses a background change committed at the same moment.** The session view now carries the event cursor of the moment it was read. The first live connection replays from it, or reads the session again when the connection opened later than the view, so a panel no longer shows an old value until the next change or reconnect.
- **Worlds start even when their data receivers are not installed.** Session creation, preflight and later imports or sync skip the unavailable contract writes with warnings through the existing world-data notices. Active projections still receive the source; sources with no consumers skip schema checks and media import too. Sync preserves existing managed data, ledger rows and media references for skipped writes, while successfully read empty data still removes old records when receivers return. Author validation keeps its existing error rules. See [World data](../en/reference/world-data.md#contract-resolution-and-preflight).
- **World update warnings no longer direct players to an unavailable sync control.** Chinese, English and Russian warnings explain that updates preserve data already imported into an existing game and that new starting data requires a new game.
- **Opening character defaults are checked in the sentence the player will send.** Character Creator returns a filled preview for model review, rejects adjacent repeated words and explicit undecided placeholders in English and Chinese, and asks for a concrete calling from the world. A revised form replaces its earlier draft before display. Preview and submission share the same template renderer.
- **Dice checks no longer offer health or trust as a check attribute.** Dice Check listed every ranged number of the player character with its modifier, so the narrator could roll against health (80/100 → +8) or trust. It now lists only the `abilities` attributes, the same ones Tabletop Rules uses for point buy and its check panel. A world without ranged abilities gets no list, and the narrator converts the attribute itself as before.
- **Emberback's five attributes are all abilities.** Grit and Insight were `stats`, so the status bar showed them as bars, and Rapport was `social`. All five are check attributes from 0 to 5 and now sit in `abilities`. Sessions started earlier keep their old character schema.
- **A plugin's world data file may end in a suffix such as `.bak` or `.min`.** `authoring.source.path` rejected `characters/characters.bak.json` or `media/scenes.min.json` as a language version of a kernel file. Now only a two-letter language code (`.fr`, `.zh-CN`) or a three-letter one with a region (`.yue-HK`) makes a language version. See `docs/reference/world-data.md`.
- **`pnpm i18n status` and `pnpm i18n lock` go on past a plugin they cannot read.** A plugin whose runtime directory holds a misnamed manifest stopped the whole run; now the command names that plugin and its problem, checks the others and exits with status 1. The translation check in `pnpm check` reports such a bundled plugin the same way.
- **Two Emberback world rules fire in Chinese sessions.** `emberback-future-echoes` and `emberback-signal-evidence` now have Chinese trigger words; before, they only matched English words in the player's message.
- **Emberback no longer contradicts itself about Mile Seventeen.** The repeater sits beneath the arch everywhere, and every line repeats the warning as "do not let the convoy reach Mile Seventeen".
- **World translation includes lowercase trigger words.** `i18n status` and `i18n translate` recognize every `keys` list, and translating other text preserves existing translated lists. World validation warns when a translated rule or lorebook entry still inherits its original trigger words. See [world translation tooling](../reference/i18n.md).
- **Lantern Barrow's and Emberback's rules text gives the attribute modifier the dice actually use.** Both worlds said checks were "d20 + attribute", which reads as +4 for an attribute of 4. Both dice plugins turn an attribute into a modifier over its declared range, so the two worlds' 0–5 attributes give +2 per point (+10 at 5). The Chinese and English world text now say so; the difficulties and the natural 20 / natural 1 rule are unchanged.
- **Provider failures keep their HTTP status when the response is empty or not JSON.** Authentication errors are recorded as `auth`, error messages keep a bounded body preview, and calls that cannot be retried report a failed call instead of exhausted retries. See [model failure reasons](../reference/slots.md).
- **Chinese tabletop turns without a submitted check keep the narrator's no-check marker.** Both narrators can recognize that ordinary action checks still apply.
- **Scene backgrounds follow the named place before another scene's sub-location.** In Harukaze Academy, "Library window seat" now selects the library instead of Class 2-B in both Chinese and English. Exact sub-location matches still work, and the longest name wins within the same rank. See [scene matching](../reference/world-data.md#scene-backgrounds).
- **Model capability controls use the same input, output, and feature names as the server.** Custom model output choices now include `video`, as documented in the [slot reference](../reference/slots.md).
- **World rules fire on the words English players type.** The trigger words of the three worlds' rules and lorebook entries now cover the common English forms of each idea instead of literal phrases that almost never matched (for example "three bells", "appraised", "favoritism", "singing").

### Security

- **Sensitive desktop IPC rejects child frames.** Reading keys or the REST token and changing settings requires a trusted main frame.
- **Listing saved key providers requires the configured desktop token.** `GET /api/config/keys` now uses the same guard as sensitive configuration routes (F-107).
- **Public hosted model catalogs hide gateway addresses.** `/api/presets` and `/api/llm-config` omit `baseUrl` unless the caller has operator access (F-108).
- **Plugin localization tools reject executable frontmatter.** Loading, installation, validation, translation, and authoring checks share the same plain YAML manifest reader. Runtime directory symlinks are excluded consistently from discovery and localization scans.
- **`permissions.http` covers a community package's entry code.** `covel.http` in tools, hooks, RPC, form validators, services, extensions and wires reaches only the origins and methods in the `permissions.http` of the root `PLUGIN.md` and of its runtimes; a wire may also reach the endpoint of the slot it serves. A service or extension handler's `context.utils` is checked against the provider's package, not the caller's. Builtin packages are not checked, as for runtimes. See `docs/internals/security.md`.
- **Media wires serve only sessions that enable their plugin.** Image, speech, transcription and music wires a plugin registers follow the same rule as text wires; generation outside those sessions fails with a configuration error that names the plugin to enable.
- **`framework` is a reserved plugin ID.** A package with `id: framework` no longer loads or installs, and `pluginId: "framework"` in plugin-rpc reaches only framework default actions. A plugin ID no longer falls back to a framework default action of the same name.
- **Covel's own requests no longer follow redirects.** The model database download and the desktop version check followed a redirect to wherever it pointed, past the address checks, so an answer that redirected to an internal address (such as a cloud metadata service) was fetched. Every framework request now fails on a redirect unless its code checks each hop itself, and the version check reads the release answer with the same size ceiling as other provider answers. Rule: `docs/internals/security.md#redirects`.
- **The local server answers only to loopback host names.** On the `self` tier (the default, the desktop app and `pnpm dev`) a request whose `Host` header is not `localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` or a name in the new `COVEL_ALLOWED_HOSTS` variable gets `403 host_not_allowed`, on every method. A web page can no longer point its own domain at your machine (DNS rebinding) to drive the token-less API. `demo` and `commercial` are unchanged. Rule: `docs/internals/security.md`.
- **Forking a session on a hosted tier needs the operator token (F-204).** `POST /api/sessions/:id/fork` creates a session, so it now asks what `POST /api/sessions` asks: on `demo` / `commercial` a session owner can no longer mint new sessions by forking their own. On `self` and the production MemoryStore, an owner still forks freely.
- **A broken `llm.toml` no longer prints environment variable values.** When a line with `${VAR}` had a syntax error (for example `apiKey = ${DEEPSEEK_API_KEY}` without quotes), the error quoted the text with the key already filled in, and it reached `server.log`, the Settings page and the `PUT /api/config/raw/llm.toml` response. The error now gives only the line, the column and the reason.
- **Plugin load errors no longer reveal the server's install path.** `GET /api/plugins` writes the plugin directory as `<plugin>` in the `error` of a package that failed to load. The full message stays in the server log.
- **Provider keys only reach endpoints the player or operator configured.** No plugin manifest or configuration file can point model calls, with the player's keys, at an address of its choice. See `docs/internals/security.md#provider-keys`.

### Documentation

- **Desktop key storage documentation now describes the IPC path.** The UI receives plaintext keys in memory; the provider-list markers belong to the optional REST transport.
- **Documentation diagrams use Mermaid 12.** The documentation site now requires an ES2024 browser (Safari 17.4+) and keeps the existing flat shapes, layout and label widths. This browser requirement applies only to the documentation site.
- **Multiple diagrams on a documentation page render reliably.** Each diagram now has its own SVG ID, so diagrams drawn together or after a theme change cannot overwrite one another.
- **Search in the Chinese documentation finds a word in the middle of a phrase.** The search box split Chinese text only at punctuation, so a query matched the start of a clause or nothing: the Chinese for "preset quests" did not find the page about them. Chinese text is now indexed from every character on, in pieces of up to four characters. The Chinese search index is larger for it (about 2.1 MB compressed, from 0.9 MB); it is loaded when the search box is first opened.
- **Covel has a documentation site.** The "Docs" link of the showcase site opens it at `docs/` instead of the repository's `docs/` directory on GitHub. It has a sidebar for each part (guides, reference, internals), full-text search, a table of contents on each page, rendered diagrams, a dark theme and a language switch. Its pages are the Markdown files of `docs/`, built by the new `apps/docs` package (`pnpm --filter @covel/docs dev`).
- **The documentation is rewritten for a reader who meets the product for the first time, in plain Chinese.** The chapters follow that reader: getting started (install, connect a model, first session, packages, backup and upgrade, run from source), what ships with Covel, creating a world, developing a plugin, verifying and shipping, common tasks, reference, and internals. The first-world and first-plugin guides build a real package step by step, and every command they show was run.
- **The documentation has an English edition.** Every page has an English page at the same path under `docs/en/`, served at `docs/en/`. The showcase site's "Docs" link opens the edition of the visitor's language. The Chinese pages are the source.
- **A chapter on what ships with Covel.** `docs/builtin/` describes the gameplay packs, each bundled plugin (what it does in play, what the player sees, its settings, what a world can preset for it) and the app's own features.
- **The changelog is a page of the site** (`docs/releases.md`, which includes `docs/CHANGELOG.md`).
- **The pages moved, and a link to an old path needs the new one.** `docs/guide/` and `docs/architecture/` are gone and `docs/reference/` holds the rewritten reference: `docs/architecture/<name>.md` is `docs/internals/<name>.md`; the guides are under `docs/start/`, `docs/worlds/`, `docs/plugins/`, `docs/ship/`, `docs/recipes/` and `docs/internals/`; `docs/guide/env-registry.md` and `docs/guide/desktop-config.md` are in `docs/reference/`; `docs/glossary.md` is `docs/reference/glossary.md`; the English pages that were `<name>.en.md` are `docs/en/<same path>.md`. `docs/README.md` lists every page. Heading anchors of the reference and internals pages are unchanged. The links in the released sections of `docs/CHANGELOG.md` were repointed the same way.
- **Reference pages were corrected against the code while they were rewritten.** Among others: the HTTP API page shows `nextCursor` as the opaque string it is and no longer lists error codes that do not exist; the SSE page lists seven action types and the real `SseEnvelope`; the tools page lists the dimension proposals; the technical-debt page matches the `ponytail:` markers in source.
- **Bundled descriptions and create-plugin guidance match the current API.** Dice use `ctx.random`, function documentation has no Chinese prompt variant, tabletop creation reads the World Model, and character handles make no assumption about ID shape. The skill points to public result/store types and uses complete runtime IDs and author SDK imports.
- **Stage choice documentation reflects the current slot contract.** Removed the obsolete icon/color unpacking debt and old guide-data explanations.
- **The hook reference says which events `ctx.signal` does not cancel, and that `PreToolUse` also fires for `ctx.tools.call`.** `docs/internals/flow.md` lists every write outside the commit transaction.
- **The component catalog states its limits.** General tables, statistical charts, custom timers and animation, and unlisted icons use webviews; existing specialized tables, transitions and media remain available.
- **Fields no bundled plugin uses moved to an advanced part.** `covel`, `homepage`, `author.about`, `author.links`, `contributes.wires`, `events[].advertise`, `prompt[].position.depth`, `commands[].context`, `arguments[].variadic` and `choices`, the settings controls `text`, `textarea`, `integer`, `slider` and `slot`, and the agent loop fields `maxSteps`, `firstTokenTimeoutMs`, `idleTimeoutMs`, `loopDetection` and `maxRecursionDepth` are listed in "Advanced fields" of `docs/en/reference/plugins.md` and "Rarely needed fields" of `docs/en/plugins/advanced.md`. They all still work.
- **The product principles are written down.** `docs/internals/design-principles.md` now opens with them: players come first; bundled plugins are demos on equal footing with community plugins, and the framework runs with every plugin removed; a missing plugin or an unused field must not stop the game; one core serves the desktop app, the self-hosted web app and, later, the official web app: the desktop and self-hosted apps are the current focus, the official web app is not supported yet, and the core stays generic so that it can be added without rewriting it; manifests ask only for what cannot be derived or defaulted; and contracts change directly during fast development. `AGENTS.md` summarises them for coding agents.
- **Pages that contradicted the principles or the code are corrected.** "Core plugin" no longer names every bundled plugin: a `kind: core` plugin joins every game by default, and the player can turn it off on the session-prep screen (the tables of `docs/builtin/index.md` say "default-enabled" instead of "always enabled"). The storage and HTTP API pages describe the three deployment forms instead of three tiers and say that the official web app is not supported yet: SQLite is the default, PostgreSQL is for professional developers and special needs, and the two must behave the same. The `commercial` tier is reserved for a possible commercial edition that this repository does not develop. `docs/reference/slots.md` no longer says that a manifest `wires` field is still accepted. The pages on the bundled worlds say which play mode each one demonstrates, and that `emberback` is written in English to show a world in several languages. The glossary has "Bundled plugin", "Deployment form" and "Official web app", and the empty page `docs/en/reference/orld-installation.md` is gone.
- **UI references use the current component names and document all four stage slot values.** Chinese and English pages now name `MediaGallery` and `JobList`, require explicit `alwaysRender`, and describe stage fields, limits, speaker-name strings and provider validation.
- **World data sync is documented as an API for authors and tools.** The Chinese and English references explain explicit sync, conflict handling and its limits for existing sessions. The player guide directs players to start a new game for new starting data.
- The world manifest reference, "Your first world" and the world generator's tool now say what each attribute `category` is for: `abilities` for checks and point buy, `stats` for status-bar bars, `social` for relationships.

### Upgrade notes for v0.0.52

- A community package whose entry code calls `covel.http.fetchWithRetry` must list the origins it calls under `permissions.http` in its root `PLUGIN.md` (or in a runtime); undeclared requests now fail with `http permission denied`.
- **Recreate pending development jobs that froze a turn digest before this fix.** Existing snapshots are not rewritten and may still contain concealed tool arguments.
- A database written by the current build opens as before and is stamped with the version on its first start. If startup reports "Data from another Covel version", back up the data directory and set a new `data_root` (desktop), `SQLITE_PATH` or `DATABASE_URL`.
- If you run the PostgreSQL Docker stack, replace `pnpm docker:build` / `up` / `down` / `down-all` / `logs` with the `docker:pg:` commands. The old names now start a separate SQLite stack on the same default port.
