- **Clear old development browser display caches after upgrading.** The state-patch cache now stores bounded field values and recent event IDs. Save checkpoints are unchanged.
- **Recover media omitted by an earlier copy manually.** If the old data root still exists, copy its `media/` directory into your backup before upgrading or recreating development data; copying the files alone does not convert an absolute-path database. If the old files were deleted and no backup exists, they cannot be recovered.
- **A self-hosted instance opened from other machines must list its names.** If players reach your instance by a domain, a LAN address or through a reverse proxy, set `COVEL_ALLOWED_HOSTS` (for example `covel.example.com,192.168.1.20`) before upgrading. Opening it at `localhost` or `127.0.0.1` needs nothing. Details: `docs/reference/env-registry.md`.
- **An existing `pgdata18` volume keeps the `vector` extension at 0.8.6** until it is updated in the database. After the new image starts, run `docker compose -f docker/docker-compose.yml --env-file .env exec postgres psql -U covel -d covel -c 'ALTER EXTENSION vector UPDATE;'` (use your `POSTGRES_USER` and `POSTGRES_DB` if you changed them).
- **A world generated by an earlier build can no longer be revised in the app.** Its `.covel-generated.json` has no file list, so nothing says which files the generator wrote; after a restart the world loads without `metadata.generated` and "Revise this world" is not offered (`409 world_not_revisable` from the endpoint). Generate the world again to get a revisable one, or keep the package and edit its files. There is no migration.
- **Development worlds without a package digest need one restore to enable update detection.** Back up app edits before restoring; the restore replaces world data and settings.

## [0.0.51] - 2026-10-12

This release makes the download smaller and moves the showcase page out of the app. The app now opens on the world list; the showcase is a separate static website. Bundled portraits and scenes ship as WebP, which takes about 95 MB off the files that go into the installers and the Docker image. Without an `llm.toml`, the built-in model can be pointed at any OpenAI-compatible provider with `DEFAULT_*` variables. Three faults a static analysis found are fixed, and release installers carry a build provenance attestation. There is no breaking change: a database created by v0.0.50 works as it is. Read the [upgrade notes](#upgrade-notes-for-v0051) before installing. macOS Apple Silicon and Windows x64 binaries are unsigned; the macOS build is not notarized.

Verified for this release: a session was started and played by hand in the development build. Not verified: the size saving is computed from the packaged files, not measured on a built installer; the art generation scripts were not run against an image provider after the change to WebP; the attestation step runs for the first time in this release's workflow; nobody played a session in the Windows build (it is built and packaged by the release workflow).

### Added

- **Point the built-in model at any provider with `DEFAULT_*` variables.** Without an `llm.toml`, the server and the runtime test harness used a DeepSeek `story` slot that nothing could change. Setting `DEFAULT_BASE_URL` (an OpenAI-compatible endpoint), `DEFAULT_MODEL` and `DEFAULT_API_KEY` now sends that slot to your own provider; left unset, it stays DeepSeek. The recorded sessions under `tests/llm-replay/` use the same variables: `pnpm e2e:replay --record` records from `DEFAULT_BASE_URL` with `DEFAULT_API_KEY`, and `--upstream` is optional.
- **A separate showcase website.** `apps/site` is a static site with new sections and copy in English and Chinese, a light and a dark theme, and the four bundled worlds. Its download button gives a macOS or Windows visitor the installer from the latest release, with the Windows portable build beside it. Run it with `pnpm --filter @covel/site dev`; `pnpm --filter @covel/site build` writes `apps/site/dist/`. The demo media script moved with it: `pnpm --filter @covel/site build:media`.
- **The showcase website is published on GitHub Pages.** A push to `main` that changes `apps/site` builds it and deploys it to `https://ackness.github.io/covel/` (`.github/workflows/site.yml`); pull requests only build it. The build follows the Pages base path, so a custom domain works without changes.

### Changed

- **The showcase website tells the plugin story.** Its headline reads "Build your own …" and swaps the last words between visual novel, tabletop campaign, epic story and multilingual world. Each swap animates the words letter by letter and carries the subtitle, the colour, the screenshot and a list of the bundled plugins with it, lighting up the ones that mode really runs (taken from the bundled packs). Below, two cards explain the plugin design and world packs. A new section lists the built-in plugins, each with its plugin ID.
- **The app opens on the world list.** `/` in the web app and the desktop shell now goes straight to the world list, and the Covel logo leads there too. The showcase page that used to sit at `/` is gone from the app, along with its video and images, so the desktop package no longer ships them.
- **The installers and the Docker image are about 95 MB smaller.** The portraits and scenes of the bundled worlds ship as WebP (lossy colour at quality 90, lossless alpha) instead of PNG, 77 MB down to 10 MB, and the PNG originals of gallery pictures stay in the repository without being packaged; the gallery already showed their WebP versions. `scripts/generate-portraits.mjs` and `scripts/generate-scenes.mjs` now write WebP and need `cwebp` (libwebp) on PATH; `scripts/encode-world-webp.mjs` converts a picture made elsewhere. Rules: `docs/v2/worlds/art-direction.md`.

### Removed

- **`VITE_ROUTER_DEVTOOLS`.** The TanStack Router devtools showed only on the old showcase page, so the button and its variable are removed.

### Fixed

- **`pnpm e2e:replay` no longer hangs after a session.** When the test server was stopped by a signal, the command waited forever for it to exit.
- **The showcase website shows the Covel icon.** The header, footer and browser tab pointed at an `icon.png` the site never shipped; `apps/site/public/` now has it.
- **The desktop startup screen is readable in light mode when startup fails.** With the system in light mode, the error message and hint were drawn in near-white text on the light background, and the error title and the spinner's centre dot were faint. They now use dark colours in light mode.

### Security

- **A base URL with a long run of slashes no longer holds the server.** The trailing-slash trim of a provider base URL took time quadratic in the length of a run of slashes that did not end the URL, and a base URL can arrive in a request's model settings (16,000 slashes cost about half a second per request). Every such trim in the model gateway, the evaluation adapter and the embedding lock is now linear.
- **A prompt body cannot stall prompt assembly with `{{` and whitespace.** `{{` followed by a few thousand spaces or tabs and no `}}` took the template interpolation tens of seconds, in the kernel (`interpolateTemplate`) and in the author SDK (`interpolate`). Both now scan the template in linear time and fill exactly the same `{{ path }}` variables as before.
- **`pnpm i18n` cannot be made to write into `Object.prototype`.** A manifest key named `__proto__` in the plugin being translated led the label writer (`writePluginTranslations`) out of the locale file's section; a label whose place holds `__proto__`, `constructor` or `prototype` is now skipped.
- **Release installers carry a build provenance attestation.** `gh attestation verify <file> --repo ackness/covel` confirms that a downloaded `.dmg`, `.zip` or `.exe` was built by this repository's release workflow, and from which commit. The installers stay unsigned; `SHA256SUMS.txt` is unchanged. Details: `docs/v2/internals/desktop-packaging.md`.

### Upgrade notes for v0.0.51

- **A session started before this version keeps the pictures it imported.** A new session imports the WebP files. A world of your own that ships PNG portraits or scenes still works: PNG remains an accepted format, and the `emit-presence` / `emit-scenes` scripts read both.

## [0.0.50] - 2026-10-11

This release makes long sessions cheaper and safer to run and gives characters aliases. Prompts keep a stable prefix that a provider's cache can serve, per-turn reads no longer grow with the length of a session, a form answer and its follow-up turn are one request, plugin HTML runs on its own origin, and start-up refuses a mistyped backend or a second process on one SQLite file. A world can declare a dimension value as derived from its clock, and the value is then computed in code instead of by a model. **Development data is not carried over: an SQLite or PostgreSQL database created by an earlier version must be recreated**, because the `characters` table has a new column and the server never alters a table. Read the [breaking changes](#breaking-changes-in-v0050) and the [upgrade notes](#upgrade-notes-for-v0050) before installing. macOS Apple Silicon and Windows x64 binaries are unsigned; the macOS build is not notarized.

Verified for this release: the macOS build was started in a separate profile and a session was played in it with a model behind a local relay; the session screen, character cards, portraits, settings, memory and the world dimensions panel were looked at in a browser. Not verified: nobody played a session in the Windows build (it is built and packaged by the release workflow), the screens were not looked at in a browser other than Chromium, and no request went to a first-party OpenAI, Anthropic or Google endpoint; model tests ran through a local relay. A world author who does not want a later-revealed name shown should not declare it as an alias: the character panel lists aliases.

### Breaking changes in v0.0.50

- **The app's page runs no inline script and frames only its own origin.** The page policy is now `script-src 'self'` (it had `'unsafe-inline'`) with `frame-src 'self'`. A self-hosted deployment whose reverse proxy or static host injects an inline `<script>` into the page (an analytics snippet, a banner) must load it from a file on the app's origin instead, or it will not run. The build has a new file, `/plugin-frame.html`, which a static host must serve as that file and not as the single-page fallback; a host that copies `dist/web` whole needs no change. Plugin authors change nothing: a `webview` document keeps inline scripts, inline event attributes and inline styles.
- **`GET /api/worlds` returns summaries.** Each item has no `lore` and no `dimensions`, and its `metadata` keeps only `source`, `storage`, `packageManaged`, `generated`, `cover`, `accentColor`, `supportedLocales`, `packageInfo`, `defaultViewMode`, `pluginSettings` and the `name` / `description` entries of `localizedText`. The list of the four bundled worlds shrinks from about 267 KB to 3.9 KB. A client that read the lore, the dimensions or another metadata key (`embeddedCharacters`, `characterSchema`, `pluginPolicy`, `worldData`) from the list must call `GET /api/worlds/:id`, which still returns the whole record. In the web client the session store and the world list hold summaries; the session-prep screen, the world page, the world editor and the World tab read the full record when they open it. The first visit of the browser-private profile now reads each catalog world with `GET /api/worlds/:id`. The `WorldSummary` type, `worldSummarySchema` and `summarizeWorld` are in `@covel/shared`. Rules: `docs/v2/reference/api.md`.
- **Diagnostic traces older than 30 days are deleted.** `COVEL_TRACE_RETENTION_DAYS` now defaults to `30` instead of `0`, so after the upgrade the server removes the runtime trace events (model requests and replies, tool calls, usage) of every session that are older than 30 days, once at start and then once a day, and after each commit for the session that committed. Stories, messages and saves are untouched; only what the debug page can show shrinks. To keep everything, set `COVEL_TRACE_RETENTION_DAYS=0` or choose "Keep everything" in Settings (`self` tier, desktop app or self-hosted web). A set variable wins over the Settings choice; `demo` / `commercial` follow the variable alone.
- **An image part of a model message is `{ type: "image", image, mediaType? }`.** `image` is a string (base64, a `data:` URL or an `http(s)` URL), as in the Vercel AI SDK; the earlier `{ type: "image", image: { url, mimeType } }` (plugin SDK `PluginTextMessage`) and `{ type: "image", image: MediaRef }` (`@covel/shared` `LLMImagePart`, `@covel/ai-provider` `ImagePart`) are gone. A plugin that sends an image through `ctx.gateway` must pass the new shape. A target known to take text only now has image parts removed; the `image_ref` JSON text that stood in for an image without a URL no longer exists.
- **`assetGenerateToLLM`, `assetGenerateViewToLLM` and the `ContentPart` types left `@covel/shared`.** Nothing called them. `pictureOf` and `picturesShown` read a picture and its caption from a stored block instead.
- **A picture's `meta.caption` or `meta.prompt` is shown to the story model.** An image plugin that put text there which the story model must not read has to move it to another `meta` field.
- **A history window counts player turns only.** `agent.history.maxTurns` no longer counts a `system` conversation row (a picture note) as a turn of its own.
- **Pictures from before this change have no note.** A development session keeps its pictures on screen, but the story model learns only of pictures generated from now on; nothing is migrated.
- **The OpenAI Responses protocol no longer lets the provider keep the conversation.** Every request of a slot with `protocol = "openai-responses-v1"` now sends `store: false`; before, the field was sent only when the slot set it, and OpenAI keeps responses for a time by default. A slot that relied on stored responses must set `store = true` in its `providerOptions`: one that passes `previous_response_id` itself through `extraBody` (Covel never sends it), or an operator who reads responses in the provider's dashboard. No stored data changes. Rules: `docs/v2/reference/slots.md`.
- **`branch-reply` takes no narrative text from a request.** The manual payload no longer reads `baseText`, `candidates` (on `createCandidates`) or `text` (on `acceptCandidate`): candidates are the turn's stored narration and the rephrasings the plugin generates from it, and `createCandidates` fails for a turn that has no stored candidate set. An adopted reply replaces the turn's narration in the model's history, so a request could rewrite a turn while the items, quests and affinity taken from the real narration stayed.
- **`character-blueprint` has no `import` runtime and `living-world-rules` has no runtime.** Both wrote game content during play (a character card, a world rule) through plugin-rpc with no panel that called them. Cards and rules come from the world package (`character.blueprints@1`, `world.rules@1`). A script that called `character-blueprint/import` or the `living-world-rules` manual runtime must put the record in the world package instead.
- **`scene-stage` keeps dialogue attribution in one row.** `stage.direction` writes `dialogue/current` instead of `dialogue/<turnId>`. A development session shows no speaker names for its last turn until the next `stage.direction` event.
- **A scene matches by each of its names.** `locationRef` in a world's `media/scenes.json` is read as `/`-separated names, not as one string. A location shows a scene when it equals a name, then when it contains one (the longest wins), then when it is a fragment of a name that only one scene has. A location that is a fragment shared by two scenes no longer shows the first of them. Rules: `docs/v2/reference/world-data.md`.
- **A tabletop check converts the attribute over its range.** `tabletop-rules` adds `round((value - min) / (max - min) * 10)` to the d20, as `dice-check` does, instead of the raw attribute value; the receipt has the value as `score`. An attribute of 4 on a 0-5 scale now gives +8, and one of 14 on a 3-18 scale gives +7 instead of +14.
- **`chat-mode-narrator` requires only the stage contracts.** `branch-reply@1`, `living-world-rules@1`, `character-blueprint@1` and `character-presence@1` left its `requires`, so a session no longer pulls those plugins in because of the narrator. The dialogue pack and the bundled worlds request them by name; a world or pack of your own that relied on the narrator to bring them must list them.
- **A world translation file named with only a language is not read.** `WORLD.en.md`, `world.en.yaml` or `data/x.zh.yaml` in a world that declares `en-US` / `zh-CN` is ignored (with a server log line) and rejected when the world is installed or imported. An author renames the file to the declared locale (`WORLD.en-US.md`); `pnpm validate:world` names the new file name.
- **An agent writes through its tools only.** `statePatches`, `pluginData`, `notifications`, `assetGenerations`, `interactions`, `ui` and events with a `topic` in an agent's final JSON no longer become effects; they are removed and the server log names the runtime and the fields. Before, they were committed without passing `agent.tools` or the runtime's declared `effects`, so an agent that was allowed only read tools could still write session state. No bundled agent used the channel. A plugin whose agent wrote this way must give the agent a tool for the write (`plugin-data-set`, `emit-event`, `create-notification`, a plugin tool) and list it in `agent.tools`. `preGameDone` / `completion` in the final JSON still report completion, and events without a `topic` stay in the output.
- **A hook finds the runtime in its context, not in the payload.** `ctx.pluginId` and `ctx.runtimeId` name the runtime an event is about for every runtime event (`PreRuntime`, `PostContextAssembly`, `PreLLMCall`, `PostLLMResponse`, `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PostRuntime`, `PreStateCommit`, `PostStateCommit`). The payloads of `PostContextAssembly`, `PreLLMCall`, `PostLLMResponse` and `PreToolUse` no longer carry `pluginId` and `runtimeId`, and the `PostContextAssembly` payload no longer carries `locale`. A hook that reads `payload.runtimeId` or `payload.pluginId` must read `ctx.runtimeId` or `ctx.pluginId`; with the old name it matches no runtime and does nothing. `createNarrativeReview` of the SDK reads the context in the same way.
- **A concealed runtime's text stays out of the conversation.** Text that a runtime with `io.concealed: true` returns (`narrativeOutput` or `content`) is no longer written to the conversation log, so it does not reach other runtimes' prompt history, `GET /messages` or the rebuilt prompt. Its forms and UI blocks are written as before. A manifest that declares both `io.concealed: true` and `io.visibility: story` now fails to load: a story runtime's text is what the player reads. A plugin that relied on a concealed runtime's text appearing in the chat must hand the result over through `io.output` or an effect.
- **One session has one declarer of an event topic.** When two plugins of a session declare the same topic in `contributes.events`, the one accepted later is left out of the session with the rejection `event-topic-conflict`, and the reason names the topic and both plugins. Before, both were active and every event of the topic was validated against the schema of the plugin whose ID sorts first, with one warning in the server log. Both plugins can stay installed; a player picks one per session. A plugin author avoids the collision with a topic prefix of the plugin's own. `GET /api/plugins` and the session plugin list return `eventTopics` for each plugin.
- **`ctx.pluginData` reads have one shape in every context.** In an extension-point handler, `ctx.pluginData.get(namespace, key)` now returns the stored value, or `null` when there is none, as it does in a function runtime and a guard; before, it returned the whole row or `undefined`. `list(namespace)` returns `{ key, value, createdAt, updatedAt }` entries in both contexts: an extension handler no longer gets `pluginId`, `namespace`, `sessionId` and `id` on a row, and a function handler now gets the two timestamps. A plugin whose extension handler reads `(await ctx.pluginData.get(ns, key))?.value` must drop `.value`. The SDK type `ExtensionPluginDataRecord` is replaced by `PluginDataEntry` and `PluginDataReader`.
- **An interaction is answered once, and its follow-up turn runs once.** `submit-form` refuses a second submission of the same `(turnId, interactionId)` with `400` and `code: "interaction_already_submitted"` as soon as a turn has started after the answer was stored (a successful turn, or a failed one that the existing retry runs again). Before, the same values were accepted again and returned the first `submissionId`, so a second tab or a repeated click ran the follow-up turn twice. While no turn has followed the answer (the response was lost, or the browser closed between the answer and the follow-up) the same values are still accepted and return the stored `submissionId`, so the client can start the follow-up; different values are refused. The text of the response is written for the player in the session's language. A script that resent a submission after its turn ran, to read its `submissionId` again, must read the stored answer from `GET /api/sessions/:id/view` instead.
- **One plugin-data value is at most 256 KiB.** A `plugin.data` / `plugin.data.batch` proposal, `ctx.pluginData.set` or an RPC store write whose value is over 256 KiB of JSON (UTF-8) fails with an error that names the plugin, `namespace/key`, the size and the limit; the stored value stays as it was and nothing is truncated. The largest seeded record in the bundled worlds is about 7 KB, so no bundled plugin is near it. A plugin that keeps a growing list under one key must split it across keys or trim it.
- **The `characters` table has an `aliases` column; an earlier SQLite or PostgreSQL database must be recreated.** The server creates tables and never alters one, so a database created before this change opens without a message and then fails every character read and write with `no such column: aliases` (SQLite) or `column "aliases" does not exist` (PostgreSQL). Stop the server, back up the database file with its `-wal` and `-shm` files, and start with a new file (`<covelHome>/data/covel.db` for the desktop app, `./data/covel.db` for a development server without a Covel home); for PostgreSQL recreate the schema. Browser-private saves need nothing: their characters have no aliases.
- **`update-character` reports a miss with other words.** The error is now `Character "<what was passed>" not found. Closest known names: …` (or `Characters in session: …`), not `Character <id> not found in session. …`. A plugin or test that matched the old sentence must match `not found`.
- **A world whose characters share a name or alias does not validate.** In `characters/characters.json`, an `aliases` entry that equals another character's name or alias, or a name that equals another character's alias (letter case, width, spacing and the middle dot do not count), is an error of `pnpm validate:world` at the later record, and a session of such a world is not created. A generated world stored before this change whose character cards repeat an alias across characters fails the same way: edit the alias or generate the world again.
- **`affinity`, `npc-graph` and `codex` key a session character's record by the character's name.** A change addressed by an alias of a session character now goes to the record under the character's name. A development session that already has a second record under the alias keeps it; it is no longer written to.
- **One plugin-data value is capped at 256 KiB on every write path.** `PUT /api/sessions/:id/plugin-data/...` used to refuse values over 64 KB and now refuses values over 256 KiB (HTTP 413); world-package `worldData` records over 256 KiB now fail the import with an error diagnostic instead of being stored. Split an oversized record into several records or keys.
- **A form is answered through `POST /api/actions`, not through plugin-rpc.** The framework action `framework.submit-form` on `POST /api/sessions/:id/plugin-rpc` is gone (it now returns `404 unknown_action`), and so is the second request that sent the filled narrative as a `send_message`. A client sends one action, `{ type: "submit_interaction", payload: { turnId, submissions } }`, and reads the answer and the follow-up turn from that one SSE stream. Contract: `docs/v2/reference/api.md`, the player interaction section.
- **A refused answer arrives on the stream, not as HTTP 400.** `form_rejected` (with `details.issues`), `interaction_already_submitted` and `invalid_interaction_submission` are the `code` of a single `error.occurred` event on a `200` stream that stores nothing and starts no turn. A request for the form provider's code grant is still a `202` JSON response before the stream opens.
- **Submitting the same answer again is refused, whatever its values.** The server no longer accepts a resubmit of identical values to hand the stored narrative back: an answered interaction always has its turn, so a client that lost the response reads `submittedInteractions` and `GET /api/sessions/:id/execution` instead of sending again.
- **`submittedInteractions[].followedUp` is removed from the session view.** An entry in that list always has a turn after it. A development session that holds an answer stored by an earlier build with no turn after it now shows that form as answered; send any message to continue it, or recreate the session.
- **`@covel/runtime` no longer exports `createSubmitFormHandler`.** `createInteractionSubmitter` validates an answer and returns the rows for the caller to store in its own transaction.
- **The player cannot take a name or an alias of a character of the world.** The character form refuses it at the name field (`form_rejected` with an issue on `characterName`); before, a name equal to another character's full name was accepted and made a second character of that name. `create-character` with `type: "player"` now fails for such a name, where it returned the other character as "already exists" (alias) or created the duplicate (full name). A plugin that creates the player through `create-character` must ask the player for another name when the call fails. Existing sessions are not changed.
- **Sessions created before dimension values held one language no longer open.** The read-time repair that rewrote locale-map dimension records is gone, together with `resolveDimensionRecordLocale` and the `_legacy.dimension-locale-maps` backup namespace. Recreate such development sessions (this release already requires recreating existing databases).
- **An unknown `STORE_BACKEND`, `MEDIA_BACKEND` or `VECTOR_BACKEND` stops the server from starting.** Before, a typo such as `STORE_BACKEND=postgres` silently ran on a local SQLite file. The error names the variable and the accepted values; fix the value in `.env`, the shell or the desktop config. `NODE_ENV` keeps its fallback. See `docs/v2/reference/env-registry.md`.
- **`GET /api/sessions/:id/view` returns only the answers to the turns of its message window.** `submittedInteractions` held every answer of the session and grew with each choice; it now holds the answers whose `turnId` belongs to a returned message (the message's `turnId` or its form block's `meta.turnId`). The web client marks only blocks of that window, so it shows the same. A client that looked up an older turn's answer in this list no longer finds it. See `docs/v2/reference/api.md`.
- **`DataStore.listTurnMessages` takes `{ turnId }`.** The second argument is `PaginationOpts & { turnId? }`; with `turnId` only that turn's messages are returned. A `DataStore` implementation outside this repository must honour the option, or a form answer is checked against the wrong messages. The three built-in backends do, under a shared contract test.
- **Five SSE event types that nothing emitted are removed from `CovelEvent`:** `interaction.completed`, `state.snapshot`, `record.updated`, `connection.restored` and `memory.updated`. A client that listed them must stop handling them; no server code ever sent them.
- **A plugin action can no longer write a player submission, and its turn-message read is bounded.** `savePlayerInput` is removed from the store view of `registerRpc` handlers (`PluginRpcStore` in `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils`), so an action cannot store input that skips form validation for another plugin to trust. `listTurnMessages(limit?)` there now returns the most recent committed messages, at most 200, instead of the whole history; use `readTurnMessages` in a function runtime for paging. No bundled plugin used either. A community plugin that called `savePlayerInput` must submit through the form flow instead. See `docs/v2/reference/api.md`.
- **Emberback's Crownfire countdown has a new shape, so an Emberback development session made before this change must be created again.** `crownfire.stage` no longer has the member `passing`; the dimension has a new required field `frontPassed` (boolean) that the tracker sets when the narrative says the front has moved on. `minutesRemaining` and `stage` are computed from the station clock. A session keeps the definition it was created with, and that definition no longer matches the world. World version 1.2.0.
- **What `ctx.world` returns is frozen.** `ctx.world.characters`, `characterSchema`, `dimensions` and `worldRecord` were a new deep copy on every read: 2.4 ms for a cast of 200, paid again by each step of a loop that looks one character up. A read now returns one frozen copy until the execution buffers another write (the same loop: 494 ms before, under 1 ms after). A plugin that sorted or changed the returned value in place now gets a `TypeError`; copy first (`[...ctx.world.characters]`, `structuredClone`). `docs/v2/reference/plugins.md`.
- **The two unpaged trace endpoints return the newest 5000 events.** `GET /api/traces/:sessionId` and `GET /api/traces/:sessionId/turns` read every trace event of the session into memory, whole prompts included. They now read at most 5000 events, return `nextCursor` when older ones remain, accept `?cursor` to read those, and send `discovery` only on the request without a cursor. A client that needs everything follows the cursor (the debug page's "Load all" and `pnpm e2e:verify` do). `docs/v2/reference/api.md`.
- **`plugin-data.changed` carries `pluginId` and `changes` only.** The `source` field is gone; no client read it. Each change still carries the row's public value, because the client writes it into its own copy and reads nothing back. `docs/v2/reference/protocol.md`.
- **A resolved suspension no longer holds its prompt.** A suspension stored the whole request it continues, and kept it after the resume committed, so a session carried one prompt for every question the player had ever answered, in the database and in every browser checkpoint. `markSuspensionResolved` now keeps the execution identity, locale and turn number and drops the rest. Code that read `pendingContinuation.messages` of a resolved record finds an empty list. Resolved rows written by an earlier version keep their content. `docs/v2/internals/storage.md`.
- **`DataStore.claimSuspension` returns the claim marker, and a claim is released with `releaseSuspensionClaim`.** `claimSuspension(id)` now resolves to the marker it wrote (a string) or `null`, where it resolved to `true` / `false`. The new `releaseSuspensionClaim(id, claim)` reopens the suspension only while that marker is still in place. A custom `DataStore` must implement both; stored data is unchanged and no session has to be recreated.
- **The context budget result reports `overBudget`.** `applyBudget` in `@covel/context` returned `budgetExceeded`, which was true both when history was pruned to fit and when the prompt was still too large after pruning. It now returns `overBudget`, true only when the prompt is still over the limit; `prunedCount` says whether anything was pruned. The assembled context no longer carries `budgetExceeded`.
- **The rules segment of `world-init` is named `dimension-rules-truncated` when it leaves rules out.** It keeps the id `dimension-rules` when every rule fits. Code that looks the segment up by id must accept both.
- **The default first-token wait of a streamed agent call is shortened to leave room for the retries.** An agent that does not set `agent.loop.firstTokenTimeoutMs` no longer waits 120 seconds on every attempt: the last attempt may wait for all that is left of `timeoutMs`, and an earlier one for its equal share of it, or 60 seconds when the share is shorter, never more than half and never more than 120 seconds. With `timeoutMs: 120000` and `maxRetries: 3` the attempts wait 60, 30, 15 and 15 seconds. A runtime whose model sends its first output (text, reasoning or a tool call) later than that must set `firstTokenTimeoutMs`, which is used as written for every attempt, and a `timeoutMs` that fits it. Rule: `docs/v2/reference/plugins.md`.
- **A dimension settlement receipt no longer has `blockedBy`.** A session that holds a pending receipt written with it (a turn whose shared extraction failed, on an earlier build) cannot be read: recreate that session.

### Added

- **Settings has a page for stored media.** Settings → Stored media lists the pictures, audio and video the app has stored (generated by plugins, uploaded, or imported from a world for a session), with the size, the date and the sessions that use each item, and shows how much space the items that no session uses take. The player deletes chosen items or everything unused after a confirmation that states the count and the size; an item that a session still uses is deleted one at a time, after a confirmation that names the sessions, and those sessions then show a "media unavailable" placeholder in its place. Nothing is deleted without that action, and files inside world packages are never touched. Served by `GET /api/media/library` and `POST /api/media/library/delete`, on a self-hosted server and the desktop app only; hosted tiers answer `503`. `MediaLifecyclePolicy` gains `onlyIds`. See `docs/v2/reference/media-store.md`.
- **A self-hosted web deployment has server-side settings.** A setting the server itself acts on is now kept by the server, in its database, and can be changed in Settings on a self-hosted web deployment as well as in the desktop app; every browser of the install sees the same value. The registry marks such a setting with `scope: "server"`; the first one is "Keep diagnostic traces". `GET /api/config/server-settings` reports each value in force, its source and whether it can be set, and `PUT` stores a change, which applies to the next commit or sweep without a restart. Writes are accepted on `DEPLOYMENT_TIER=self` only and checked against the list of known keys and their allowed values; on `demo` / `commercial` the operator sets these through the environment and the control is locked. The environment variable still wins over a stored value. The control stays disabled until the server has answered, and a write the server refuses shows the reason and puts the earlier value back. A new `server_settings` table is created at start on SQLite and PostgreSQL. See `docs/v2/reference/settings-store.md`.
- **Trace retention is a player setting, and a sweep reaches idle sessions.** Settings → General has "Keep diagnostic traces" (7 / 30 / 90 days or keep everything) with a plain-words explanation; on a deployment that fixes the period, or on a `demo` / `commercial` server, the field shows the value in force and is disabled. The server also sweeps every session at start and once a day, so traces of a session nobody plays again are deleted too. A new SQLite database is created with incremental auto-vacuum and the trace delete hands pages back to the file; a database created earlier keeps its size (freed pages are reused, see `docs/v2/internals/storage.md`). Precedence: `docs/v2/reference/env-registry.md`.
- **OpenAI requests carry a prompt cache key.** Every model request of an agent runtime sends `prompt_cache_key` on the OpenAI Chat and Responses protocols: a hash of the session and the runtime, equal for the requests that share a prefix. OpenAI routes by it, so a runtime's request on the next turn reaches the machine that holds the cached prefix. The field goes to `api.openai.com` only; a relay that forwards it is switched on with `promptCacheKey = true` in the slot's `providerOptions`, and `false` turns it off. See `docs/v2/reference/slots.md`.
- **The story model knows which pictures the player saw, and a model that takes images sees them.** When a picture is committed, the conversation history gets one line at that point: a picture was shown, and what it depicts (`meta.caption`, else `meta.prompt`; no IDs, paths or timestamps). Every model that reads the history gets it. A story agent whose model slot is known to accept image input also receives the newest 2 pictures themselves, as inline image parts read from the media store (session-checked, PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF, at most 3,750,000 bytes each). They sit in the per-turn part of the request, so the cached history prefix does not change when a picture ages out; the cost is about 1,500 input tokens per picture on each story call. Every protocol adapter (OpenAI Chat, OpenAI Responses, Anthropic, Gemini) encodes inline image data, and traces record its length instead of the bytes. See `docs/v2/reference/prompt-structure.md`.
- **A world's translation files have one spelling, in the game as in the validator.** A file such as `WORLD.en-US.md`, `world.en-US.yaml` or `data/items.en-US.yaml` is read for a session when its locale is written exactly as the world's `supportedLocales` writes it. A file that names only the language (`WORLD.en.md`) is no longer read through a language fallback; the server log names it and the file it should be called, `pnpm validate:world` reports it as before, and installing or importing a community world with such a file fails with the same message. Fallback between editions the world has (a `zh-TW` session reading the `zh-CN` edition) is unchanged. Plugins (`locales/<locale>.yaml`) and prompt bodies (`*.zh.md`) keep their own rules.
- **A refused form shows each message under its field.** The `form_rejected` response now carries `details.issues` (`{ field?, message }`), one entry for every field that failed instead of only the first. The form in the message list shows each message under its field, focuses the first one and keeps a summary above the fields for errors that belong to no field; the "Check the form" toast is gone. A plugin form validator may return a string (form-level, as before), `{ field, message }` or a list of them (`PluginFormIssue` in the SDK). The `error` text of the response is all messages joined by newlines.
- **A form's `notice` is a declared field.** `create-form` takes an optional `notice` (the note above the fields when a form is offered again), and the SDK exports `PluginFormInteraction` for a form sent through `effects.interactions`. `char-creator` now passes it through the tool instead of patching the returned object.
- **History compaction keeps a new summary when the merge of older ones fails.** The model call that wrote the new summary is no longer thrown away; the provider returns it with `deferred: { reason }` (new optional field of `history.compact@2`), the reason is recorded as `context.compaction.failed`, and the merge is tried again on a later turn. Meanwhile the summaries may exceed their budget, up to twice the segment and token limits.
- **`pnpm prompt:prefix <covel.db>` shows what a prompt cache can serve.** For each runtime of a recorded session it lists the size of a turn's first model request, how much of it repeats the previous turn's from the first character, and the message and offset where the two first differ. It reads the recorded requests in `trace_events` and needs no model. See `docs/v2/reference/prompt-structure.md`.
- **The world page says when the lore does not fit.** A world whose `WORLD.md` (narrator-only parts included) is over the 8,000-token budget of the story prompt shows a notice with the estimated size. The budget and its measure now live in `@covel/shared` (`fitWorldLore`), shared by the prompt, `pnpm validate:world` and the page.
- **A world declares its own cover and accent colour.** `world.yaml` takes `cover` (an image one directory under `media/`) and `accentColor` (`#rrggbb` or `oklch(L C H)`). The world card, the world page, the preparation screen and the session backdrop show what the world declares; a world without them gets the app's default picture and a hue derived from its ID. `pnpm validate:world` rejects a `cover` that names a file the server cannot show. The four bundled worlds declare theirs.
- **A hook knows the session's language.** Every hook context has `locale`, the session's content locale, for the turn, commit and session events alike. A hook that adds text to a prompt picks its language with `instructionLocaleFor(ctx.locale)`; the bundled hooks no longer guess it from the prompt text.
- **`docs/v2/reference/hooks.md` describes every hook event**: when it fires, its payload, what a handler may return, how failures are handled and what the context holds. A test fails when an event is missing from the page.
- **Fonts ship with the app, and the app contacts no font host at start.** Inter, Fraunces, Newsreader, Geist and Geist Mono (Latin subset, SIL OFL 1.1, from `@fontsource-variable/*`) are bundled; the render-blocking Google Fonts stylesheet and its preconnects are gone, so the first paint no longer waits for a host that is unreachable in some regions, and the desktop app makes no third-party request at launch. Syne, which no theme used, is dropped. The added files are about 530 KB, loaded only for the faces a theme uses. An imported theme's own remote CSS still loads.
- **Images from other websites in the story or a world document wait for the player.** Images the app serves itself and `data:` / `blob:` images load as before; an image on another origin shows a placeholder naming the host, with "Load this image" and "Always load images from this host in this world" (kept per world in the settings store). The built page also carries a Content-Security-Policy (`docs/v2/internals/security.md`).
- **Memory search no longer depends on an embedding model for good results.** Without an embedding slot, `memory-search` ranks with BM25 instead of counting shared words: a name or an item outweighs a word that every passage holds, and a long passage is not put last for being long. On a synthetic 60-message Chinese session with paragraph-length narration, the first result was the right one for 11 of 12 questions, against 6 before. An embedding slot stays optional and is used when present.
- **`memory-search` reads the history summaries.** Recall covers the summaries that history compaction wrote as well as the latest 500 messages, so a turn that is older than those messages can still be found. A summary result has the role `summary`.
- **A plugin can make a data namespace searchable.** `contributes.data.<namespace>.search: { text: <field> }` puts the records of the namespace into `memory-search` (source `archival:plugin_data`) while the plugin is active in the session, and into the vector index when an embedding model is configured. A namespace without the declaration is not read.
- **The plugin SDK exports the ranking the kernel uses.** `rankTexts`, `searchTerms` and `searchExcerpt` from `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils` rank a plugin's own texts against a query with the same BM25 implementation, with no model call.
- **The story remembers what happened, not only how things are now.** The `memory` plugin adds up to three facts of each turn to a `facts` namespace that is never rewritten. `memory-search` finds them, and each story prompt brings back the earlier facts that the player's message is about.
- **OpenAI Decisions is an evaluation protocol.** A model with `protocol = "openai-decisions-v1"` (today `gpt-6-luna`) answers `gateway.evaluate()` and `ctx.gateway.evaluate()` through `POST /v1/decisions`. The settings page offers it under Evaluation and preselects it for the `openai` provider. The public question and answer types do not change: a boolean is sent as a `predicate`, choices and score levels as lists, and the answers come back keyed by question. A question the model declines fails the call as a refusal. See `docs/v2/reference/evaluation.md`.
- **Nine more providers can be picked by name.** xAI, Mistral AI, Groq, Together AI, Fireworks AI, Moonshot AI, Zhipu AI, SiliconFlow and Volcengine Ark join the built-in providers. Settings → Providers & Models → Add provider has a provider list that fills in the ID and the official endpoint; "Custom provider" keeps the manual fields. The endpoints are in `docs/v2/reference/slots.md`.
- **A plugin can provide a text protocol.** `covel.registerWires({ text: [{ id, generateText, streamText }] })` registers a request and response format beside the four built-in ones. A model whose `protocol` is `<pluginId>/<wireId>` uses it for every text call, the story model included; `llm.toml` and the settings page accept the ID, and the protocol list of the settings page shows it (`GET /api/ai/protocols`). Object calls work through `generateText`, and an optional `listModels` feeds the model list. See `docs/v2/reference/plugin-extensions.md`.
- **`ctx.gateway.generateText` is typed for `providerOptions`.** The runtime already passed it on; the plugin SDK type did not have the field.
- **Services on this machine can be picked by name and need no key.** Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp server, vLLM and LiteLLM Proxy are in the provider list at their default ports; a local gateway on another port, or one that serves several protocols, is a custom provider at `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/v1`. A model on a loopback address counts as ready without an API key, so it can start a session, run background tasks and be tested. Before, such a model was shown as not configured until a placeholder key was entered.
- **The settings page reads a service's own model list.** "Read the model list from the service" in the add-provider and add-model dialogs asks the endpoint for its model IDs (`POST /api/ai/models`); a tick adds one, a filter narrows the list, and IDs typed by hand stay.
- **A failed connection test says what kind of failure it was.** `POST /api/ai/ping` and the model list return `errorKind` (`unreachable`, `timeout`, `auth`, `quota`, `rate_limited`, `not_found`, `bad_request`, `overloaded`, `server`, `refused`, `config`) and the settings page shows what to do about it. A refused connection names the host and port instead of `fetch failed`.
- **An `llm.toml` slot of a built-in provider needs only `provider` and `model`.** `baseUrl` and `protocol` come from the provider when the slot omits them. For any other provider `protocol` defaults to `openai-chat-v1` and `baseUrl` stays required.
- **A world can keep notes for the narrator out of the player's view.** In `WORLD.md`, the lines between `<!-- narrator-only -->` and `<!-- /narrator-only -->` go to the model and are not shown in the World tab, on the session preparation screen or on the world page. It keeps spoilers out of sight and is not secrecy: the files and the API hold the whole text. `pnpm validate:world` reports a marker that is not closed. The four bundled worlds use it for hidden plotlines and game-master notes, and world generation writes such parts this way.
- **Character cards reach the story.** The `character-blueprint` plugin gives the narrative each card's voice, traits, goals, fears, secrets (for the narrator only), relationships, rules and example lines as a prompt segment, for the characters that are in the session, within a budget of 6000 estimated tokens. Before, only the panel read the cards. World generation writes preset characters by default and no longer asks for character fields that had no effect.
- **A wrong alias can be taken away.** `update-character` and `sync-characters.updates[]` accept `removeAliases`, and a `character.upsert` proposal with `expectedVersion` reads the same field. Names are compared as everywhere else (case, width, spacing); a name that is not an alias of the character changes nothing, and the character's own name stays. An alias-conflict error now says that the other character's alias can be removed when it is wrong. There is no editor for it in the app.
- **A character has aliases, and one rule turns a name into a character.** A story calls one person many things; the models that track characters, relationships and affinity looked a person up by one exact name, and a miss made a second character, a relationship on the wrong one or a lost update. A character of the World Model now has `aliases` (ordered, as shown). A world author writes them in `characters/characters.json`; the bundled worlds carry the ones their character cards already listed. A model adds one through `aliases` of `update-character` / `sync-characters` when the story reveals a name, and `create-character` takes them too. `resolveCharacter` in `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils` is the one lookup: exact ID, then name, then alias, compared without regard to letter case, full and half width, spacing, surrounding quotation marks, the middle dot of a transliterated name, the kind of apostrophe and a leading "the"; titles and honorifics are not removed, and there is no fuzzy match. A name that two characters have is reported as ambiguous. An alias that belongs to another character is refused, by the tools and again at commit, with that character named. `update-character` accepts the id, the name or an alias; a miss names the closest known characters. `create-character` returns the existing character for a name that is an alias. `get-character` and `list-characters` show aliases. `affinity`, `npc-graph`, `codex`, `scene-stage` and `world-ir` resolve names through it, and the character panel shows aliases (read-only). In prompts a name with aliases reads `Name (aka a, b)`: 5 to 9 tokens per character on the bundled worlds. See `docs/v2/reference/world-model.md`.
- **A post-turn runtime can read the time settled for the current turn.** `world-time/advance` publishes the contract `world-time-evolution@1` with a schema. Its `summary` holds the display text, the base unit, the time elapsed since the session started and the time elapsed in this turn. Bind it as an optional input with `select: /summary`; the binding also orders the consumer after the time settlement. Reference: `docs/v2/reference/world-time.md`. For world authors: write the `updateRule` of a time-bound dimension as a function of the clock (`docs/v2/reference/dynamic-dimensions.md`).
- **`interaction.submitted` SSE event.** It is the first event of a `submit_interaction` stream: the values the server stored, each filled narrative, and the id and text of the player message the follow-up turn carries. Payload: `docs/v2/reference/protocol.md`.
- **`lateSystemAsUser` makes the story model's prompt cache reach the end of the previous turn's history on a relay that moves system messages.** Off by default. The turn's data and rules are a system message placed after the history; a relay that moves system messages to the front (the local Codex-style proxy used for tests does) puts that changing text ahead of the history, so the narrator read only the relay's own fixed instructions from cache. With `lateSystemAsUser = true` in a slot's `providerOptions` on `openai-chat-v1` or `openai-responses-v1`, a system message after the first conversation message goes out as a `user` message in `<system-instruction>` tags, in the same place and with the same text. On that proxy, in an eight-turn session, the narrator's first request of a turn read 55% of its input from cache instead of 22%. It changes the role the model sees; other servers were not measured. See `docs/v2/reference/slots.md`.
- **Settings says when the environment overrides a saved provider key.** On desktop, a provider key in the shell, `.env` or `.env.llm` outranks the key saved in Settings. `GET /api/llm-config` now lists such providers in `envKeyOverrides`, and the API keys page shows a notice under the key. Precedence is unchanged (`docs/v2/reference/api.md`).
- **A new model's reasoning levels can be added without a release.** A `reasoning-models.json` in the user configuration directory (`~/.covel/` on desktop, `COVEL_USER_CONFIG_DIR`) lists model name patterns and the levels they take; the server reads it at start, before the bundled entries. An invalid file is reported in the log and ignored. Format and an example: the reasoning level data section of `docs/v2/reference/slots.md`.
- **One request restores all plugin data of a session.** `GET /api/sessions/:id/plugin-data` returns the readable rows of every active plugin with the same visibility rules as the per-plugin and per-namespace reads; the web client uses it for reconnect, return to the tab and reset instead of one request per active plugin. See `docs/v2/reference/api.md`.
- **Releases attach a `SHA256SUMS.txt` for the installers.** The release workflow lists the SHA-256 of every `.dmg`, `.zip` and `.exe`, so an unsigned download can be checked against it. See `docs/v2/internals/desktop-packaging.md`.
- **A world can declare that a dimension field is computed from the world clock, and code then computes it every turn.** A schema node takes `x-derive`: `source: clock.elapsedSinceStart`, a linear form (`start`, `perUnit`, optional `min` / `max`), and for a label an ordered list of `ranges`. The new function runtime `world-init/dimension-clock` reads the settled clock through `world-time-evolution@1`, after the time settlement and the tracker, and commits the value with a versioned `dimension.update`, in the same turn and without a model call. The value depends on the clock only, so a turn that is run again or a session forked from an earlier turn has the value of its clock. `pnpm validate:world` checks the declaration: where it may stand, the type, that every label is a value the field allows, that the bounds lie inside the field's `minimum` / `maximum`, and that `initialValue` is the value at the start. Reference: `docs/v2/reference/dynamic-dimensions.md`.
- **The SDK exports the derivation helpers.** `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils/dimensions` has `applyDimensionDerivations`, `derivedDimensionFields`, `deriveDimensionValue`, `keepDerivedDimensionFields`, `dimensionSchemaWithoutDerived` and `dimensionValueWithoutDerived`. A schema in a `world.dimensions@1` snapshot can carry `x-derive`.
- **`compareText` in the plugin SDK and `@covel/shared`.** Compares two strings by Unicode code point, the order SQLite and PostgreSQL give a binary-collated column. Use it to sort IDs, keys and timestamps and to break ties. `docs/v2/plugins/essentials.md`.
- **The server log names an in-process session lock wait that passes 30 seconds.** The lock used by the Memory and SQLite backends still waits (a turn holds its session for the whole model run, and a request queued behind it must run when it ends), and now writes one line when a wait is that long: the first sign of an owner that never returns.
- **Context and model-call hooks are told which prompt segments were used.** The `PostContextAssembly` and `PreLLMCall` payloads carry `promptSegments`: the `prompt.segment@1` segments the runtime's prompt was built with, each as `{ pluginId, id }`. A plugin reads it to learn what it put in the prompt and no longer has to search the prompt text. Reference: `docs/v2/reference/hooks.md`.
- **`ctx.gateway.generateText` takes `responseFormat`.** A function runtime passes `{ type: "json_schema", schema }` to ask for a JSON reply: the provider's JSON mode is used where there is one, and the schema is added to the instructions. `text` is still text, which the plugin parses and checks.
- **A plugin's warnings and errors reach the server log.** `ctx.logger.warn` and `ctx.logger.error` still write to the session's `_logs` ring, and now also print one line (`[plugin-log] <runtimeId>: <message> <meta>`, the detail cut at 2000 characters).

### Changed

- **`list-characters` returns at most 50 characters.** The most-interacted come first; `count` stays the total, and `_text` ends with how many were left out and how to find one (filter by `type`, or `get-character` by name). The `characters` array holds only the listed ones.
- **Anthropic requests send every message text as a block.** With cache breakpoints on, a message's text is always a one-block array instead of a string on some turns and an array on the turn it carries the history breakpoint, so the cached prefix is the same bytes on the next turn.
- A browser-private checkpoint carries only the newest revision of each runtime export, not every revision ever published, so the upload and download at each action stop growing with the number of turns on that part.
- **Tools on the Responses protocol are no longer strict by accident.** Function tools are sent with `strict: false`. The Responses API treats a tool as strict when the field is absent (Chat Completions does the opposite), which makes every optional argument required: the model then filled each one with an empty value (`defaultValue: ""`, `min: 0`, `options: []`) that the tool refused. In three-turn sessions through a local proxy, the same world gave 1 and 5 rejected tool calls on Responses (one runtime failed) against 0 on Chat Completions, and 0 on Responses after the change.
- **A Responses tool loop works without provider-side storage.** With `store: false` (now the default) the provider cannot find a reasoning item of the previous response by its ID, so Covel asks for `reasoning.encrypted_content` whenever the request has reasoning on, for any model name (before: only when the slot set `store = false` and the name matched GPT-5 / o3 / o4), and sends the items back as they came. A reasoning item that arrived without encrypted content is left out of the next request and the other items go back without their IDs. When a model answers 400 to the request for encrypted reasoning, the request is sent once more without it and the endpoint is remembered for the life of the process. A stream whose final event has an empty `output` now keeps the items that arrived one by one; before, the reasoning of that response was lost for the next call. Recorded provider requests in traces show `include`.
- **`inventory` and `core-quest` ledgers are plain functions.** They were wrapped as model tools (a factory taking `tool` and `z`, parameter descriptions for a model) although only their own function runtime calls them; `inventory`'s unreachable `set` operation is removed. `story-events` reads multi-language text with the SDK's `resolveI18nText` instead of its own copy.
- **The two narrative engines have package tests**: the stage and contract, every player setting reaching the prompt in both languages, and the same placeholders in the English and Chinese bodies.
- **Provider errors carry a type, and nothing decides by their wording.** An HTTP failure of a model endpoint is thrown as `AiProviderError` where the response is read (before, it was an `Error` whose message was JSON that the gateway parsed back). `@covel/ai-provider` exports `OutboundFetchError` (no response from the endpoint), `SsrfPolicyError`, `ProviderBaseUrlError` and `WireAlreadyRegisteredError`, and marks a request rejected for tool arguments that are not JSON with `details.requestFault`. The retry and fallback rules read these, an error's `statusCode` / `status` / `isRetryable` / `code` fields and the provider's own error code; matching on a message remains only as a marked fallback for an adapter that throws plain errors. A schema failure reads `[provider] reply does not match the requested schema` instead of `[provider] HTTP ? — …`.
- **The agents that run after the story send less new text each turn.** A provider's prompt cache serves a request only as far as it repeats an earlier one from the first character, so the part of a request that does not repeat the previous turn's is what a turn costs at the full price. In three-turn sessions on the bundled worlds that part fell by about a third (Lantern Barrow: 47,000 to 32,000 estimated tokens over three turns; Mistport: 53,000 to 34,000), and the requests are 7–9% smaller. These are measurements of the recorded requests with estimated tokens, not a provider's bill. What changed:
  - An input whose whole value is this turn's story text is no longer sent twice. The conversation already carries the text as a message, so the slot in `<runtime-inputs>` names that message. `ctx.inputs` and `ctx.inputSlots` keep the value. A prompt body that tells the model to read `runtime-inputs.<name>.value` needs no change; a hook that searched the rendered prompt for the story text must read `payload.inputSlots`.
  - The dimension tracker's rules and schemas are in the system prompt (`<dimension-rules>`, a session segment) and only the current values change with the turn (`<dimension-values>`). Neither block carries a version.
  - The character tracker's roster (`<existing-characters>`: id, name, type, description) is a session segment and the current fields are a block of their own (`<character-fields>`, one line per id). A changed number no longer changes the roster.
  - `world-ir` puts the character roster and the vocabulary ahead of the narrative in its request.
  - `affinity`, `codex`, `npc-graph/extractor` and `world-time/advance` read no earlier turns (`agent.history.maxTurns: 0`), and `char-creator/character-tracker` and `world-init/dimension-tracker` read one. They kept two from before the running turn's story became part of the conversation; the player's message and this turn's story are always sent.
  - The prompt of `chat-mode-narrator` no longer carries a note for maintainers, and the `npc-graph` prompt no longer describes an update time that its rows do not have.
- **An Anthropic request marks the end of the history for the cache.** The one moving breakpoint sat on the last block of a request, after this turn's data, so the next turn could read only the system prompt from the cache and the whole history was written again. A second breakpoint now sits on the last message of the history. This follows the API's documented rules and was not measured against the API.
- **Breaking: a world's translation files use the locale exactly as `supportedLocales` writes it.** Bundled worlds mixed `WORLD.en.md` and `WORLD.en-US.md`; they now all use the full tag (`WORLD.en-US.md`, `world.en-US.yaml`, `data/items.en-US.yaml`, `media/gallery.en-US.json`). `pnpm validate:world` reports a translation file named with a bare language (`.en`, `.zh`) as an error and names the file it should be called. A community world that still uses `.en` files should rename them; the server still reads them, but the validator rejects the package until it does.
- **Editing a world's files reaches the app without a restart.** The world watcher used to reload a package only when its dimensions changed, and only for `.yaml` and `.md` files. It now reloads when any text file of the package changes (the `WORLD.md` body and its language editions, `world.yaml`, `data/`, `characters/`, `media/*.json`) and updates the world record when anything in it differs. The log says what was reloaded. A new session uses the edited package, and a running session reads the new lore on its next turn unless it has its own lore override. Characters, lorebook entries and dimension values a session already imported are not rewritten.
- **Breaking: the web app no longer holds a table of world covers.** The picture and colour per world ID, and the by-tag fallback to a bundled picture, are gone, as are the unused `cloudmere`, `neonridge`, `emberback`, `haruka-academy` and `lantern-barrow` files in `apps/web/public/visuals/worlds/`. An installed, community or generated world that has no `cover` now shows the default background. Add `cover` to such a world's `world.yaml` to restore a picture.
- **The raw Database tab is a developer view.** It no longer sits among the player's tabs, where it showed every public plugin row, narrator-only character secrets included. Turn on "Developer view" in Settings to see it; the `/debug` page is unchanged.
- **A plugin form validator receives the session's language**: a third argument `{ locale, messages }` to pass to `translate`, so the text a player reads when a form is refused is translated.
- **A memory block over its limit is shortened by the model.** The `memory` plugin asks for a shorter version of the block and cuts at the end of a sentence only when that fails. Before, the text after the limit was cut off.
- **`finishReason` has one vocabulary for every protocol.** The model gateway returns `stop`, `length`, `tool_calls`, `content_filter`, `error` or `other`, and keeps the provider's own word in `rawFinishReason`, as the AI SDK does with `unified` and `raw`. A plugin that compared `ctx.gateway` results with a provider's word (`end_turn`, `max_tokens`, `tool_use`) must compare with the unified value. The runtime and world generation read the same table (`unifyFinishReason` in `@covel/shared`) in place of three separate ones.
- **Transport retries follow the providers' SDKs.** HTTP 408 and 409 are sent again like 429 and 5xx, `x-should-retry` decides when the provider sets it, and `retry-after-ms` is read before `Retry-After`. When the provider asks for a wait of more than 60 seconds, or more than the call has left, the call does not wait: it ends as rate limited and can use a backup model. Before, it waited until its time ran out and ended without a backup.
- **A connection test is one request.** It no longer retries, so its result and latency are those of the request it sent. An embedding model is tested with a vector request; before, it was sent a text request and reported as failed.
- **A protocol is declared in two places instead of thirteen files.** The protocol IDs, their names in the settings UI and whether they produce text or evaluations come from one table in `@covel/shared`; a protocol's reasoning fields, provider options, optional parameters and media-wire rule are fields of its `ProtocolDefinition`. `docs/v2/reference/slots.md` has the steps for adding a provider or a protocol.
- **Forking a session is rate limited.** `POST /api/sessions/:id/fork` accepts at most 10 requests per minute for one parent session and client address, and answers `429 rate_limit_exceeded` beyond that.
- **`list-npc-graph` lists the most recently seen people first.** When the cap cuts the node list, the newest nodes stay and the result says how many were left out (`nodesTruncated`).
- **`create-form` no longer offers `submitBehavior.immediate`.** Nothing read it. A plugin that still passes it is not rejected; the key is ignored. The `char-creator` prompt no longer tells the model to send it.
- **A changelog entry is a file of its own.** A change adds `docs/changelog.d/<slug>.md` with its entries under `### <Section>` headings and no longer edits `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, so two pull requests do not conflict over the changelog. `pnpm changelog:preview` prints the pending `[Unreleased]` section, `pnpm changelog:release <version>` moves the entries into `docs/CHANGELOG.md` at release, and `pnpm check` validates every fragment (`pnpm check:changelog`). A branch opened earlier may still add lines under `[Unreleased]` until the next release; after it `pnpm check` refuses them. Format: `docs/changelog.d/README.md`.
- **The pre-push hook tests what the push changed.** It still installs in a clean checkout and runs `pnpm check` and `pnpm e2e --list`, and now runs the tests of the packages the branch changed since the remote's main branch, with the packages that depend on them and the suites that read the changed files from outside their package; CI runs every suite. A change to the lockfile, `turbo.json`, `vitest.base.ts`, the root `package.json` or `tsconfig.json`, a push to `main` and a pushed tag still run everything, and so does `COVEL_PUSH_CHECK=full git push` or `pnpm check:push --full`. A commit that passed is remembered for 12 hours: a push that is repeated, or one that follows `pnpm check:push`, sends at once.
- **`pnpm release:preflight` fails when `docs/CHANGELOG.md` has no section for the version in `package.json`,** and the release workflow refuses to publish a tag while a changelog fragment is not assembled.
- **`submitBehavior.echoFilledNarrative` is read by the server.** It comes from the committed interaction, not from the request. With `false`, that interaction's narrative is left out of the follow-up turn's player message, as before.
- **On the native Gemini protocol, the turn's data and rules no longer break the prompt cache at the start of every request.** `google-generative-ai-v1` used to put every system message into `systemInstruction`, which precedes the conversation, so the per-turn text changed the very beginning of the request. Now only the system messages ahead of the conversation go there; a later one stays in place as user text in `<system-instruction>` tags, as the Anthropic protocol already does, so the request is identical up to the end of the previous turn's history. Neighbouring turns of one role are merged into one, and function responses always directly follow their function call. This is the default; `lateSystemAsUser = false` in the slot's `providerOptions` restores the earlier shape (every system message in `systemInstruction`) and is the way back if Google's endpoint refuses a request. The model now reads that text as user text instead of a system instruction; quality was not compared. The request shape was accepted across a tool loop by a local proxy that imitates the Gemini interface; it was not verified against Google's endpoint, and cache reads on Gemini were not measured. See `docs/v2/reference/prompt-structure.md` and `docs/v2/reference/slots.md`.
- **A function runtime's `ctx.gateway.generateText` calls carry the prompt cache key.** The host fills `promptCacheKey` with the hash of the session and the runtime (`promptCacheKeyFor`), so a runtime that keeps its instructions and stable data at the start of the request reads them from cache on the next call; a key a plugin passes is replaced. History compaction (a short fixed instruction, calls far apart), memory embeddings (no prompt cache) and world generation and translation (no session; only a retry repeats a prefix) send no key. See `docs/v2/reference/slots.md`.
- **Four reads no longer grow with the length of a session.** An automatic snapshot takes the last ID and the compaction tags of the model history instead of reading every message with its text. A runtime that writes several characters in one turn reads the character list once for the batch instead of once per character; the name and alias check runs against the batch's own earlier writes, as before. `POST /api/sessions/:id/messages/sync` looks up only the IDs in the request instead of reading every message of the session. The local media store reads and writes picture files without blocking the server, so a multi-megabyte picture attached to a story call or uploaded by one session no longer pauses the streams of the others. `DataStore` gains `listCompactedTurnMessageTags` and `listExistingMessageIds`; a custom backend must implement both.
- **`GET /api/sessions/:id/messages` and `GET /api/sessions/:id/state` are rate-limited** like the paged reads: 120 requests a minute per client and path, then `429` with `code: "rate_limit_exceeded"`. See `docs/v2/reference/api.md`.
- **A refused `emit-event` to a topic nobody declared no longer fails the runtime.** The call is still reported to the model as failed and is no business work (`requireToolUse` and `completeAfterTools` are not satisfied by it), but an agent that then finishes with text or `runtime-done` succeeds, so removing the plugin that consumes a topic does not break the plugin that emits it. A payload that breaks the declared schema stays a failure. See `docs/v2/reference/tools.md`.
- **A browser-stored session warns before it reaches the upload limit.** From 80% of the 64 MiB checkpoint limit the web client shows one notice per session and page load. Before, the first sign was a refused upload. See `docs/v2/internals/storage.md`.
- **A template variable inside a tagged block is XML-escaped, and world lore cannot close its own block.** In a prompt body, a `{{ ... }}` value that sits between a line starting with `<name>` and the matching `</name>` (for example `{{ world.description }}` inside `<world-summary>`) is escaped, so a closing tag in a name or a description cannot leave its block. In the `<world-lore>` block the kernel writes, only a `</world-lore>` inside the lore is rewritten; quote lines, comment markers and `&` in the lore read as written.
- **`soundtrack` matches a track's scene names as whole names.** A name inside a longer word, or a longer scene name inside a shorter location, no longer plays the track ("Hall" is not "Great Hall"; "inn" is not in "Dunn"). The rule is the one `scene-stage` uses for a name inside a location.
- **`story-events` counts turns with `ctx.logicalTurn`**, the scheduler's frozen logical turn, instead of reading the session row, so a background run counts the turn it was started for.
- **The session-prep world document editor shows the same over-length note as the world page** when the draft is longer than the story prompt carries.
- **The `codex`, `affinity` and `guide` prompts** use examples from more than one genre and no longer name other plugins or "working memory".
- **Which reasoning levels a model takes is data.** The model name checks that decided it are now entries in `packages/ai-provider/src/capability/reasoning-models.data.json`; the code only writes a selected level into each protocol's request. The levels offered and the fields sent are the same for every model name in the bundled tables (recorded in `packages/ai-provider/tests/__snapshots__/reasoning-characterization.snap.txt`, which the weekly model-table refresh re-records), apart from the fix below. A wire a plugin registers receives the matched level and the parameter form as `option` and `parameterStyle` in its `reasoningFields` request.
- **The dimension tracker no longer settles a derived field.** The schema and the value that `world-init/dimension-tracker` is given leave derived fields out, its prompt says not to write them, and `update-dimensions` keeps the current value of a derived field whatever the model writes. `followsClock` stays for rules that depend on time and are not a derivation (Emberback's power drain per hour). Plugin `world-init` 0.0.37.
- **Emberback's storm countdown is computed, not calculated by a model.** `minutesRemaining` is 180 minus the minutes on the station clock since the start, and `stage` follows it by ranges. The prose rule that asked the tracker for the subtraction is gone.
- **Order no longer depends on the server's language.** 66 sorts in the framework and the bundled plugins used `localeCompare` without a locale, so prompt lines, scheduling ties between runtimes, stored rows and API lists could come out in a different order on a machine with another system language. They now compare by code point. Names made of lower-case letters, digits and hyphens keep the order they had; names with upper-case letters, underscores or non-Latin characters can move. The two lists a person reads (model IDs, built-in providers) use a collator with a fixed locale.
- **The event trail is deleted with the traces.** The `events` table holds a copy of every event the bus publishes, the whole value of each changed plugin row included, and nothing deleted it. It now follows `COVEL_TRACE_RETENTION_DAYS` (30 days by default) in the same sweeps as `trace_events`. `docs/v2/internals/storage.md`.
- **The character roster in the tracker's prompt has a budget.** It listed every character with its whole description on every turn: 22,500 estimated tokens for a cast of 100 with a paragraph each. Within 6000 tokens nothing changes; past that every description is cut to 120 characters, and past that again descriptions are left out (1,300 tokens for the same cast). IDs, names, aliases and types are always listed.
- **A history summary call has an output ceiling.** The call for a summary of N tokens passes `maxOutputTokens` of twice N (at least 1024), so a model that ignores the requested length stops there and not at the slot's whole output budget. A reply cut at the ceiling that already fills its budget is kept and marked `truncated`; a shorter one fails the attempt as before, and the raw messages stay.
- **`npc-graph` stores no adjacency index.** `upsert-npc-graph` wrote one `index` row per character on every change, and nothing read them: the retriever builds the adjacency from the edges. Sessions keep working; `index` rows of an existing session are left in place and unused.
- **`pnpm check:i18n` compares every `t()` fallback in web code with the `en-US` text.** A fallback that differs from the catalogue fails the check. 94 fallbacks that had drifted from the catalogue now equal it; the interface shows the same text as before.

### Fixed

- **A form answered in another tab or on another device shows as answered, and a lost follow-up can be sent again.** `GET /api/sessions/:id/view` returns `submittedInteractions` (`{ turnId, interactionId, values, followedUp }`), the answers the server stored; `followedUp` is derived from the trace journal (a turn started after the answer). The client marks the matching form blocks answered from it on restore and reconnect when `followedUp` is true, and replaces its own browser cache with it. A block whose answer no turn followed stays open with the stored values filled in, so one click sends it again and starts the follow-up. A submit that the server refuses as already answered shows the refusal and loads the stored answer. Before, only the browser that submitted knew the answer, so a reload elsewhere showed an answered form as open, and a lost follow-up left an answered form with nothing running. A development client that reads the view must expect the new field; nothing stored needs recreating.
- **A point-buy error appears under its attribute.** `tabletop-rules` returns an out-of-range attribute as `{ field, message }`, so the form shows it under that attribute instead of at the top; the over- or under-spent budget stays a form-level message.
- **PostgreSQL returns a stored string that spells JSON as that string.** Plugin data, state entries and changes, event payloads, message metadata, trace payloads, job status data and runtime export values holding `"30"`, `"true"`, `"null"`, `"[1]"` or `"{\"a\":1}"` came back parsed (the number 30, and so on) on PostgreSQL only. The PostgreSQL schema now reads a `jsonb` value once, and the shared store contract checks these strings on every backend. Stored rows are unchanged: they were written correctly and now read back as written.
- **A slash command typed right after a session opens is not sent to the story.** The command list is read after the session opens. A command entered before it arrived matched no command and went to the narrator as a player turn, with a model call.
