A world that keeps the file at another path names that path in its own descriptor. Every bundled plugin passes.
- **Model data refreshed.** The bundled LiteLLM snapshot is pinned to `be448177` (2026-10-05, 4,472 models).

### Removed

- **`COVEL_LLM_REPLAY` and `COVEL_LLM_REPLAY_DIR`.** They were registered as planned and no code read them. Recording and replaying model calls is done by `pnpm llm:replay`, outside the server process. The `documented` status of the environment registry, which only these two used, is gone with them.

### Fixed

- **A story cut at the model's output limit stays.** A reply that ends at the output limit is an error for a tool call or JSON, which cannot be used cut. A story runtime that wrote only prose now keeps it: the text is committed, and the player reads a notice that it may stop mid-sentence. Before, the whole turn failed and the text the player had read was removed.
- **`WORLD.md` and the size warnings of `validate:world` are measured in tokens.** The story prompt carries up to about 8,000 estimated tokens of lore and cuts longer lore after the last line that fits. The limit was 8,000 characters, which cut the English editions of `haruka-academy` and `mistport` to less than half while their Chinese originals fit. The warnings for constant lorebook entries (2,000) and initial dimension values (8,000) use the same measure.
- **A lorebook key matches its plural and possessive forms.** `lantern` matches `lanterns` and `lantern's`, and still not `lanternfish`. Whole-word matching had stopped matching the plural.
- **Claude requests keep the tool choice the player set.** For the models that reject a forced tool choice (Claude Fable 5.1, Mythos 5.1, Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5) only `any` and `tool` become `auto`; `none` and `auto` are sent as set. A conversation whose kept history starts with the model's turn gets a user turn first, which the Messages API requires. The per-model rules are in one place and follow Anthropic's API reference; they were not run against the live endpoint.
- **A fallback model that is too small is skipped, and the error names the first failure.** The error of a failed call was the note that the backup could not fit the request; it is now the failure of the model that was tried, followed by that note.
- **A private browser session no longer grows toward the upload limit.** A checkpoint still carried about 0.6 MB per turn of execution journals and reached the 64 MB limit near turn 100. It now carries the results and runtime outputs of the latest 40 executions and the three trace rows the execution status is read from; the tool-call log and the event trail stay on the server. Messages, the prompt history and game state are complete.
- **A plugin whose task cannot be loaded says so when it is approved.** The server loads a plugin's approved runtimes as soon as the player allows its code and returns the ones that fail (`runtimeLoadErrors`); the app shows them. Before, the failure appeared at the first turn that scheduled the task.
- **The desktop app clears the storage of earlier page origins.** An install from before the port was kept had one origin per launch, each with its own media cache.
- World revision results now refuse publication after concurrent edits, deletion or replacement in server and browser-private storage. Browser-local revisions remain return-only even when the server holds a mirror, and initial browser generation refuses occupied world IDs. Package replacement preserves the old package for recovery if both publication and rollback fail; an incomplete generation stream is rejected.
- World-data sources can declare `localeArrayKeys` for nested translation identities; translation extraction preserves the selected identity field. Bundled memory-block translations follow their labels when reordered, and Mistport's English portraits inherit current media references from the main file. Manual handler test contexts accept the complete optional host capabilities.
- Whole-settings edits persist additions, changes and removals atomically and reject a stale editing base. Late configuration-file reads cannot replace the selected file. Settings backups avoid same-millisecond collisions, hydration rejects misplaced secrets before making repair copies, and provider names that match inherited object keys survive secret reloads.
- Hosted configuration discovery omits operator-only paths and diagnostics from public responses. Unknown configuration failures use the global error boundary; malformed archives and refused character proposals retain input-error responses, and required state reads no longer return incomplete success.
- Media cleanup holds SQLite's write lock until content removal completes and refuses physical deletion inside an outer database transaction; metadata writes share the NUL normalization contract across all media backends. Literal outbound IP addresses use the same public-address policy as resolved hosts, including reserved and transitional IPv6 ranges, and text-model parameter overrides no longer leak into image API requests.
- Runtime exports use individual savepoints, so a failed diagnostic export cannot poison a PostgreSQL domain commit. Malformed optional-runtime domain effects are isolated from successful story commits. Long cooldowns allow a runtime's first execution, optional incompatible inputs are omitted, and input schema/pointer checks no longer reject valid branched patterns or accept non-canonical array indices.
- Repaired community packages that failed at startup can be rediscovered in development without executing unapproved code. Event schema validation follows the published plugin generation across hot reload.
- Character-sheet prompts filter internal bookkeeping fields and use compact JSON. Runtime input bindings, template paths, locale objects and test-runtime reports preserve explicit data keys without consulting or modifying inherited object properties. Test-runtime image exports allocate distinct filenames when record keys sanitize to the same name.
- State responses preserve explicit plugin-data and character-schema keys such as `__proto__`. World authoring streams hide unknown internal failures while retaining conflict, validation and package-recovery diagnostics.
- Invalid persisted desktop window dimensions and flags fall back to safe defaults; valid secondary-display coordinates still restore normally.
- Allocate PostgreSQL log positions atomically across connections, reject plugin-data batch CAS for missing sessions on every backend, and retain background-worker wake hints received during maintenance. Unexpected RPC and installation failures reach the global error handler without exposing production exception details.
- **Embedding poison recovery retains actual HTTP error codes.** A rejected input reported by an OpenAI-compatible endpoint no longer blocks later vector rows indefinitely. Truncated history summaries preserve the original history, and compaction verifies the canonical prefix before hiding source messages. Prompt admission reads only the latest form and a bounded oldest/recent plugin-data window, lore scanning includes the canonical recent tail, and losing a diagnostic trace reference leaves state replay intact.
- Queued actions recheck session ownership, deletion state and incarnation after acquiring their lock, and retain the corresponding machine-readable error code in SSE.
- Keep retry results durable after diagnostic trace cleanup, query execution records directly, share background job scans, and prevent diagnostic events from filling subscription replay buffers or being stored twice.
- Validate world presets, settings and data-source combinations; report unused nested files. Move free dimension editing into Debug and show settlement recovery during play. Price cache reads/writes when rates are known.
- World import previews validate direct and derived characters against the world schema and reject malformed or untriggerable lorebook records before any writes, with per-record locations and correction hints.
- Expose locale resolution, prompt loading, UI tool factories and dimension-provider helpers in the public plugin SDK; bundled plugin runtime code no longer imports host internals.
- Keep in-progress turn streams owned by their original action; remove uncommitted assistant output, deduplicate steering messages, and preserve same-timestamp message order after reload. Confirmation buttons and stage input respect focus and IME composition.
- Isolate plugin prompt/runtime loading failures; validate package file references, tool names, stages and contract dependencies consistently before installation. Preserve editor changes and deletion state when world translations finish.
- Reject truncated agent/world-generation output, preserve provider failure details, use streaming for all supported agent calls, and check the input budget before falling back to a smaller model. Anthropic caches transcript/tool history without moving late instructions; other protocols remove cache markers. Media submissions no longer retry automatically at the HTTP layer.
- Load localized WORLD.md into story context, preserve edited default lore, and use generic dimensions without reserved gameplay IDs. World generation resolves occupied IDs early and includes rejected answers in repair requests.
- Isolate invalid embedding inputs, improve CJK keyword recall and matching excerpts, and use filtered exact vector search with consistent L2 distance. Keep backend NUL handling, fractional lore ordering and deterministic text ordering consistent.
- Keep mobile panel state across drawer openings, expose panel retry and runtime skip reasons, debounce and bound execution display caches, and unmount folded historical cards. Detect model slots without usable credentials before presenting them as configured.
- Enforce desktop single-instance startup, preserve navigation during sidecar recovery, and route desktop imports through the shared validated installer. Hold SSE single-flight guards until asynchronous work finishes; background lock waits remain cancellable.
- Bound resume schema compilation caches, compact terminal dimension receipts, and avoid full hook prompt copies in rewrite traces. The guide runtime uses a short history window with persisted summaries.
- **The world generator writes no file outside the package.** A contract's records go to the path its plugin names (`contributes.data.<ns>.authoring.source.path`), and that path was joined to the package directory unchecked: `../escaped.json` passed manifest validation and was written next to the package. The manifest schema now takes only a world-package-relative path (no leading `/`, no `..` segment), and the writer refuses any path that leaves the package.
- **Two contracts that name one file no longer lose a contract's records in a generated world.** The second contract's records replaced the first's, and no descriptor was written, while the importer reads a path that two contracts name for neither. The generator is now given a plugin's path only while it is a convention (no other contract of an installed plugin names it), and the writer gives a contract its own `data/contract-<n>.json` when its path is taken by another source or is one of the package's own files (`world.yaml`, `WORLD.md`, the kernel's data files); `data/world.data.yaml` then lists every source.
- **A resumed runtime keeps the whole turn under the context budget.** A runtime that runs after the story protects two user messages of the current turn from the budget: the player's and the cue after the story. Only an ordinary run did; a resume protected one, so with a large resume payload the request went out without this turn's story and turn context, and the runtime still returned success. The count is now part of the suspension's continuation (`currentTurnUserMessages`) and the tool loop applies it on every call. A player's interjection during a turn is counted the same way. A suspension saved before this change resumes as it did.
- **The tracker is shown no text limit below `minLength`.** The dimension tracker reads 80% of every `maxLength`; a schema with `minLength: 4, maxLength: 4` was shown as `minLength: 4, maxLength: 3`, which no text meets. The shown limit is now at least `minLength`, and a string with `enum` or `const` keeps the author's limit.
- **A retried provider request is sent at most four times.** Retries after an HTTP 429/5xx and after a dropped connection share one limit of three, but a retry that a response asked for was counted only after it returned: HTTP 500 followed by a connection that stayed down made five requests. It is now counted before the request is sent again.
- **MemoryStore orders plugin-data keys and character IDs as the SQL backends do, byte by byte.** It compared UTF-16 code units, which put a character above U+FFFF (an emoji) before U+E000–U+FFFF (a fullwidth letter); SQLite and PostgreSQL order them the other way. With keys `Ａ` and `🐉` written in one millisecond the two backends listed them in opposite order. The contract suite now checks this on every backend.
- **The dev server no longer starts on a plugin SDK build that is older than its source.** `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils` is the one workspace package read from `dist/`, and only `pnpm install` built it. After a pull that added an SDK export, the next server start logged `narrator: failed to activate entry … does not provide an export named 'characterSheetSegments'` (and the same for `chat-mode-narrator`), kept listening and answered requests without those plugins. The server's `dev` and `dev:pg` scripts now preload `scripts/ensure-plugin-sdk.mjs`, which runs at every start of the server process, the watcher's restarts included: when a file under the SDK's `src/` or its build config is newer than `dist/index.js`, it runs the package's build first. A change to the SDK source now restarts the server. A type error does not stop the start (the compiler reports it and still writes its output, as the rest of the workspace runs unchecked from source); a build that writes nothing stops the process with the command to run.
- **Opening the address of a session that no longer exists shows no error.** The page went to the world list, as intended, but also showed the raw response (`404 /api/sessions/<id>` and its JSON) as an error. Seen when a browser tab still pointed at a session after the database was removed. For this request a 404 is the answer "no such session"; any other failure is still shown.
- **A world-data sync no longer stops at the first conflict.** `POST /api/worlds/:id/sync-data` (and `sync-dimensions`) wrote nothing when any row was in conflict, and still answered `ok: true` with the count of rows it would have written. A session that was played always has a conflict: its dimensions evolved, and `force` does not override those. So a session never received what its world package gained after it was created, such as portraits: its character portrait tab said the world provided none. A row in conflict is now left as it is and listed in `conflicts`; every other row is written, updated or removed, and `upserted` / `deleted` count what was done. Checked on a copy of a database with two sessions created before their worlds' portraits were imported under `character-blueprint`: after one sync each had its portraits (7 and 6), with 13 and 10 evolved rows kept.
- **The image generation helper draws the random part of an image ID from `ctx.random`**, not from `Math.random`.
- **MemoryStore keeps a rewritten plugin-data row's `id` and `createdAt`.** SQLite and PostgreSQL change the value and `updatedAt` only; MemoryStore replaced the whole row, so `createdAt` moved to the last write. The contract suite now checks all three.
- **A story runtime is no longer told to finish with `runtime-done`.** The framework's `[COMPLETION]` line told every runtime without an output schema to call `runtime-done`, and the tool was in the narrator's tool list. A model that follows instructions to the letter called it and wrote no story: on a GPT-6 model 6 of 33 story runs failed, and the others needed about three model calls each. A story runtime now reads that its result is the story text of its reply, and `runtime-done` is not in its list; in two sessions of 18 story runs none failed, with under two model calls each. A model that still calls it is asked for the story, as before.
- **A refused or dropped connection is retried.** The transport retried a 429 or 5xx response and nothing else, so one moment in which an endpoint was restarting failed the runtime call at once. A connection that was refused or closed before it answered is now sent again with the same backoff and the same limit (three retries in all). A timeout is not retried there.
- **A reasoning level reaches GPT-6 models.** A `reasoningEffort` set on a slot was dropped without a word for a model named `gpt-6…`: the name was taken for the OpenAI family but for no known model of it. GPT-6 and later are known by name, with the levels `none` to `xhigh` and `max`.
- **An empty `upsert-npc-graph` call means nothing changed.** The tool refused a call with no node and no edge and told the model to call `runtime-done`; the model did, and the runtime failed all the same, because `runtime-done` does not settle a failed call. The call now succeeds and writes nothing. The same holds for a call that held only places and items.
- **Three more slips of the story planner no longer cost another model call.** A plan without `reason` is taken: the reason is a note for debugging that nothing reads. An event planned under the ID of an event that already fired is what follows that event, and it gets the next free ID (`meg-offer-2`); a fired event is still never planned again. A text among the events (`"reason ="` after the last event, from a broken end) is left out when the call holds an event.
- **The story planner's calls are rejected far less often.** In replays of 15 recorded turns `plan-story-events` rejected 18 of 90 calls on qwen3.8-flash and 23 of 90 on deepseek-flash; with this change 5 and 3. Its prompt now gives the four forms of a condition and says that only story events have IDs for `revealed`: the models had taken things that happened in the narrative, and entries of World IR, for events. The tool settles what has one meaning (a `turnsSince*` bound written as an entry of its own, a condition written on the event, `none` inside `all`, a field given as `null`, `notExists`, an operator and its value as two keys, `dimension: "worldTime"` on a world-time condition, a path where the dimension ID goes, a number comparison on an object with one number field) and leaves out an "unless that event fired" guard on an event that does not exist. It now also refuses a condition that the schema of a dimension can never meet (a field that is not there, a number comparison on text, a value outside an enum), with the fields that can be used: until now such an event was accepted and waited forever in one of the eight places for planned events.
- **Three more slips in a model's tool arguments no longer cost another model call.** A closing bracket that does not match the open one is left out, or at the very end replaced by the right one (`[{"id":"a"},{"id":"b"}}]`). The opening of an object written twice is written once (`{"{"id": …`). In an array or object sent as text inside a field, brackets still open at the end are closed: the arguments around it parsed, so the model ended the text itself. Each is accepted only when the text then parses with no other change. On qwen3.8-flash, 36 of 1,695 replayed calls had broken JSON, 35 of them inside a field; 4 were usable before and 20 are now.
- **A tool whose schema has a Unicode-property pattern no longer fails on APIs that check schemas.** `upsert-npc-graph` declared its relation label with `\p{Letter}`; an OpenAI-style API answered every request of the NPC graph extractor with HTTP 400 ("is not a 'regex'"). `tool()` now leaves such a `pattern` out of the JSON Schema it sends, and Zod still checks the value. The relation rule is checked in the tool and stated in the field's description.
- **`submit-world-facts` settles three more slips.** A relation, event, or statement without an `id` gets one from its content. A reference with the letters of exactly one session character's handle means that character (`雷恩-修女` for `雷恩修女`, or the name itself). A statement subject that names an event of the same output is left out: subjects are entities. Over 627 replayed calls on qwen3.8-flash in Chinese, 107 were rejected before and 60 are now, and no call that was accepted is rejected. The id examples of its prompt no longer name an item and a person of a bundled world.
- **Agents that run after the story no longer answer the previous turn** ([ackness/covel#139](https://github.com/ackness/covel/issues/139)). The conversation sent to a `post-turn` or `audit` agent stopped at the previous narrative and the new player message: the committed history ends one turn back, and the story just written was only in a JSON block of the system prompt, ahead of the whole history. As the history grew, the model took the end of the conversation for the present. Action suggestions then recapped the previous turn and offered what the player had just done. The kernel now adds the story text of the running execution to the conversation, as the next turn's history will show it, so every such agent reads the turn it is working on. A retried runtime already read it from the committed history; both paths now send the same conversation.
- **Action suggestions do not repeat what just happened.** `guide` reads the last story reply as the present moment, writes its recap up to that point, and does not offer an action the narrative completed or a question it answered.
- **Three slips in a model's tool arguments no longer cost another model call.** They are settled in the whole argument string and in an array or object sent as text inside a field. Closing brackets after complete arguments (`{"updates":[…]}]}`) are dropped; until now this was done only for text inside a field, and the same slip in the whole argument string failed with `INVALID_ARGS`. Quote marks that a model writes bare around a word or a line of speech inside a string value (`"note": "The page calls it a "lock" and not a lamp."`) are escaped, only where they come in pairs and never in a key. An array element left open where the next one starts (`[{"id":"a","attributes":{…}, {"id":"b"}]`) is closed, only when the text then parses with no other change. Every other fault is reported as before: an array that was not closed, a key that was left out, output that stopped early, a missing comma. See `docs/v2/reference/tools.md`.
- **The NPC graph no longer takes places and items for people.** The extractor's prompt said that the `entities` of its input are people, groups, and factions. They also hold places, items, skills, and concepts, and the model wrote those as nodes: about a third of all nodes in replays of recorded turns, most of them labelled `group` or `individual` to fit the tool. The prompt now says what the entities are and that only people, groups, and factions are nodes. `upsert-npc-graph` reads the `character` type as `individual`, leaves out a node typed `location`, `item`, `skill`, or `concept` instead of rejecting the call, and skips an edge to it. Nodes of that kind that an earlier turn recorded stay in a session's graph.
- **`submit-world-facts` accepts two more forms of reference.** A reference that holds the name of an entity of the same output, where its id goes, means that entity when no other entity has the name. A reference to an item or quest that the session already tracks (a name of the `world-ir.vocabulary@1` input) is declared from the vocabulary, as a session character is declared from the roster. On qwen3.8-flash these were 3 of the 7 recorded rejections for an undeclared reference.

### Breaking changes and upgrade notes for v0.0.48

- **This version does not read the data of an earlier version, and does not migrate it.** Sessions saved by an earlier version are lost. Covel is in early development and keeps no migration code for stored data.
- Server database: `messages`, `trace_events` and `turn_results` have new columns. An earlier SQLite database opens, and nothing reports a problem at startup; then every chat read and write fails with `no such column: seq`. Delete the database file and its `-wal` and `-shm` files before starting this version: `<covelHome>/data/covel.db` (typically `~/.covel/data/covel.db`) for the desktop app, `./data/covel.db` for a development server without a Covel home. For PostgreSQL, recreate the schema.
- Browser-private saves: BrowserVault is schema 6 and refuses an earlier vault. Delete the site's IndexedDB database `covel-browser-vault`.
- `history.compact@1` is `history.compact@2`: the output is a list of summary segments, each with the summaries it replaces. A compaction provider written for `@1` is not called.
- Prompt templates: `{{ world.tone }}`, `{{ world.openingScenario }}` and `{{ session.id }}` are removed. Read the tone and the opening from `<world-lore>` and the dimension projection; name a turn by its number.
- Hooks: a handler that returns nothing continues. A handler that throws or times out vetoes only on `TurnStart`, `PreCompaction`, `PreRuntime`, `PreToolUse` and `PreStateCommit`; on every other event it is skipped.
- Plugin packages: the loader rejects a runtime whose declared schema file is missing. `pnpm validate:plugin` and the installer also reject a referenced file that does not exist, an `auto` or `scheduled` runtime without a stage, an unknown framework tool name, and a plugin tool that `contributes.tools` does not list.
- World packages: the import preview and `pnpm validate:world` reject a selective lorebook entry without a key, a lorebook record without `content`, and a character that does not fit `characterSchema`. `pnpm validate:world` also rejects an unknown `presetId` and an invalid plugin setting.
- `create-character` gives a word ID (`char-lin-yao`) in place of a UUID.
- `agent.loop.callTimeoutMs` limits a call that is not streamed. Every agent call streams when the model adapter can; such a call is limited by `firstTokenTimeoutMs` and `idleTimeoutMs`.
- Desktop: the IPC channels `covel:import:pick-plugin` and `covel:import:pick-world` are removed. The import menus open the installer in Settings.
- Plugin and world authors who upgrade from before v0.0.47 also read that release's [upgrade notes](#upgrade-notes-for-v0047).
- macOS Apple Silicon and Windows x64 artifacts remain unsigned, and macOS artifacts are not notarized. First launch may show Gatekeeper or SmartScreen warnings.

## [0.0.47] - 2026-10-05

This release adds world galleries, scene- and mood-based background music, and author credits, and fixes world storage selection, background model readiness, runtime scheduling, and turn settlement.

### Added

- **World gallery.** The art a world package ships shows before a session exists. In the `showcase` world list the selected world's scenes rotate behind its title — with a pause control, and without starting for a player who asked for reduced motion — and its portraits sit under the summary; a picture opens at full size. The world details list every picture. A world without a built-in cover opens on its own scenes. A package says what its gallery holds in `media/gallery.json`: the pictures, their order, what each is (`hero`, `map`, `scene`, `still`, `portrait`), and a name and an introduction in every language of the package; the gallery shows them with the picture, up to the opening. A package without that file shows the raster images of its public `kind: media` sources. New routes: `GET /api/worlds/:id/gallery` and `GET /api/worlds/:id/gallery/:source/:file`, which serve only listed files.
- **World art on the cards.** In the `cards` world list a cover shows a few faces of the world and the number of its pictures; they open the gallery. The world's scenes pass over the cover while the pointer rests on it. The "continue" banner rotates the scenes of the world and lists its pictures.
- **Background music.** A world can ship music: files under `media/music/` and a track list `media/music.yaml` that says when each track plays — in which scenes, for which moods, or as the theme of the world. The moods are the author's own words: the track list names them and says when each applies, and the narrative is told that list. The new bundled `soundtrack` plugin records the mood (the new `music.cue` event, emitted by the narrative) and the scene (`scene.set`), and chooses the track. The kernel UI slot `stage.music@1` carries the choice to the app, which plays it: tracks change with a crossfade, the music lowers itself under a narrated line, pauses while the page is hidden, and has a switch in the session header and a volume in Settings → General. Other plugins can provide the same slot. See `docs/v2/reference/world-data.md` (Background Music) and `docs/v2/reference/ui-panels.md` (Stage music).
- **Theme music on the world list.** `world.yaml` takes `themeMusic`: an `.mp3` or `.wav` file under `media/`. The world list (in the `showcase` arrangement) and the world details play it for the world they show, with the same switch and volume as session music. No bundled world ships one yet.
- **Music generation API.** Function runtimes get `ctx.music.generate()` and `ctx.music.isAvailable()`, the gateway gets `composeMusic`, model slots get the `music` tag, and a plugin registers a provider's format with `covel.registerWires({ music })`. A music request resolves the `music` role and never falls back to a speech or a text slot. The framework ships no music wire and no plugin uses the API yet: it is the place a provider plugs into.
- **A world list of any length.** The strip of covers in the `showcase` world list stays one row: what does not fit scrolls with a faded edge, steps to either side, and the arrow keys; the selected world stays in view; "All worlds" opens a searchable grid.
- Lantern Barrow now ships six full-body character portraits and a dedicated world-selection cover. Emberback adds portraits for Priya Nair and Eli Varga. The four active worlds include a first gallery asset collection with localized introductions, setting background, source references and clearly identified illustrative scenes.
- Gallery descriptions now also cover 35 reviewed existing portraits, expression and outfit variants, day/night scenes and world covers. Each includes Chinese and English descriptions grounded in the original setting, with visual observations separated from story facts; the existing image files are preserved.
- **Credits for plugins, worlds and collections.** `PLUGIN.md`, `world.yaml` and `covel-collection.yaml` take the same optional fields: `author` (`name`, `url`, a short `about` message, and up to six labelled `links`), `license` and `homepage`. World cards show the author and version; the world page, the session preparation screen and the installed-plugin list show the full credits, and an install preview shows the author's name. The play view does not show them. `about` and link labels are translated like other labels (`locales/<locale>.yaml`, `world.<locale>.yaml`); the author's name and the license are shown as written. Fields and limits: `docs/v2/reference/plugins.md`.
- **A warning before a third-party link opens.** Links in credits are `https` only. A click shows the full address and its host and says the link comes from the package author, not from Covel; it opens after the player confirms.
- **Versions on world cards and bundled plugins.** A world's `version` now reaches its cards (`metadata.packageInfo`), and every bundled plugin states its `version`, `author` and `license`. `pnpm validate:plugin` fails when a plugin's `PLUGIN.md` and `package.json` state different versions.

### Changed

- **Stage is the default appearance for new profiles.** First launch uses the scene-based Stage layout and world showcase. Saved appearance choices are preserved.

- **Panel tabs that do not fit.** With labelled tabs (`panelTabs: "bar"`) the context panel no longer shows a scrollbar under a cut-off row. The cut-off side fades, the mouse wheel moves the row, the selected tab stays in the middle, and a button at the end lists every panel with its full name.
- **Existing sessions run plugin setup once more.** The bundled plugins had no `version` and counted as `0.0.0`; setup completion is recorded per version, so each setup runtime runs again on the next turn of an existing session and skips work that is done. `pregame` no longer repeats its welcome in a session that is already playing. A session whose world changed its dimension definitions after the session began can fail this rerun; use the setup recovery control in the plugin panel, or start a new session.
- `GithubPluginPreview` and the collection preview carry `author` (a name or `null`).
- Losslessly compress bundled world art, documentation screenshots and application icons. Image dimensions, decoded pixels and transparency are preserved, and world media hashes and byte counts are refreshed. Already compact assets remain unchanged when re-encoding offers no meaningful saving.

### Fixed

- **Partial regional plugin translations keep available language translations.** Plugin text written into session state and model context now resolves each message by exact locale, then the same language and script, then its English source. A partial regional catalog no longer hides the rest of its language catalog.

- **Selected panel tabs stay visible after layout changes.** The active label can grow as its font weight changes, and the panel menu or a late font can resize the strip after the initial scroll. The tab strip now follows those size changes so the selected tab is not clipped.

- **Switching gameplay packs replaces the previous pack's requests.** Selecting the traditional pack after the dialogue pack kept the dialogue narrator enabled, and selecting the smaller pack kept optional plugins from the previous pack. Pack requests now have their own source, retained through manual edits; switching replaces that source while preserving other defaults, manual additions and explicit exclusions. A default that the world's initial pack requests belongs to that pack even when the world's preferred tags also select it, so another pack can replace the world's default narrative engine.
- **Synchronous event subscribers honor their dependencies.** A legal subscriber with `needs` or a required input from another subscriber in the same fan-out batch was skipped because the batch ran in parallel. Matching synchronous subscribers now run in DAG levels with results merged between levels. Cycles are skipped while independent branches continue; background jobs remain independent.
- **Late background handoffs keep the job's latest status.** A delayed queued status or `runtime.deferred` announcement could replace a completed background row with a waiting row. The client now checks the durable job's control sequence and keeps its terminal state during live updates and trace recovery, while allowing a new job to start and a plugin's reported success to be corrected after a failed commit. The row keeps the handoff's start time whichever event arrives first.

- **World generation waits for its save destination.** Starting generation before server storage discovery finished could save a browser-private world to the server's world directory. Generation now resolves storage before contacting the model, reports discovery failures for retry, and respects cancellation while waiting. Regenerating a world with the same ID replaces its list entry instead of adding a duplicate card. Browser tests use a temporary user profile and assert that browser-private generation stays in the browser.
- **Detached jobs were judged by the default preset, not the slot they call.** A detached function runtime with no declared model slot (the story-memory `extract` runtime) had its credential readiness resolved against the system default preset, which ignores request-level UI slot bindings. On a desktop setup whose models are configured only in Settings (no `llm.toml`), the check never passed: the job sat queued for its full `maxQueueMs` (5 minutes), died as `queue-deadline-exceeded`, and every following turn stalled for the 30-second settle wait. Function runtimes can now declare `function.model` (the slot their `ctx.gateway` calls use); `memory/extract` declares `model: memory`.
- **Dimension initialization obeys the commit policy.** With a committed story, an optional runtime's `dimension.initialize` was written before `PreStateCommit` hooks and outside its own savepoint, so a vetoed or rewritten initialization still left the value first proposed. Initialization now has one write path, its own proposal, and settlement receipts are built from the records that did commit.
- **A malformed UI part no longer discards the turn.** An optional runtime that returned `effects.ui` with a non-object part made commit preparation throw and rolled the story back with it. Such an entry (also in `effects.interactions` and `effects.notifications`) is now a rejected write of that runtime alone; a function runtime fails with `output-schema-invalid` when its handler returns one.
- **A resumed runtime keeps its turn.** After suspend and resume, tools and function handlers lost `ctx.locale`, `ctx.logicalTurn` and plugin translations, and agent tools lost `ctx.world`, so the built-in dimension tools failed. The continuation now carries the locale and clock of the suspended execution, and a resumed agent reads the World Model like any other run.
- **A failed tool is never the required work.** An agent with `completion.require: tool-use` could finish without doing the work after a business tool had failed: by calling `runtime-done`, by reaching its step limit or being stopped by a `PostToolUse` hook while its reply carried a valid output, or, after suspend and resume, with a text answer. The contract is now judged where the loop returns, so every way out asks for a business tool that succeeded, in this run or before the suspension.
- **`ctx.music` reaches function handlers.** The runner built the music capability and did not pass it on.
- **Disabling the plugin that holds a session's dimensions is refused.** It used to succeed and then leave a browser-private session unable to save or restore its checkpoint. Enable and disable now answer `409 dimension_provider_required` when the change would drop the bound provider.
- **A deleted session receives no late job status.** The background worker could write an old job's status into a session deleted, or re-created under the same id, in the meantime — when it claimed a job as well as when it reconciled finished ones. The status row is now written in one transaction with the check that the job and the session still exist.
- **A fork that cannot keep its dimension provider is refused.** Forking a session whose dimensions belong to a community plugin produced a child that could not be opened or saved, since a fork does not carry the plugin's approval. The fork now answers `409 dimension_provider_required` while the snapshot holds dimension data, and creates an unbound child when it holds none.
- **A pending settlement protects dimensions initialized in play.** `sync-dimensions` could redefine such a dimension, which has no import ledger row, while a narrative was still waiting to be settled.
- **Hidden story events: `not` on an unavailable reference.** A condition such as `not: { time: phase, equals: 3 }` held in a session without world time, revealing the event early. A leaf that cannot be judged now stays undecided through `not`; `any` and `all` still use their decidable branches. Localized event text follows the shared rule: the session's locale, the same language and script, then English as `en-US` reads it.
- **An optional plugin that is not installed no longer blocks its consumers.** A runtime with an optional input that accepts a contract (`accepts: "contract:world-ir@1"`) failed to load, and every turn with it, when no installed package published that contract. The runtime now loads and the unchecked value is withheld.
- **One plugin folder in a retired layout no longer stops the server.** The desktop folder import accepted a package whose runtime manifests were still named `PLUGIN.md`, and discovery then failed for every plugin. The import refuses that layout, and discovery reports such a package as its own load error.
- **Writes from another site are refused.** A browser's "simple" cross-site request could reach a write route of a local server, since CORS only withholds the response. `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` requests with an `Origin` that is neither allow-listed nor the server's own host now get `403 origin_not_allowed`.
- **A setup runtime that pauses now completes when it is resumed.** A setup runtime that suspended for the player's input and reported `completion: done` after the resume committed its writes, but its setup record stayed `pending` and the session stayed in the setup phase, so the next action ran the setup again. The commit that resolves the suspension now records the completion and, when it was the last setup runtime, moves the session to `playing`; the pause and its resume count as one attempt. No bundled plugin pauses during setup.
- **A satisfied setup dependency is no longer reported as a cycle.** With `needs` of `scope: session` on a contract, a setup runtime whose need was already met by a completed provider was marked `blocked` (`setup-session-cycle`) when another provider of the same contract waited on it. The check now reads completed providers, and a `cardinality: one` need is released by any provider that can complete; only runtimes that no order can release are blocked. No bundled plugin declares such a need.
- **A turn without its story is not committed.** When the plugin that provides dimensions failed in a turn, the narrative was skipped but the turn still committed: the writes of the runtimes before it were kept (a story event could be marked as revealed and never told) and the turn was counted. Such a turn now fails as a whole, like a failed story, and the player can retry the action. The bundled dimension provider does not fail this way.

### Removed

- Unused code with no caller: the fixed world-detail sections of the web app, the earlier server-restart probe (`ensureServerSession`), the whole-table provider-key and `messages/sync` wrappers of the web API client, the earlier portrait replace helper, and the desktop copy of the provider-key helpers (it now uses `@covel/shared`). `createSlotRegistry()` no longer takes the preset registry it never read.

### Upgrade notes for v0.0.47

- Bundled plugins now declare versions. Existing sessions run their setup checks again on the next turn and skip completed work. If the world changed its dimension definitions, use the setup recovery control in the plugin panel or start a new session.
- World theme music requires a package-provided audio file; no bundled world includes one yet. Music generation exposes an API but ships no provider wire.
- When upgrading from before v0.0.46, also read that release's [breaking contracts and upgrade notes](#breaking-contracts-and-upgrade-notes).
- macOS Apple Silicon and Windows x64 artifacts remain unsigned, and macOS artifacts are not notarized. First launch may show Gatekeeper or SmartScreen warnings.

## [0.0.46] - 2026-10-04

This release adds style schemes that change the layout as well as the colours, puts each language of a world or plugin in its own file with tooling to translate it, writes an AI-generated world one part at a time under a limit on silence instead of total time, and keeps Settings usable when an upgrade meets older settings (#117–#121, #123–#127).

### Added

- **Configuration files can be edited in the app.** Settings → Configuration files shows the raw configuration as text: the settings of this device as JSON, `llm.toml`, and `config.toml` in the desktop app. A save is checked first — the server's own parser for the TOML files, the registered schema for each setting — and a text that fails is not written. The version before the change is kept (`llm.toml.bak`, `settings.edit.bak.json`), `llm.toml` is applied at once, and an earlier copy of the settings can be loaded into the editor. New routes: `GET /api/config/raw`, `GET` / `PUT /api/config/raw/:name`, behind the guard of the install endpoints.
- **Style schemes.** A theme package can now change structure as well as colour: it names a `layout` preset (`classic`, `book`, `stage`, `panel`) and may override where navigation sits, how the context panel shows its tabs, what the session does with world art, how the world list is arranged, and where a turn's results go. Three built-in schemes use it — Panel, Book and Stage — and Settings → Appearance has a style picker. The earlier themes are colourways of a fourth style, Classic, through the new `group` field. See `docs/v2/reference/theme-packages.md`.
- **One rendering for options.** The catalog gains `ChoiceList` and `Choice`. Kernel `choice` blocks, the reply variants of `CandidateList`, and the stage decision panel draw options the same way, so each scheme shows them one way: tagged cards, a numbered list, or key-capped rows. Number keys 1–9 pick the n-th option of the turn being played.
- **Session summary.** The kernel UI slot `session.summary@1` lets a plugin put one to three lines — text, a gauge, or a short list — where the active layout keeps status. The bundled world-time, core-quest and inventory plugins provide the time, the next open objective with its progress, and the carried items. `appendSummaryEntries` in `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils` builds the result.
- **Status at a glance.** The player's bounded `stats` show as gauges, unbounded ones as numbers, and `equipment` string lists as carried items: under the panel tabs, or on a scene HUD beside the cast when the layout shows the scene. The world list offers "continue" for each world's latest session.
- **Events from plugin UI.** `POST /api/sessions/:id/plugin-rpc` accepts `kind: "event"`, and catalog specs and widgets get an `emitEvent` action. A plugin's UI can emit the topics that plugin declares; the runtimes that subscribe run as background jobs. Community plugins are asked for the event grant and for each subscriber's runtime grant.
- **Plugin widgets in the new layouts.** A sandboxed `webview` receives the active scheme as `theme` and as `--covel-*` custom properties; a side panel can queue a line for the composer with `draftMessage`; and every plugin panel can be opened in a large dialog.
- **Clickable map example.** `tests/third-party/clickable-map` is an installable example of a self-drawn panel that travels on a click. `pnpm e2e:extensions` plays it in a real browser.
- **One language per file, for worlds and plugins.** A world's main files hold its own language; `<name>.<locale>.<ext>` beside a file holds only the translated text under the same keys and ids. A plugin's `PLUGIN.md`, `RUNTIME.md`, UI specs and handler code hold English; `locales/<locale>.yaml` translates manifest labels (one section per manifest file) and other text (`messages`, keyed by the English text). Handlers read text with `translate(ctx, "…")` and `labelText(ctx, "…")` from `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils`. Rules: `docs/v2/reference/i18n.md`.
- **Translation tooling.** `pnpm i18n` works on a plugin or a world directory: `status` counts what each language has and lists what it lacks, `extract` prints the missing entries, `translate` asks the configured model for what is missing or stale and checks every reply, `lock` records the English text each plugin label translation belongs to. A world can also be translated from its detail page ("Translate to …", `POST /api/worlds/:id/translate`).
- **Translations outside a package.** `$COVEL_HOME/translations/plugins/<id>/<locale>.yaml` (`COVEL_USER_TRANSLATIONS_DIR`) fills what a plugin's own locale file does not translate: labels and messages only, never a prompt.
- **Language tiers.** English and Simplified Chinese have their own instruction text; every other language gets interface and content with English instructions and is marked experimental in the language switchers. A plugin never fails for a language it lacks: `validate:plugin`, the plugin summary (`languages`) and the session prep row show what it has, and `POST /api/sessions` returns `pluginsWithoutLocale`.
- **Chinese edition of Emberback, full English edition of Haruka Academy.**
- **Revise a generated world in one sentence.** The world detail page takes a request such as "add a fourth character" for a world the generator made (`POST /api/ai/revise-world`). The model writes only what changes; the server merges it into the current world by key and by item id, and the result passes the same checks as a new world.
- **World content from plugin declarations.** A plugin that accepts world data says how to author it (`contributes.data.<namespace>.authoring`: title, hint, example, conventional source, and whether the generator offers it). The world creation dialog lists the offered content from `GET /api/framework/authoring`, and `pnpm describe:authoring` prints what a world may contain for the scanned plugins, with a ready descriptor entry and example for each contract.
- **Whole-package world validation.** `pnpm validate:world` runs the session-creation preflight: every record of every contract source is checked by the receiving plugin's schema, for each declared language, with plugin IDs, settings keys, lore files and locale files. It returns structured diagnostics; `--strict` turns warnings into errors.
- **Generated schema reference.** Every author-facing field of the world manifest, world data descriptor, dimension definitions, plugin manifest and runtime manifest has a description in its Zod schema. `pnpm schemas:generate` writes the JSON Schemas and `docs/v2/reference/schema/*.md`; `pnpm check` fails when a page is stale. The bundled `world.yaml` files name their schema, so editors offer completion.
- **Prompt gate.** `pnpm check:prompts` (in `pnpm check`) fails when a canonical prompt is not English, when a variant other than `*.zh.md` exists, when the two languages name different tools, blocks or inputs, or when the English text changed and the Chinese did not (`pnpm prompts:lock` records a pair). It also reports style findings against `docs/v2/plugins/prompt-style.md`.
- **World generation shows what it is writing.** The creation dialog and the revision panel list each part of the world — settings, lore, characters, lorebook, rules, and each kind of plugin content — as written, being written (with the number of characters received and the attempt), waiting, or not generated. `POST /api/ai/generate-world` and `revise-world` carry this in `parts` on their `progress` frames.
- **The wait for the model is a setting.** Settings → General has "World authoring: wait for the model" (15–1800 seconds, 120 by default), sent as `idleTimeoutMs` with world creation, revision and translation. The API accepts a wait from one second, so a test of the timeout path takes seconds. When a request ends because the model stayed silent, the error names the setting (`code: "model_idle_timeout"` in the error frame).
- **Browser test for AI world generation without a real model.** `tests/e2e/world-generation-progress.spec.ts` drives the creation dialog against a provider that runs in the test process (`tests/e2e/helpers/fake-llm-provider.ts`): the part list, a slow answer that outlasts the idle timeout, a model that stops answering, and a world that falls short of the brief. The four tests take about 14 seconds and run in `pnpm e2e:smoke`. See `docs/v2/internals/e2e-testing.md`.
- **Real-model harness reports.** `pnpm e2e:verify` takes `--locale`, counts rejected tool calls by runtime and tool, checks the language of what models wrote against the session language, and reports text of another script that was sent to a model.

### Changed

- **Settings dialog and its entry points follow the theme.** The navigation has an icon per page; General and each plugin page open with a title and one line of context, one setting per row. Controls use the theme's corner radius, rule colour and accent. A switch sits beside its text, and a number field is as wide as a number. On the world list, "AI Create World" and "Configure Providers & Models" are two rows in one frame, each with its full text. In the classic layouts the studio panel lists each model role with its model, and "Settings" and "New session" are plain rows with a gear in place of the key.
- **Earlier settings that this version cannot use no longer lock the settings.** One of two things made every setting read-only: a `settings.json` (desktop) or `covel:settings` entry (browser) in the v1 format, without a version or damaged; or one stored value that an upgrade no longer accepts, such as a removed option or a plugin setting with a new range. The app now keeps a copy first. An unusable file is moved aside (`settings.v1.bak.json`, `covel:settings.v1.bak`) and the settings start from their defaults. For single values, the file is copied (`settings.conflict.bak.json`) and only those values go back to their defaults. A notice names the settings to enter again and the copy, and Settings → Data shows and downloads the copies. API keys are not affected. A file from a later version is still left in place and read-only.
- **Model Roles page is shorter.** The page opens with one block (the active `llm.toml` and "Reload config"); the notes are folded. A role card no longer repeats the provider and model under the two pickers, and its two actions share a row. With more than six roles a filter box narrows the list. The model picker names the configured model "llm.toml default · …" where it said "Use default · …".
- **Plugin settings show what the current world sets.** For a setting you have not changed, the page shows the value from the world being prepared or played, and names the world. It showed the plugin's own default, which that world does not use.
- **Operator Access appears only where a token is checked.** `GET /api/health` reports `operatorTokenRequired`. Settings hides the page on a server that reports `false`, unless this browser holds a token.
- **Language lists name each language in that language** ("English", "Русский"), in Settings, the top bar and the getting-started guide.
- **The Data page says what each action covers.** Reset names what it clears: every setting and every saved API key on this device, with the providers, models and themes you added.
- **The world list has a "Settings" link** beside the guide link. "Configure Providers & Models" still opens the providers page.
- **Settings fields save when you leave them.** Text, number, slider and API key fields write on blur or Enter, not on each key press. A number outside its range becomes the nearest allowed value, and the range is shown. A setting you have changed shows "Use default"; for a plugin setting this is how it follows the world or the plugin again.
- **A new world is written one part at a time.** The manifest, the lore, each requested supplement and each plugin data contract are separate model requests; a later part is given the parts already written. A part that cannot be imported is asked for again alone, up to three times, instead of the whole package being generated again. A supplement that still fails is left out with a warning and the world is created without it; only the manifest and the lore are required. A revision stays one request.
- **World authoring is limited by silence, not by total time.** A request ends when the model sends no text and no reasoning for the idle timeout. A model that keeps writing is not cut off; one request may run for 30 minutes. A request that ended in silence is not sent again: generation stops, or finishes with the parts it has when only a supplement was missing, so a model that does not answer costs one wait and not three. `createWorld` takes `idleTimeoutMs` and `onProgress`; its `attemptTimeoutMs` option is removed.
- **Plugin scaffolds outside the repo have no install step.** `pnpm create-plugin <name>` no longer writes `tsconfig.json`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, a `preinstall` script, or a dev dependency on `@covel/plugin-handlers-utils`, which is a workspace package and is not on npm. Check a scaffolded plugin with `pnpm validate:plugin <dir>` and `pnpm test:runtime`. `--with-tools` plugins, which live in the repo workspace, keep `pnpm lint` and are now named `@covel/plugin-<name>` like the bundled plugins.
- **Issue and pull request templates follow the current workflow.** The PR template lists `pnpm check`, the database, UI, package and real-model checks, a "not verified" note, and the changelog; the bug report asks for the run mode, storage, world, community plugins and where to find logs.
- **Panel is the default look.** A browser with no saved appearance opens in the Panel scheme; a saved choice is kept.
- **Session toolbar.** The world, turn and current scene sit on the left; the text/stage switch, settings and the panel toggle on the right. Detailed and raw views, studio configuration, "back to setup" and traces moved into an overflow menu.
- **Turn results open for the latest turn** in the Panel and Stage schemes, and sit in the page margin in Book. Classic keeps them folded.
- **English is the instruction language; Simplified Chinese is the one variant.** The canonical `PLUGIN.md` / `RUNTIME.md` body is English and `*.zh.md` is read for Simplified Chinese sessions; every other session reads the English body and answers in its own language. `COVEL_INSTRUCTION_LOCALE` (`en` / `zh`) fixes the instruction language for all sessions. **Breaking:** `PLUGIN.en.md` and variants for other languages are no longer read; a plugin whose canonical body is Chinese must move it to `*.zh.md` and supply an English body.
- **A session's language is fixed when it is created** and must be an edition the world has; otherwise the session uses the world's own language and the response carries `requestedLocale`. **Breaking:** `POST /api/actions` rejects a request that carries `locale` with 400.
- **World packages hold one language per file.** **Breaking:** an inline locale map in `world.yaml`, a dimension file or another structured source is rejected (`inline-locale-map`); move every language but the default one into a locale file. A session stores dimension values in its own language, and `world-dimension-get` no longer takes `resolveI18n`. A session created earlier is converted when it is opened (see Fixed).
- **Plugin packages hold English, with translations in `locales/`.** `validate:plugin` rejects an inline locale map in a manifest or a UI spec. Built-in and plugin tool definitions are English.
- **A world needs no data descriptor.** Without `worldData` in `world.yaml`, files at well-known paths are the sources (`data/dimensions.yaml`, `data/lorebook.yaml`, `characters/characters.json`, and the path each plugin names for its data). A source's `schema` defaults to the one of its destination. The smallest world is `world.yaml` with five fields and `WORLD.md`. **Breaking:** recreate development sessions of Emberback, whose source IDs changed.
- **Plugin manifests repeat less.** A single-runtime package's runtime gets the tools the package contributes when it lists none, and a contract that a data namespace accepts takes its schema from that namespace.
- **Dice checks.** The narrative reports what it decided (action, attribute, modifier, difficulty and the outcome it wrote); the dice are taken in order and the DC, total and outcome are calculated by the plugin. A wrong outcome is sent back while the turn can still be corrected. **Breaking:** `check.resolved` items no longer carry `roll`, `dc` and `total`.
- **World generation.** A requested content kind that falls short of its target is kept and reported as a warning in place of regenerating the whole world, and a dimension that does not validate is dropped on its own. **Breaking:** `brief.content` no longer accepts `memory`; send `brief.contracts: ["memory.blocks@1"]`.
- **Imported characters are `npc-<name>`.** A blueprint without `instantiate.characterId` instantiates `npc-<blueprint id>`; `char-…` stays the player's prefix. Recreate development sessions of Emberback and Lantern Barrow.
- **Text limits fit every language.** The guide's recap, decision and prompt limits and the relationship graph's node summary were sized for Chinese and rejected most English calls.
- **A rejected tool call says what the tool accepts.** The story-event planner lists the event IDs, dimensions and time fields a condition can use; the character form lists every refused field and the attributes the world lets it collect; the clock names its units (`worldTime.value.units`); a select default names the option values; a text over a dimension's length limit is told how many characters to remove.
- **A streamed model call is limited by silence, not by total time.** A stream is ended when the model sends no text, no reasoning and no tool call for the idle timeout (`agent.loop.idleTimeoutMs`, 120 seconds by default); a model that keeps writing is not cut off, and the time it writes does not count against the runtime's `timeoutMs`. The wait for the first output (`firstTokenTimeoutMs`) is 120 seconds by default, up from 30. `callTimeoutMs` now limits only a model call that is not streamed. The gateway's default request budget follows the same rule: output of a stream moves its 120-second deadline, up to 30 minutes; a budget with an explicit `timeoutMs` or `deadline` stays fixed unless it also sets `idleTimeoutMs`.

### Fixed

- **Sessions created before dimension values held one language open again.** Such a session stored the world's locale maps in its dimension records; every read failed validation, so opening it returned HTTP 500 and the page showed the error again and again. The server now rewrites those records in the session's language the first time the session is used, and keeps the records as they were under `_legacy.dimension-locale-maps`.
- **A number setting can be retyped.** Each key press was validated and saved, so a half-typed value outside the range was refused and the old value came back: "World authoring: wait for the model" could not go from 120 to 300 by typing.
- **Errors show above an open dialog.** Toasts were drawn inside the app root, under every dialog, so a failed save in Settings showed nothing. They are drawn above dialogs now, and using a toast does not close the dialog.
- **"Expand turn updates" shows its description, and "Chat window message limit" has one.**
- **The getting-started guide always closes.** The guide stayed open until "finished" was saved. When the settings could not be loaded — a `settings.json` from before the v2 format, for example — every write is refused, so the guide showed "Could not save setting" and no button closed it. It now closes, shows the reason as a toast, and opens again on the next launch. The desktop log records a settings file that could not be read, and logged errors show their message and stack instead of `{}`.
- **Generated worlds no longer reuse the prompt's example dimension names.** The generation prompt showed one example with the IDs `setting`, `opening`, `repairBudget` and `discoveries`, and one numeric resource. The default model copied those IDs into most worlds, whatever the world was about, and wrote one resource where the opening kit asks for two, so those worlds came with the same warning. The example now has placeholder IDs and shows two resources.
- **AI world generation no longer fails at 120 seconds on a slow model (#122).** The generator passed no request budget to the model gateway, so the gateway's default 120-second deadline ended every attempt before the route's 150-second timeout applied. A world that took longer to write failed three times and showed "Generation failed" after six minutes, and no setting changed that. World revision and translation had the same limit.
- **"Edit llm.toml" works on a fresh desktop install.** The button failed with `"llm.toml" is not available` until the file had been created by hand, and the error appeared where it was easy to miss. The first click now creates `llm.toml` from the built-in default and opens it; failures show as a toast.
- **Settings no longer attributes the built-in default model to a file that does not exist.** Providers & models tagged the built-in DeepSeek default "From llm.toml" even with no `llm.toml` on disk. It is now tagged "Built-in default", and providers that come from the file or the built-in default explain why they have no delete button and how to remove them.
- **Local builds ship a production web client.** With `NODE_ENV=development` in the repo-root `.env` (the value in `.env.example`), `pnpm build` and `pnpm build:electron` bundled a development-mode web client: the router devtools were visible and React ran its development build. The web build now defaults to production; a `NODE_ENV` exported in the shell still takes precedence. CI and released installers were not affected.
- **A session no longer mixes languages.** An English session of a bilingual world sent about 3,400 Chinese characters to the model each turn (tool definitions, world name and summary, dimension labels, plugin data stored in two languages, the default calendar), and the fact-extraction agent was never told the session's language because its context hook dropped the framework's opening. All of these are corrected, and a system prompt that a hook replaces keeps the language directive.
- **Imported world rules reach the prompt with their titles** (`[World Rule: The Party Is Alive]`), where the model read the rule's ID or its first trigger word.
- **Field names that are language codes are not taken for translations.** `{ id: "torn-letter" }` was read as an Indonesian locale map and replaced by its value.
- **Fewer rejected tool calls with small models.** Array arguments sent as JSON text are explained when they do not parse, and closing brackets after a complete value are dropped; an inventory change lists its item when the model named it only there; the dimension tracker accepts a slash path, a `reason` on a change, and one dimension split over two entries; a zero duration is accepted in any time unit; `emit-event` says that the event is recorded and must not be sent again.
- **A turn's memory is not lost to one unreadable reply.** Memory extraction asks the model once more when the reply is not the JSON it asked for. A job reads only its own turn, so a failed job dropped that turn's facts.
- **`pnpm create-plugin` scaffolds follow the current prompt and label contracts** (English manifests and prompts, Chinese labels in `locales/zh.yaml`).
- **A slow model is no longer cut off in the middle of a turn.** Every model call of an agent runtime had a fixed 120-second budget, whatever the runtime declared, so a narrator with `callTimeoutMs: 120000` and `timeoutMs: 240000` was ended at 120 seconds with half its text shown, and a retry after a silent first attempt had no time left to run. One model call may now use the runtime's remaining time, and a stream that keeps writing is not ended.
- **`pnpm dev:electron` reads and saves settings without the dev server.** In dev mode the desktop main process sent settings and API keys to the Vite dev server as if it were its own sidecar. Where Vite listens on the IPv6 loopback only, the request to `127.0.0.1` was refused and each save logged `settings:save sidecar fallback`. Where the request arrived, the dev server, which runs without `COVEL_DESKTOP_REST`, answered an empty settings bundle and refused the write. Dev mode now reads and writes the files of its home directly, and a window reopened from the Dock loads `localhost`. A logged error also shows its cause, such as `ECONNREFUSED` behind `fetch failed`. Packaged builds are not affected.

### Removed

- **`docs/v2/internals/refactoring-plan.md`.** The current-only convergence it planned is finished; the resulting contract is described in `AGENTS.md`, `docs/v2/internals/flow.md` and `docs/v2/internals/storage.md`.
- **`.ui-stage-cat` theme hooks.** Stage options use the shared `.ui-choice` hooks; a theme that styled `.ui-stage-cat` or the earlier card-shaped `.ui-choice` should restyle `.ui-choice-index`, `.ui-choice-content` and `.ui-choice-eyebrow`.
- **`apiCall` UI action name.** It was never handled by any mount point.
- **Inline locale maps in world and plugin packages**, `PLUGIN.en.md` / `RUNTIME.en.md`, the `locale` field of an action request, the `resolveI18n` option of `world-dimension-get`, and the `memory` content kind of the world generator. See Changed for what replaces each.

### Breaking contracts and upgrade notes

- Plugin prompts: the body of `PLUGIN.md` / `RUNTIME.md` is read as English and `*.zh.md` is the only variant. `PLUGIN.en.md`, `RUNTIME.en.md` and variants for other languages are no longer read. A plugin whose main body is Chinese must move it to `*.zh.md` and supply an English body.
- Plugin packages: `pnpm validate:plugin` rejects a locale map written in a manifest or a UI spec. Write English in the main files and put the translations in `locales/<locale>.yaml`.
- World packages: `pnpm validate:world` reports a locale map written in `world.yaml`, a dimension file or another structured source as `inline-locale-map`. Keep the default language in the main file and move each other language to `<name>.<locale>.<ext>`. `world-dimension-get` no longer takes `resolveI18n`.
- Sessions: the content language is set when the session is created and must be an edition the world has. `POST /api/actions` answers 400 to a request that carries `locale`.
- Sessions created by an earlier version that stored locale maps in their dimension records are rewritten in the session's language the first time they are used. The records as they were stay under `_legacy.dimension-locale-maps`.
- Recreate development sessions of Emberback (its world data source IDs changed) and of Emberback and Lantern Barrow (imported characters are now `npc-<name>`).
- `check.resolved` items no longer carry `roll`, `dc` and `total`; the dice plugin calculates them. A third-party consumer of that event must not read them.
- World generator: `brief.content` no longer accepts `memory`; send `brief.contracts: ["memory.blocks@1"]`. `createWorld` takes `idleTimeoutMs` and `onProgress`, and its `attemptTimeoutMs` option is removed.
- Agent runtimes: `agent.loop.callTimeoutMs` limits only a model call that is not streamed. A streamed call ends after `idleTimeoutMs` of silence (120 seconds by default), and `firstTokenTimeoutMs` is 120 seconds by default, up from 30.
- Themes: the `.ui-stage-cat` hooks are removed; restyle `.ui-choice-index`, `.ui-choice-content` and `.ui-choice-eyebrow`. The `apiCall` UI action name is removed.
- Settings: a `settings.json` (desktop) or `covel:settings` entry (browser) that this version cannot read is moved aside (`settings.v1.bak.json`, `covel:settings.v1.bak`) and the settings start from their defaults. Single values that are no longer accepted go back to their defaults after the file is copied (`settings.conflict.bak.json`). Settings → Data shows the copies. API keys are not affected.
- A browser with no saved appearance opens in the Panel scheme. A saved choice is kept.
- `pnpm create-plugin <name>` scaffolds have no install step and no `pnpm lint`; check them with `pnpm validate:plugin <dir>` and `pnpm test:runtime`. `--with-tools` plugins are named `@covel/plugin-<name>`.
- macOS Apple Silicon and Windows x64 artifacts remain unsigned, and macOS artifacts are not notarized. First launch may show Gatekeeper or SmartScreen warnings.

## [0.0.45] - 2026-10-03

This release adds hidden story content that unfolds during play, lets a world require capabilities and install together with its plugins, merges the bundled plugins while cutting model calls per turn, and makes turn commits and background jobs follow what was actually saved (#101–#108, #110–#115).

### Added

- **Worlds can require a capability instead of naming a plugin.** `pluginPolicy.requires` lists contract IDs such as `action-check@1`. The only installed provider is enabled automatically, and any plugin that provides the contract satisfies it. With no provider, or several and none chosen, the prep screen says why and starting is disabled; a provider the player turns off leaves a notice and the game still starts. Lantern Barrow now requires `action-check@1`. The prep screen labels providers of a required contract "Required by world" and the world's other default plugins "World default".
- **One install for a world and its plugins.** Settings → Install & manage has a single GitHub field. A link lists the plugins and worlds found there together; tick what to install and confirm once. The selection installs as a unit, plugins first, and anything already written is removed if a later package fails. The preview reports up front when a world's required contract has no provider among the installed and selected plugins.
- **Collections.** A `covel-collection.yaml` lists the worlds and plugins an author ships together, including packages pinned by commit in other repositories. `pnpm create-collection` scaffolds one, `pnpm validate:collection` runs the installer's completeness check in CI, and `pnpm pack:collection` builds one ZIP for offline import through the new "Collection package" drop zone. See `docs/v2/ship/collections.md`.
- **Host version ranges.** A plugin's `PLUGIN.md` and a collection manifest accept `covel: ">=0.0.45 <0.1.0"`. The installer refuses a plugin outside its range and states both versions.
- **Hidden world data.** worldData sources accept `visibility: hidden`. Hidden data imports into the receiving plugin's `_hidden.<namespace>` and stays out of prompts, LLM data tools, extension handlers, and every public API until a plugin reveals it (#101).
- **Hidden story events.** The new `story-events` plugin reveals world-authored events when their dimension, world-time, or `revealed` (chained) conditions hold, handing narration a one-turn cue. All bundled worlds ship hidden events (#101, #102).
- **Story planner.** `story-events` can plant hidden follow-up events during play every few turns. Its `plot` runtime runs only when the `planner` setting is on. Plans travel through the `story-event.plan@1` contract; `story-events/intake` validates them and stores accepted events in `_hidden.planned`, where they fire once. The planner sees the world summary, the hero, and this turn's WorldIR when available. Mistport, Emberback, and Lantern Barrow turn it on through `pluginSettings`; Haruka Academy keeps its authored romance routes without it.
- **Concealed runtimes.** `io.concealed: true` strips a runtime's prompts, tool arguments, tool results, and outputs from traces, the live stream, the session view, `/turns`, and manual RPC responses, keeping only names, status, timing, and usage.
- **Bounded agent history.** `agent.history.maxTurns` limits the shared conversation an agent runtime sees; the bundled bookkeeping agents keep two turns.
- **Plugin gateway limits and paged history.** `ctx.gateway.generateText` / `generateObject` accept `maxOutputTokens` and report the slot's token limits; `ctx.readTurnMessages({ after, limit })` pages the turn journal.
- **Retention settings.** `COVEL_AUTO_SNAPSHOT_RETENTION` (default 20) bounds automatic snapshots, and `COVEL_TRACE_RETENTION_DAYS` optionally prunes trace events.
- **`ctx.logicalTurn` for plugins.** Function handlers and plugin tools see the scheduler's turn (committed main-loop player turns plus one), the number `startTurn` and `interval` count in; `turnNumber` stays the raw player-message count.
- **Dropped event payloads are reported.** Only one payload per topic is delivered at each event fan-out depth; a later one now raises an `event-payload-dropped` scheduling hazard that names the topic and the emitting runtime, instead of disappearing silently.
- **`pnpm validate:world <dir>`.** Runs the production world schema and worldData checks that release preflight uses, for world authors and CI.
- **Plugin log level.** `COVEL_PLUGIN_LOG_LEVEL` (`debug` / `info` / `warn` / `error`) sets the lowest level `ctx.logger` stores; it defaults to `info` in production and `debug` elsewhere.

### Changed

- **Unknown models get a 256K context window.** A model with no known limits (not in `llm.toml`, the built-in model data, or the LiteLLM snapshot) now falls back to 262,144 tokens instead of 32,768, for story and plugin calls alike. The small window was a testing aid for compaction; it refused large plugin prompts such as the story planner's (`Context budget exceeded … limit 16384`).
- **Model data refreshed.** The bundled LiteLLM snapshot is pinned to `5dbe4f95` (2026-10-03, 4,459 models), so newer models such as `qwen3.8-flash` resolve their real limits and prices. A weekly `Update model data` workflow now pins the newest LiteLLM revision and opens a pull request with the regenerated snapshot.
- **Story-first chat layout.** Each turn's read-only plugin cards (codex discoveries, achievements, status changes) fold into one "Turn updates · N" line that previews the first titles, so narration keeps most of the screen. Forms, choices, suggestions, images, and warnings stay open. The new General setting "Expand turn updates" restores the open layout.
- **Faster dimension settlement.** The tracker's prompt now carries every rule, schema, and frozen value it needs (within a 24000-character budget), it runs without reasoning, and `update-dimensions` accepts `changes: [{path, value}]` so large dimensions are patched instead of rewritten. In a Mistport session settlement dropped from 20–100 s to about 5 s per turn.
- **Faster character tracking.** The character roster given to `char-creator/character-tracker` now carries each character's current fields (within a 12000-character budget), so most turns settle in one call without a `get-character` read. The tracker runs without reasoning, and a stalled call times out after 30 s and retries once instead of failing the runtime after 60 s.
- A plugin's own code (function runtimes and its local tools) may now write its own `_hidden.*` namespaces. The REST API and the builtin `plugin-data-set` / `plugin-data-set-batch` tools reject every `_` namespace.
- **Background jobs share one durable queue.** plugin-rpc `execution: background` runtimes, prompt builders invoked with `expectsBackgroundFollower`, and background event followers now run as `_runtime_jobs` on the runtime job worker, queued in the commit that produced them, with the same lease, cancel, and retry routes as detached stages. The in-process queue and the legacy `_jobs` rows are removed; panels keep the virtual `_jobs` data source. A runtime that reports its own failure settles its job as failed while its writes still commit.
- **Leaner storage.** Structured runtime outputs no longer enter the conversation timeline; automatic snapshots keep one row per completed player turn and exclude control-plane namespaces; terminal runtime jobs keep the newest 20 per runtime with their status rows; the write-only `runtime_results` table is removed and dropped at boot.
